## How to use this sheet

- Each fact has an ID (for example M12 or R3). PAPER_OUTLINE.md cites these IDs.
- Every number carries its source: a file path, plus a Part number where one exists. "Part N" means the entry in engine/PENDING_PARTS.md.
- **Status key:**
  - **final**: read from a frozen run file, a board file, a signed Part or a ruling.
  - **final, post hoc (Part 622)**: computed after the test labels were opened, from saved predictions and frozen engine files, by two independent scripts that agree (Part 622). Report-only: it never changes a verdict and is never a headline, and the paper labels every such number "post hoc".
  - **final, post hoc (Part 628)**: computed after the test labels were opened, by the post hoc SHAP analysis of Part 628 (rulings AW, AY, AZ1, BA and BB), from its output files and run records. An independent re-computation by two implementations that agree matches, within 1e-9, every number of the comparison's output files that both implementations also hold, and supplies the numbers that the comparison does not write (ruling BB1) and every number of treeroute v1's B1 sensitivity except the B1 runs' background pairs and seconds, which are read from their logs. Report-only: it never changes a verdict and is never a headline, and the paper labels every such number "post hoc".
  - **provisional**: computed by an agent from saved rows and not yet logged as a Part, or a literature figure that still lacks a primary citation.
  - **conflicting**: two sources disagree; see section 12.
  - **pending**: not yet produced.
- **ID collision: C18.** "C18" names two different things. In section 2.3, K24 and the source note of S7.10's Table S7.1 (rule R30) it is the engine constant C18 = 2 months, the longest gap the last-value fill bridges. In section 12 it is conflict C18, the short-history fallback (candidate or scored locations; resolved 2026-10-28, ruling AO3). Notes written from 2026-10-28 on say "conflict C18" for the second; older notes that pair a bare C18 with M29, K18 or the fallback mean the conflict, and "C18 record fix" is the check that settled it. (Noted 2026-10-28, AO fix pass; paper/draft/review_logs/AO_edit_log.md.)
- **Authority rule.** A newer Part or a board/run file outranks notes. A run's derived.json outranks the generic pre-registration draft. Memory notes are background only.
- **Reporting rules the owner set**, which apply to every number here:
  - Report MAE together with anomaly R2 and hit rate against the guess-normal. Never report R2 alone.
  - On zero-heavy targets, put predict-zero beside MAE and corr2, and do not use AUC.
  - Flag TIMID wins.
  - No seed is varied. Uncertainty comes from a jackknife over regions (groups).
  - When a rival's guard failed, write "the published method did not beat the seasonal normal; ours did".
  - Report losses as prominently as wins.

Paths below are relative to the project root (round 5, 2026-11-11: its old path is no longer written, because it held the user name) unless they start with D:/ (since the machine move of 2026-11-08 the project sits in a new folder on the reinstalled machine, and the old path is a directory junction to it, so the paths still resolve). In paths, `<pkg>` is one of pm25, ndvi, fire, defo or coral.

Two independent scripts (A sha256 0cee249c0834dc2b, B 6b781c848bd84391) agree on all 3,917 compared values, and all 82 input files match their recorded hashes. Their status is "final, post hoc (Part 622)", and N6 and N25, which rest on them, are updated.

### 2.2 Plug and predict
- **M4 (final). One generic engine.** The engine has no problem names, presets or per-problem branches.
  - The only hand-set input is a dataset package: data, catalog.yaml (column facts), an optional feature script (which also builds the router context), ban.yaml, split.yaml, and an optional Type-3 plug-in.
  - Every modelling setting is derived by a written rule (R1-R36) and written to derived.json before any compute.
  - Sources: APP_PLAN.md 'In short', 1.1, 1.7, 2; engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md section 2.
- **M5 (final). Enforcement in code.**
  - G-NOKNOB refuses package keys that name a derived setting.
  - The CLI accepts only a package path and --run-root.
  - G-NONAMES scans the engine for dataset names, 4-digit years, drive letters and month arithmetic with 12.
  - The launcher refuses foreign numeric environment variables and pins BLAS/OpenMP/oneDAL to 4 threads.
  - Sources: engine/README.md; APP_PLAN.md 1.7.
- **M6 (final). Four qualifiers on plug-and-predict.**
  1. Router context is dataset-owned feature engineering. PM2.5 and fire use month and lat. NDVI uses 4 harmonics plus a top-5 driver list picked from a bank that had 19 test regions as donors (test-exposed). Defo has no context. Coral uses month, doy sin/cos, |lat| and MMM.
  2. Coral runs through a "table option": the owner vouches for its features and train/test marks (rulings U1, U2).
  3. 46 coral groups are train-only donors (W1).
  4. Coral's 2016-2019 window departs from R3: it was set by leaving out the 2020 survey visits, and signed after the yearly counts were seen (V3; edited 2026-10-27, Figure 3 revision).
  - Sources: derived.json R14 context_columns; OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md U1, U2, V3, W1; APP_PLAN.md 'Honest limit'.

### 2.3 Windows, timing and leads
- **M7 (final). The window rule.** The test window is the last 4 years of coverage, and the inner score window is the 3 years before it. The inner fit runs from Y0 to the year before the inner score window. The donor train end is the year before the test window. Derived windows:

  | Problem | Inner fit | Inner score | Test |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | PM2.5, NDVI | 2003-2016 | 2017-2019 | 2020-2023 |
  | Fire | 2003-2015 | 2016-2018 | 2019-2022 |
  | Defo | 2004-2016 (Y0 2004, ruling N S17 a) | 2017-2019 | 2020-2023 |
  | Coral | 1994-2012 | 2013-2015 | 2016-2019 |

  Sources: derived.json R5-R7 in each run folder; engine_constants.py C1, C2.
- **M8 (final). Feature lags.** Each feature's lag comes from its content time (stamp, window, cadence). It is the smallest whole number of steps that puts the newest data at or before the reference instant.
  - The reference instant is the end of the target's own window at the nowcast, and the end of the issue step at a lead.
  - Co-measured columns get at least 1 step.
  - A gap inside the record takes the same unit's last value from at most 2 months back (C18). The remaining missing values are 0-filled (R30).
  - Sources: APP_PLAN.md R8, R30; OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-05.md J2 d.
- **M9 (final). Leads.**
  - PM2.5, NDVI and fire: nowcast + 6 and 12 months.
  - Defo: nowcast + 1 year (the two leads merge on annual data).
  - Coral: 0, 183 and 365 days.
  - Sources: ruling I1; derived.json R10.
- **M10 (final). What the nowcast sees.** The nowcast is not driver-only. The own-history block ends at t-1, or at t-2 on NDVI and fire (R17 start offset 1). Test-window labels up to the issue step are served by the vault before the lock (D54 a). The older claims "never observes NDVI" (FINDINGS.md section 1) and "no lagged target" (Part 403) are false for v1. Sources: OWNER_RULINGS_2026-09-28.md A (D54 a); derived.json R17.

### 2.4 Specialist tree bank
- **M11 (final). Bank recipe (R19).**
  - Each group trains 100 trees: DecisionTreeRegressor, depth 8, min leaf 5, max_features 0.10 per node, squared error.
  - Each tree fits on a bag of max(30, floor(0.5 x the group's rows)) drawn with replacement from that group's bank-unit rows at or before the cut.
  - Bank sizes: PM2.5 12,700, NDVI 13,300, fire 4,000, defo 4,200, coral 8,300.
  - Sources: engine_constants.py C13; fit/bank.py; derived.json R18/R19; Part 597.
- **M12 (final). Seeds.** No seed is varied. Each tree's seeds come from SHA-256 of '<group>|<tree>|<role>' (D47), and the GLS forest uses random_state 0. Uncertainty comes from a jackknife over groups. Sources: fit/bank.py docstring; engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md section 3.
- **M13 (final). Tree feature counts.**

  | Problem | Tree columns | of which history | Router context columns |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | PM2.5 | 122 | 6 | 2 |
  | NDVI | 498 | 6 | 9 |
  | Fire | 131 | 6 | 2 |
  | Defo | 308 | 5 | 0 |
  | Coral | 63 | none | 5 |

  Source: run.log 'pool:' lines.
- **M14 (final). Backend.** sklearn is patched by sklearnex (oneDAL) with oneDAL at 4 threads in every run. A oneDAL forest is not equal to a stock-sklearn forest at the same seed. Versions (read 2026-10-27 from all five headline manifests, D:/treeroute/runs/<pkg>/headline/<run>/manifest.json 'versions' and 'launch'): scikit-learn 1.6.1, scikit-learn-intelex 2025.10.0, daal (oneDAL) 2025.10.0, numpy 2.0.2, Python 3.9.13; identical in all five. Sources: manifest.json launch.backend and versions; ruling C; memory reference_forest_semantics.md.

- **M15 (final; corrected 2026-10-28). Correctors and placebo.**
  - Each tree gets an out-of-bag corrector: depth 6, min leaf 10, max_features 0.10, fitted on y - tree(X) over its group's out-of-bag rows.
  - The "corrector" column is tree + 1.0 x corrector. A tree with fewer than 50 out-of-bag rows gets no corrector.
  - The placebo is the same fit on the residual rolled by half its length. It is never selectable.
  - On coral, 5,851-6,384 of the 8,300 trees per bank have no corrector, over the run's six banks (fit cut, then guard cut): nowcast 5,851 and 6,193; 183 d 5,915 and 6,345; 365 d 5,932 and 6,384. 56-61 of the 83 groups have no corrector at all. At least 70.5% of each bank's trees (5,851/8,300) have none. Source: D:/treeroute/runs/coral/headline/20261020_f3b8e3da/run.log lines 27, 35, 155, 162, 170, 177 (line 182: the run removed its 6 bank checkpoints). Corrected 2026-10-28: Part 597 printed 5,915-6,384, leaving out the nowcast's fit-cut bank (Part 597 addendum; figsupp critic M1).
  - Sources: fit/correctors.py; ruling E.

### 2.5 The leave-one-block-out tree bank (the project's "LORO") and masking
- **M16a (final). What the project's "leave-one-region-out" (LORO) does and does not mean.** Do not use "leave-one-region-out" in the paper's title or abstract.
  - The TEST is a temporal hold-out: the last 4 years of every region (M7).
  - Each unit's router is fitted on that unit's OWN rows at or before the cut ("fit rows: every scored unit's own rows at or before cut", route/kinds.py docstring; M22, M30).
  - Only the TREE BANK leaves anything out: a unit's router never sees the trees of its own region's overlap block (M16-M18).
  - The common rivals also see the test region's own pre-test rows (RC2). Zero-shot claims on never-seen regions are barred (M57).
  - Paper wording: "a temporal hold-out of the last four years in every region; each region's router weights only other regions' specialist trees, with its own region and overlapping regions masked (a leave-one-block-out tree bank)". The jackknife over regions (P16) is a separate thing: the uncertainty method.
  - Sources: route/kinds.py; APP_PLAN.md R14; engine/SIGNATURES_LORO_ADDENDUM.md (the project's term).
- **M16 (final). No hand-made split.** Every non-excluded group trains its trees and is scored; coral's donor-only groups are the exception (W1). A unit's router never sees the trees of its own overlap block (G-DISJOINT is asserted at every router fit). Sources: rulings D, E (L2); APP_PLAN.md R14; route/kinds.py.
- **M17 (final). Overlap blocks.** Groups are linked when a unit of one touches the 8-neighbourhood of a unit of the other; tables use a spacing rule. Blocks are the connected components:

  | Problem | Groups | Blocks | Two-group blocks |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | PM2.5 | 127 | 119 | 8 |
  | NDVI | 133 | 125 | 8 |
  | Fire | 40 | 39 | 1 |
  | Defo | 42 | 40 | 2 |
  | Coral | 83 | 78 | 5 |

  Source: derived.json R14:overlap_blocks.
- **M18 (final). Why the whole block is masked.** The scored units are a subset of the bank units, so the group's own trees would answer that unit's fit rows in-bag. Masking the block makes every tree answer an out-of-group prediction. No out-of-fold refits are needed, unlike classical stacking. Sources: APP_PLAN.md R14 'The reason'; memory reference_three_structural_laws.md LAW 2 (Part 284).
- **M19 (final). Inner-cut bank.** The bank and correctors are refit at the inner cut for the nowcast decision (L6 b; A7.inner_cut_bank_refit = True, test-exposed). Each lead also refits a guard bank at its guard cut (H2). The logs show 0 guard rows inside the bank's label window. Sources: rulings L6, H2; run.log ladder guard lines.
- **M20 (final). A7 residual exposure.** At lead 0, the GLS error target is built from the FIT-cut bank, whose trees saw the inner-score years. The code flags this for the owner. Source: fit/weights.py docstring.

### 2.6 Routers
- **M21 (final). Router kind (R12).** The kind is set by h, the median number of valid fit rows per candidate unit: h >= 24 gives per-unit, 2 <= h < 24 gives region, and h < 2 gives neighbour.
  - Measured h: PM2.5 168, NDVI 168, fire 156 (all per-unit); defo 13 (region); coral 1 (neighbour).
  - Sources: APP_PLAN.md R12; derived.json R12.
- **M22 (final). Per-unit router.**
  - Design: [intercept | the unit's link-matched normal | standardised context | tree answers], fitted on the unit's own rows at or before the cut, in link space.
  - The intercept and normal are unpenalised. The normal's coefficient is fitted, not fixed at 1, so the paper calls the normal an "unpenalised anchor (its weight fitted)", never an "offset" (owner ruling AN1, 2026-10-28). The trees-off design is the intercept and normal alone, fitted unweighted. Sources: route/kinds.py per_unit_router_design (unpenalised=(INTERCEPT, NORMAL)), trees_off_router_design and _trees_off ("Intercept + normal, unweighted").
  - The ridge penalties a_ctx and a_tree are each scaled by the mean diagonal of their own block's Gram (trace-normalised).
  - Sources: route/kinds.py; route/solve.py.
- **M23 (final). The shrinkage target is the unit's own climatology.** The penalty pulls the prediction toward intercept + normal, not toward a uniform forest average. The tree weights are signed ridge coefficients, not convex mixture weights. ARCHITECTURE_EXPLAINED.md section 4 ("pulls w(u) back toward the average") describes the predecessor. Sources: route/solve.py (UNPENALISED_COLUMNS = 2); route/kinds.py R16.
- **M24 (final). Penalty grid (R14, R26).**
  - 7 levels (0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 10, 30, 100) x 5 ratios (0.0625 ... 1.0) = 35 points, with a_tree = 0.5 x level and a_ctx = a_tree x ratio.
  - The region grid uses lambda = 100 x level over the same 7 levels.
  - The centres (0.5 / 0.25 from fire_architecture.py, and region lambda 100) are test-exposed. Lambda 100 coincides with defo's pooled test-window argmax.
  - Sources: engine_constants.py C12; engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md Appendix B.
- **M25 (final). Solver.**
  - Because rows < columns, the solver uses the exact dual: QR projection of the unpenalised columns, then a numpy Cholesky.
  - The first 3 units of each sweep are re-checked against the direct solve within 1e-8, or the run fails.
  - In the predecessor, the dual was 463x faster than the primal at 10,000 trees, and they agreed to 9.1e-10.
  - Sources: route/solve.py; MASTER_RESEARCH_RECORD.md 2.2.2.
- **M26 (final). The GLS-weighted solve is new code.** It is checked against brute-force weighted least squares within 1e-10. It was accepted as a build departure ("unsure: weighted GLS solve", S21). Sources: route/solve.py lines 209-320; engine/FREEZE_SIGNOFF_SHEET.md S21.
- **M27 (final). Region router (defo).** One pooled ridge per group, with intercept and normal unpenalised and no context. The fit keeps the largest evenly spaced set of whole units within 60,000 rows: 4,615 units x 13 = 59,995 rows at the inner cut, and 3,750 units at the test pass. There is no GLS solve, so defo had no GLS arms. Sources: route/region.py; derived.json R14:region_fit; defo adoption.json.
- **M28 (final). Neighbour router (coral).**
  - For each scored row it builds a strict pool of rows dated strictly before the issue day.
  - It takes the 150 nearest by haversine distance, then the 50 most similar in standardised tree-feature space.
  - With fewer than 12 eligible rows it falls back to the donor median.
  - It drops the blocks of the row's own group and of every group in its pool, and it has no normal column.
  - Sources: route/neighbour.py; rulings U3, U4, V2.
- **M29 (final; the scored-row reading confirmed by the owner 2026-10-28, ruling AO3, so conflict C18 (section 12) is resolved; corrected 2026-10-28, C18 record fix; status label aligned in the round-2 fix pass). Short-history fallback.** By rule (K18), scored units with fewer than 24 valid fit rows are routed by their group's region router (at any router cut: every lead's fit and guard cut). In the runs the fallback routed no scored row. The counts are candidate units, not scored ones: R12 counts, over every candidate unit of every group, the units with fewer than 24 valid labels from Y0 to the inner-fit end: NDVI 75 of 2,289,116, fire 317 of 925,710, PM2.5 0 of 2,028,667; none of them is scored. R20:fallback_rows (scored units only, at every router cut) gives groups_with_fallback 0, short_at_cuts {} and extra_rows 0 in all three runs; R12's window ends at the lead-0 guard cut (NDVI step 24204, fire 24192), after the earliest router cut R20 checks (24193, 24181), and a count at an earlier cut is never larger, so a scored unit among the 75 or 317 would be listed there. At run time decision_checks.json has fallback null; every fallback_levels entry (decision surface; each lead's guard surface) has fallback_units_scored 0, used_centre true and surface_mae_by_level null; no test_pass_router.json, ladder/contract_lead_0.json or ladder/lead_*/lead.json holds a fallback or all_short record. Quote 75 and 317 only as candidate units. Sources: derived.json R12 inputs and R20:fallback_rows; decision_checks.json; test_pass_router.json; ladder/*.json (read-only, 2026-10-28, C18). Confirmed by the owner 2026-10-28 (ruling AO3: "the short-history fallback is in the design but routed no scored row in any run"); conflict C18 resolved. The check script and its output are kept in D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/c18/: c18_check.py (sha256 18bdaffe) and c18_check_output.txt (sha256 44f6886d).
- **M30 (final). Routers are fitted once per pass and never refitted inside the test window.** Only the history features update. Example: PM2.5 had 319,980 fit rows on the decision surface and 388,539 on the test pass, for 1,905 units. Source: PM2.5 run.log ~1325, ~1342.
- **M31 (final). The router does not see the unit's own recent level.** It reaches the prediction only through the trees, via the history block. Source: engine/PAPER_NOTES_RIVALS.md, vetted PM2.5 paragraph (Part 611).

### 2.7 Normal, climatology identity, history block
- **M32 (final). The normal (R15).** It is the mean of link(y) over the unit's own fit rows, per season key (the month, or one class for annual data), with fallbacks to the all-season mean and the donor mean. The neighbour kind has no normal. Sources: APP_PLAN.md R15; route/kinds.py season_normal.
- **M33 (final). Climatology identity (R16).** Trees-off = intercept + normal only. OLS returns coefficient 1, so trees-off equals the normal exactly.
  - This is asserted on every fitted unit within max(k x s, 9e-7), with k = 5.230771281733543e-08.
  - Sources: APP_PLAN.md R16, R36; engine_constants.py C9; rulings N (S12 a), O4.
- **M34 (final, post hoc (Part 622)). The climatology identity in the saved test rows.** At the nowcast, the identity-link trees-off MAE equals the guess-normal MAE to 6 significant digits in all four runs with an identity link: PM2.5 4.03161, NDVI 0.0394141, fire 0.00625876 (added by Part 622), defo 0.00840486.
  - Relative differences: PM2.5 -5.4e-11, NDVI -6.2e-09, fire +2.1e-08, defo +4e-11. Largest per-row difference: 4.1e-09, 1.76e-07, 8.09e-07 and 4.76e-09, each inside the run's identity tolerance (1e-06 on PM2.5, 9e-07 on the other three; K60). The guess-normal equals the clipped normal exactly. Trees-off anomaly R2: 5.2e-11, 1.45e-09, 5.5e-09 and 2.0e-10.
  - **Wording:** write "equal to 6 significant digits" or "within 2.1e-8 relative". "Within 1e-8 relative" is false for fire.
  - Coral is excluded: the neighbour router has no normal (M28).
  - Sources: Part 622; D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/POSTHOC_REPORT.md (M34); D:/treeroute/runs/{pm25,ndvi,fire,defo}/headline/*/test_preds.parquet and score_pooled.csv. The headline tables agree: NDVI trees-off 0.03941 = normal 0.03941 (ndvi/headline_9e5590eab94b.csv, final).
- **M35 (final). Own-history block (R17).** The engine builds the block, and a dataset's own-lag columns are refused (G-HIST).
  - Monthly packages: hist_last_1-3, hist_mean_12, hist_mean_60 and hist_mean_all, ending at the issue step.
  - Defo: hist_last_1-3, hist_mean_5 and hist_mean_all.
  - Coral: none.
  - Start offset: 1 on NDVI and fire, 0 on PM2.5 and defo.
  - Sources: APP_PLAN.md R17; derived.json R17; io/apply.py.
- **M36 (final). History fallback (F1).** The fill order is: the row's own trailing means, then the as-of season mean, then the as-of all-season mean, then the donor mean. It never uses 0 and never a label at or after the row. Two readings remain marked "owner confirmation asked" in the frozen code (io/apply.py ~536-540). Source: ruling F1.

### 2.8 Label vault and locks
- **M37 (final). Vault reads.** LabelView is the only holder of labels, and every read is logged. A label counts at a cut only if it is dated at or before the cut AND its window ended by then (R11, D26). Source: labels/vault.py.
- **M38 (final). Locks.** decide() accepts only a vault-issued, content-hashed DecisionSurface with no test-window row. Adoption files are written once, with a SHA-256 lock. Test labels open only if the hash matches, and a write-once marker is then written (G-LOCK). Sources: labels/vault.py; layer/decide.py.

### 2.9 Adaptation layer (Type 2)
- **M39 (final). Links (R22, R23).**
  - Identity always.
  - log(y + c) when the legal minimum is >= 0 and donor y has more than 2 distinct positive values. c is the 1st percentile of positive donor y: 1.5 on PM2.5, 0.012987 on fire and 4.956e-05 on defo.
  - arcsine(sqrt(y/UB)) on [0, UB].
  - Link sets: PM2.5 identity + log. NDVI identity only. Fire, defo and coral identity + log + arcsine.
  - Sources: APP_PLAN.md R22, R23; layer/links.py; derived.json.
- **M40 (final). Arms (R24).** Per link, the arms are plain and corrector, each unweighted and GLS-weighted. Trees-off (the guard reference) and the placebo are never selectable. The candidate order is fixed and breaks ties. PM2.5 had 282 candidates on its surface. Sources: layer/arms.py; PM2.5 run.log.
- **M41 (final). GLS weights (R25).**
  - A random forest of 120 trees (depth 8, min leaf 20, random_state 0) predicts the cross-region error |y - mean of the out-of-block plain answers|. On rows it was fitted on, the out-of-bag prediction is used (L5 c).
  - Sigma is floored and clipped to the 5th-95th percentiles. The weight is 1/sigma^2, with max/min capped at 100.
  - Sources: fit/weights.py; ruling L5.
- **M42 (final). Penalty search (R26).** Each arm's grid point is chosen by the lowest decision-surface MAE in y units on clipped predictions (R28). Edge picks are flagged "EDGE PICK" and never extended (Q13 a). Sources: layer/decide.py; APP_PLAN.md R26, R28.
- **M43 (final). Selection guard ("N2" in the code; D64).** An arm is eligible only if its surface MAE is strictly below its own link's trees-off MAE (the donor median for the neighbour kind). There is no margin. C9's "guard tolerance" is the tolerance of the identity check, not a margin. Sources: layer/decide.py; ruling V1.
- **M44 (final). Adoption and decline (R27).**
  - The eligible arm with the lowest surface MAE is adopted. If none is eligible, the layer declines to the decline target: the identity (arithmetic) normal for grid kinds, the donor median for neighbour.
  - The best tree arm is recorded either way.
  - MAE is the only selection score (Q37). Predict-zero and corr2 are printed and never used.
  - Sources: layer/decide.py; APP_PLAN.md R27.
- **M45 (final). Guard reference vs decline target.** For log arms the guard reference is the geometric normal (log trees-off), but a decline falls back to the arithmetic normal. The fix exists only for the neighbour kind (V1); for grids it "waits for the next engine version". Sources: APP_PLAN.md R16, R27; engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT_coral.md line 268.
- **M46 (final). Decision surface.** For grid kinds it is the scored units' inner-score rows (3 years); PM2.5 had 68,559 valid rows. Coral uses leave-one-group-out folds (2,046 rows in 33 groups). The nowcast guard is evaluated on the same surface as the penalty search; there is no separate holdout. Sources: APP_PLAN.md R13; Part 595; layer/decide.py.

### 2.10 Forecast ladder
- **M47 (final). Direct forecasting, one model per lead.**
  - At lead L the feature row moves to the issue step, and the history block is rebuilt.
  - The bank, correctors and c are refit at the lead's fit cut.
  - The arm and penalty are PINNED to the nowcast's adoption.
  - A guard bank at the lead's guard cut serves the guard. If it fails, the lead declines (D86).
  - Sources: pipeline/ladder.py; APP_PLAN.md R29; rulings H2-H5.
- **M48 (final). The lead-0 contract.** The ladder code at L = 0 must reproduce the nowcast byte for byte, and it held for defo (Part 592). Source: pipeline/ladder.py.

### 2.11 Units, rows left out, costs
- **M49 (final). Scored units (R18).** Per group: max(10, floor(K x n / G)) with K = 20, capped at the bank units.

  | Problem | Scored units |
  |---|---|
  | PM2.5 | 1,905 (15 per group) |
  | NDVI | 1,995 planned, 1,983 scored |
  | Fire | 2,000 (50 per group) |
  | Defo | 92,274 (2,197 per group; bank 4,615) |
  | Coral | 2,312 test visits in 37 ecoregions |

  K = 20 comes from the size of the old hand splits. Sources: derived.json R18; ruling E L1; engine/HEADLINE_MODEL_TABLE.md.
- **M50 (final). Rows left out (H4, S1, G1).** Rows with missing issue-step features or context are left out, flagged and counted, never filled.
  - NDVI: 12 coastal cells (365 decision rows and 576 test rows per pass), plus 13,481 router fit rows at lead 12.
  - Defo lead 1: 103,823 test rows with a 0-filled issue-row tree feature (R30), counted and not left out.
  - Sources: Parts 589, 592.
- **M51 (final). Run cost.** PM2.5 about 15.5 h; NDVI 19.3 h (from a checkpoint); fire 4.3 h; defo about 47 h; coral about 35 min. Sources: Parts 584, 589, 590, 592, 597.
- **M52 (final). Engineering changes were number-neutral.** Checkpoints (P), RAM waiting (Q/R) and per-group tree checkpoints (S3) were each gated by byte-identical smoke outputs. Sources: rulings P, Q, R, S3; Parts 586-588.
- **M53 (final). Type 3 was not built or run in v1.** Run records list "Type 3 (plan 4.1 step 10) is not built". Source: D:/treeroute/runs/pm25/headline/20261007_74e67609/run_record.json 'limits'.

### 2.12 Novelty and related work
- **M54 (final). Novelty is a composition of known parts.**
  - The prior-art verdict is "novel_combination_of_known_parts".
  - Part 403's external rating: novelty 5/10, impact 6/10, with tree routing "INCREMENTAL, not novel".
  - Approved wording (CLAIMS_LEDGER X14): "a novel composition of known parts". Its tail "plus a cross-entity router" refers to the predecessor's design (see M58).
  - Sources: TREE_ROUTE_ALGORITHM_AND_PRIOR_ART.md Parts 2-4; CLAIMS_LEDGER.md X14 (line 768).
- **M55 (final). Prior art to cite up front.**
  - Forest Garrote (Meinshausen 2009); ISLE/RuleFit (Friedman & Popescu); Hedged RF (Beck et al. 2023); Tree-Weighting (Ramchandran et al. 2020); cross-study learners (Patil & Parmigiani 2018; added 2026-10-28, Crossref-verified, see M55b).
  - Stacked regression (Breiman 1996; Wolpert 1992; Super Learner).
  - Meta-GLAR (Grazzi et al. 2021); R2D2/MetaOptNet.
  - Ridge as BLUP (Robinson 1991).
  - FWLS, Covariate-dependent Stacking, Bayesian Hierarchical Stacking (Yao et al. 2022; Bayesian Analysis 17(4); REFERENCES.md: do not cite it as 2021).
  - Multi-source adaptation (Crammer, Kearns & Wortman 2008; Mansour et al. 2009). **Source correction:** these two are NOT in TREE_ROUTE_ALGORITHM_AND_PRIOR_ART.md (a search finds 0 hits). They come from CLAIMS_LEDGER.md line 771 (the X14 skeptic note, citing Part 403). Verify both against the primary literature (O22).
  - Forecast combination and global-vs-local forecasting, listed in the prior-art file but missing from the list above: Bates & Granger (1969) and the Granger-Ramanathan lineage; the forecast-combination puzzle (Claeskens et al. 2016); FFORMA (Montero-Manso et al. 2020); Montero-Manso & Hyndman (2021) on locality and globality (TREE_ROUTE_ALGORITHM_AND_PRIOR_ART.md lines 69, 73, 164-166).
  - Other sources: TREE_ROUTE_ALGORITHM_AND_PRIOR_ART.md Part 2, 'Citations to have on hand' (line 159 onward).
- **M55a (final, 2026-10-28: checked against the primary texts; first written 2026-10-26 from the reviewing agent's knowledge, when a search for krishnamurti/superensemble in the root and engine .md files returned nothing).** The closest geoscience precedent is the multimodel superensemble (Krishnamurti et al. 1999, Science; 2000, J. Climate, full text not reached; its form is confirmed in Krishnamurti et al. 2003, ECMWF seminar, read in full): least-squares weights over member-model forecast anomalies, fitted at every grid point and for every variable on a training period, and added to the observed mean over the training period (the observed climatology; eq. 2 of the 2003 paper); the weights may be negative. That is close to "a per-unit ridge over a bank anchored to the climatology", with one difference: the superensemble adds the climatology as it is, while v1 fits the normal's weight without a penalty (M22; wording "anchor", not "offset": owner ruling AN1, 2026-10-28). Operational seasonal outlooks put climatology, or nothing, in place of a forecast, but only CPC ties this to skill: CPC's "equal chances" areas are where its tools show no skill in preferring a category (NWS et al. 2011 brochure); IRI's 1997-2000 net assessments often issued the climatological probabilities (Wilks and Godfrey 2002; a practice, not a stated rule); ECMWF blanks points whose forecast distribution is not significantly different from the model climate, which its guide says is not a skill measure (ECMWF 2021 SEAS5 guide). The earlier wording "shows climatology where skill is low" was too broad and is replaced by the above. Both must be cited and contrasted. Sources: REFERENCES.md Section A keys Krishnamurti1999_superensemble, Krishnamurti2000_superensemble, Krishnamurti2003_ECMWFseminar, NWS_CPC2011_EC, WilksGodfrey2002_IRI, ECMWF2021_SEAS5guide.
- **M55b (final, 2026-10-28; primary texts read for Table 2, completeness critic M4). What the closest prior art shows, by Table 2 column.** Reading notes: draft/tables/table_0.md, end comment; keys in REFERENCES.md Section A.
  - Cross-study learners (Patil & Parmigiani 2018, PNAS; first read through the PMC page with a summarising fetch tool, then re-read 2026-10-28 from a full copy of that page, PMC5856504, saved locally, sha256 f68945c6): learners of one or more types, each trained on one study, and one global weight set (eq. 1, a weighted sum; the Results' regression weights add an intercept); replicability weights use only each learner's errors on the other training studies (its own study excluded), with no refits; stacked-regression weights also use each learner's own study; a learner trained on the merged studies is a benchmark.
  - Tree-weighting (Ramchandran et al. 2020; PSB open-access PDF read): one stacking vector over all trees, ridge-penalised and non-negative, with an intercept; the stacking rows hold every tree's predictions on every training study, its own included; no anchor and no decline rule.
  - Meta-GLAR (Grazzi et al. 2021; arXiv v2 read): closed-form ridge head per series; in its transfer setting the representation is trained on a source dataset and each target series' head is fitted on its own context window without retraining. So "a per-series ridge over a basis trained elsewhere, with no refits" is NOT new; v1 differs in its basis (other regions' specialist trees, masked by overlap block) and its anchor.
  - FFORMA (2019 working-paper version read): softmax weights from series features; every pool method is fitted to each series; no anchor and no decline rule.
  - Bayesian hierarchical stacking (arXiv v2 read): simplex weights; leave-one-out densities by importance sampling, so no refits; the held-out unit is one row.
  - Stacked regressions (Breiman 1996b; read): a weighted sum of predictors with non-negative weights and no added term.
  - Superensemble (Krishnamurti et al. 2003; read): no rule replaces the forecast by climatology; every member needs past forecasts for the training phase.
  - Not reached: Super Learner full text (bepress 403, publisher 405), Krishnamurti et al. 2000 (AMS 403); Wolpert 1992 not read. Their Table 2 cells stay "—".
  - Final edits (2026-11-23), dated note: the check of five cited works of 2026-11-18 read the abstracts of the Super Learner, Krishnamurti et al. 2000 and Wolpert 1992 (record: paper/supp_build/citations_check_2026-11-18.md; verdicts in the dated note under Q12). The Super Learner's publisher page now loads, with the full text behind a login, so the 405 above is out of date; the AMS page of Krishnamurti et al. 2000 was blocked again (403); no reading of the full text of Wolpert 1992 is recorded. In Table 2's stacking row only the Super Learner's anchor and decline cells print a dash, and no cell does for Wolpert 1992. No cell of Table 2 changed (copy of this sheet before this note: paper/draft/_snapshots/20261123T0014Z_final_edits/).
  - Consequence: Section 1.2 and Table 2 delta 1 name Patil & Parmigiani 2018 and Meta-GLAR's transfer setting as precedents for leaving a member's own source out of the weight fit and for per-series heads without refits. (Tier 1 length plan (2026-11-12, ruling AU7), dated note: Table 2's deltas list, delta 1 included, moved verbatim to Supplement S7.1 (A1-12); Section 1.2 still names both precedents (A1-10, A1-11).)
- **M56 (final). The verified distinguishing axis.** Published tree-reweighting uses ONE global weight vector. The defensible claim is "unlike the (global) tree-reweighting literature, we compute the tree weights per unit from each unit's own history". Source: TREE_ROUTE_ALGORITHM_AND_PRIOR_ART.md Part 2, 'Global vs per-entity'.
  - **Caveats.** (1) The per-unit pattern itself is Meta-GLAR's ("THE dangerous collision - identical skeleton"; prior-art file line 60). (2) The per-unit axis has never been isolated by an ablation in v1: there is no comparison with a uniform average or one global ridge over the same masked tree bank. The prior-art file says such ablations must carry the claim (lines 144-152: "Let ablations carry the composition claim"; "show the lift survives a genuine uniform-average baseline"). engine/PENDING_PARTS.md has no v1 router ablation; the only ablation is the Ridge refit (Part 611). (3) The per-unit router runs on 3 of the 5 problems only (M59).
- **M59 (final). Which router each problem actually used.** Per-unit (with the exact trees-off = normal identity): PM2.5, NDVI and fire. Region (one pooled ridge per group, no context, no GLS): defo. Neighbour (no normal column, no climatology identity, constant donor-median reference): coral. So the abstract's per-unit description fits 3 problems. Of those, fire's gain depends on the link (R12, R3b) and NDVI's design was test-exposed (P23), which leaves PM2.5 as the cleanest per-unit test; there Ridge beats us at 6 and 12 months (N1). Sources: M21, M27, M28; P13.
- **M57 (final). Relation to MoE, MOB and transfer learning.**
  - **MoE:** there is no learned gate and no softmax. The experts are frozen, and the weights are signed ridge coefficients (ARCHITECTURE_EXPLAINED.md section 4; route/solve.py).
  - **MOB (Zeileis et al.):** no project document contrasts v1 with it. In v1 the partition is given, not learned.
  - **Transfer:** "transductive multi-source domain adaptation with target-side calibration" (CLAIMS_LEDGER X15, Part 403).
  - Do not claim "zero-shot on never-seen regions" or "memory-free".
- **M58 (final). There is no recipe layer in v1.** v1 routes over individual tree answers directly. The recipe dictionary and mother router of ARCHITECTURE_EXPLAINED.md sections 5-7 belong to the predecessor. Source: route/kinds.py, route/solve.py.

## 3. Evaluation protocol and governance

- **P1 (final). Pre-registration rule.** A pre-registration Part is written before any number, with a hash stamp (Q24 a). The owner's O3 signature stands for each package's freeze taken before its run. Sources: HEADLINE_QUESTIONS_AND_FIXES.md Q24; OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md O3.
- **P2 (final). Freeze tool.** It writes a lock plus a filled pre-registration, and refuses while any OPEN marker remains. `freeze verify` re-hashes the engine, constants, package, input stores, library versions and launch pins. Source: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md sections 1, 12; Part 582.
- **P3 (final). Freeze locks** are listed in M1. Lock SHA-256 prefixes: PM2.5 cb41d40c, NDVI 960bc7d5, fire f8c297e5, defo a59408bb, coral 70b163cd. Sources: Parts 583, 588, 591, 596; D:/treeroute/registry/freeze/.
- **P4 (final; corrected 2026-10-26, consolidation pass). Re-freezes.** Fire and NDVI were frozen 4 times (Parts 585-588: one freeze and three re-freezes). A full-size check found NDVI's derived_sha256 e2836c5e... unchanged. Sources: Parts 585-588.
  - **Correction:** not every re-freeze was an engineering-only change. Parts 586 (crash-safe saving) and 587 (RAM handling) were number-neutral engine changes, proven byte-identical on smoke runs. The third re-freeze (Part 588) also carried ruling S1, a rule change: a scored row whose router context is missing is now left out of scoring and counted instead of refused. It was byte-identical on the pm25 smoke (which has no such rows); on NDVI it let the run proceed, leaving out 365 decision-surface rows and 576 test rows of 12 coastal cells per pass (M50). (draft/review_logs/protocol_review_log.md item 22.)
- **P5 (final). Derived settings not filled in the grid pre-registrations.** No plan existed at freeze time, so DERIVED_SHA256 / PLAN_SHA256 / DERIVED_SETTINGS read NOT FILLED (12 markers per grid copy). Each run derived its settings under the frozen rules. Coral's fields are filled. Sources: Parts 583, 585, 591.
- **P6 (final; corrected 2026-10-27). Grid signature lines are blank.**
  - All four gridded filled copies carry an approval line that the agents wrote under ruling O3 (Parts 583, 585, 591): PM2.5, fire and NDVI "Signed by the owner (ruling O3, 2026-10-06)"; defo "Signed by the owner (ruling O3, 2026-10-06; disclosures ruling T, 2026-10-17)", as the last line of its section 13 (line 377 of PREREGISTRATION_20261017T105545Z.md), after the blank lines. In all four the "Signed by" and "Date" lines are blank, and no separate signature act is recorded. (Corrected 2026-10-27: the earlier text said defo's copy had no O3 line.)
  - Only coral, the common rivals and human Part A have separate .SIGNED records.
  - Source: D:/treeroute/registry/freeze/PREREGISTRATION_*.md.
- **P7 (final). Coral was signed after its run.** The run (13:15-13:52 local) started before the owner signed in chat. The Part was hashed at 10:09Z, before the run, and is unchanged (sha 2b644584...). Source: Part 597; .SIGNED.md.
- **P8 (final). Bug-fix rule after the freeze.** Only bug fixes are allowed. Any changed constant, rule, link, arm, lead, metric or verdict needs a new Part first. Source: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md section 11.
- **P9 (final). Signed decisions.** Ruling A accepted D1-D101. Ruling E settled the LORO addendum L1-L7. Ruling N S20 b confirmed 17 extra constants. Sources: OWNER_RULINGS_2026-09-28.md A, E; OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md N.
- **P10 (final). Lean pre-headline testing.** Deep checks ran on the 4 smoke groups per package only, with no full-size rehearsal (ruling M1-M3).
- **P11 (final). Groups.** PM2.5 127, NDVI 133, fire 40 and defo 42, with no exclusions. Coral has 37 scored + 46 donor-only groups, with 11 excluded (V5) and 11,338 train / 2,312 test visits. Sources: datasets/*/split.yaml; verdict.json; rulings V5, W1.
- **P12 (final). Headline metrics.**
  - MAE is the headline and the selection score (Q37 a, which reversed C5).
  - It is printed with anomaly R2 against the per-unit normal, corr2 and hit rate, beside the guess-normal and the last-5-years normal, plus predict-zero and, at the leads, persistence and damped persistence.
  - Hit rate is the share of rows within half the donor rows' mean absolute deviation from their normal (R32). Declared floors: PM2.5 2 ug/m3, NDVI 0.02.
  - Source: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md section 6.
- **P13 (final). Coral metrics.** The normal is the donor median (one constant), and anomaly R2 is undefined. Results are reported per programme first; the pooled number is labelled "mixes programmes". Source: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT_coral.md 6.1-6.2.
- **P14 (final). Verdict rule (D74).** Tags are tested in order:
  1. declined;
  2. IDENTICAL;
  3. CANNOT TELL (fewer than 3 groups);
  4. EQUIVALENT (the whole CI inside +-band);
  5. HELPS (CI low > 0 AND point >= band);
  6. HURTS (mirror of HELPS);
  7. otherwise CANNOT TELL.

  On the rival boards HELPS reads as BEATS and HURTS as LOSES. Sources: ruling A (D74); engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md section 7.
- **P15 (final). Deciding contrast and band.** Per group: MAE(the adopted arm's own-link trees-off) - MAE(adopted). For coral the reference is the donor median.
  - The band is the median jackknife SE of the run's own arm contrasts: PM2.5 0.0462, NDVI 0.000597, fire 1.36e-5, defo 1.10e-4, coral 0.918.
  - The nowcast band decides at every lead.
  - Sources: verdict.json; D:/treeroute/runs/_headline_tables/*/verdicts_*.csv.
- **P16 (final). Uncertainty.** A leave-one-group-out jackknife at z = 1.96 over all scored groups, with no multiplicity correction. The blocked jackknife is report-only. Source: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md sections 7-8.
  - **The estimand** is the equal-weight mean over groups of the per-group MAE contrast. It is not the pooled-MAE difference that the headline tables print, and the two can differ a lot (coral: group mean 5.21 vs pooled 1.597; R5).
- **P17 (final). Lock before test labels.** At every lead of every run, adoption.json was locked at the same recorded second as the test labels were opened, with the lock first under G-LOCK. Source: adoption*.json.lock.json and test_labels_opened_lead_*.json in each run folder.
- **P18 (final). Ban lists are per problem.**

  | Problem | Verdicts | Keep | Watch | Conditional | Ban |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | PM2.5 | 190 | 101 | 17 | 24 | 48 |
  | Fire | 204 | 113 | 14 | 7 | 70 |
  | Defo | 404 | 285 | 18 | 1 | 100 |
  | NDVI | 562 | 464 | 37 | 0 | 61 |
  | Coral | 76 | 65 | 3 | 3 | 5 |

  Watch columns enter the trees and are named in every report; conditional columns are out. Counts in BAN_INSTALL_REPORT.md and Part 580 are superseded. Source: engine/datasets/*/ban.yaml, counted 2026-10-26.
- **P18b (final; S2 recount, added 2026-10-27). Ban reasons per problem (Table S2.2).** Reason words of the banned columns: PM2.5 label 1, post_window_snapshot 9, trend_proxy 10, incomplete 19, structural 9 (48); NDVI label 1, coproduct 13, target_derived 2, post_window_snapshot 9, trend_proxy 11, incomplete 16, structural 9 (61); fire label 4, target_derived 5, post_event_state 9, post_window_snapshot 10, trend_proxy 13, incomplete 24, structural 5 (70); defo label 1, coproduct 10, lookahead_unfixable 10, post_window_snapshot 9, trend_proxy 24, incomplete 40, structural 6 (100); coral label 1, trend_proxy 3, structural 1 (5). Source: engine/datasets/*/ban.yaml (verdicts: verdict, reason), recounted 2026-10-27.

Sources: ban.yaml; catalog.yaml role router_context; K12.

Of the 90 lags of the 18 dropped bases, 84 were never built; 6 lag-12 copies (five SPEI scales and AMO) were built under J2 (a). PM2.5's 24 conditional columns are 15 satellite columns, 2 surface-radiation columns, phenology, burn_lag1 and 5 EDGAR columns. Sources: defo/ban.yaml; pm25/ban.yaml.

Feature-lineage lines in the run logs.** PM2.5, NDVI and fire list 12 derived columns each (PM2.5 and fire also list ctx:lat); defo lists 25; coral logs only "vouched by the package owner (U2)". Defo's store copies (MOD44B lagged 2 years, DeepRec k3) are not listed. Source: the five headline run.log files ('feature lineage' lines).

Ban lists match the locks; DeepRec lag.** Each package's ban.yaml SHA-256 (PM2.5 b30b2af5, NDVI 7ec17f23, fire a7ab7864, defo 6386e7cb, coral 7a86de1c) is the hash its run lock records (M1).   Sources: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md Appendix B; PAPER_REFERENCE.md 8.12; engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT_coral.md sections 4.4, 10.
- **P24 (final). Rival protocol timing.** Read from the dates.
  - The common-rival rulings Y-AD (2026-10-20/21) came after all five of our headline scores, but before any rival test score.
  - The human-rival rulings AG (2026-10-22) and AH (2026-10-23) were made while common boards were still pending: AG predates the defo board (Part 614, 2026-10-23 09:12) and the NDVI board (Part 616, 2026-10-25 09:39), and AH predates the NDVI board. AG4 itself schedules human steps "after the defo common board, before NDVI". AI is dated 2026-10-25, the same day as the NDVI board.

- **P25 (final). Continuity rows.** The old hard-split test groups are scored alone, labelled "continuity, not a replication". Every verdict (contrast = reference - model, group-jackknife 95% CI):

  | Problem | Groups | Nowcast | Lead 6 / 1 yr | Lead 12 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | PM2.5 | 20 | HELPS 0.319 [0.174, 0.465] (4.088 vs 4.407) | CANNOT TELL 0.0028 [-0.160, 0.166] (4.444 vs 4.447) | declined (4.641; damped persistence 4.415) |
  | NDVI | 44 | HELPS 0.00847 [0.00620, 0.01074] (0.03241 vs 0.04093) | declined | declined |
  | Fire | 10 | **CANNOT TELL** 0.000114 [-0.00004, 0.000269] (0.003663 vs 0.003778; **predict-zero 0.003111 beats both**) | declined | declined |
  | Defo | 17 | HELPS 0.000194 [0.000016, 0.000372] (0.004933 vs 0.005126) | HELPS (1 yr) 0.000210 [0.000012, 0.000407] | - |
  | Coral | - | no continuity group in this run | | |

  Quote all of them or none. Sources: D:/treeroute/runs/_headline_tables/continuity_90e463075a20.csv (PM2.5), ndvi/continuity_a96f8e83dc35.csv, fire/continuity_e8f6c05681a3.csv, defo/continuity_5df3be88bf06.csv, coral/continuity_bbff151092ad.csv; engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md section 9.
  - **Naive baselines at the five declined cells (final; added 2026-11-10).** Read read-only on 2026-11-10 from the same CSVs (rows "all"; columns mae_adopted_pooled, mae_reference_pooled, mae_guess_normal_pooled, mae_last5_normal_pooled, mae_predict_zero_pooled, mae_persistence_pooled, mae_damped_persistence_pooled). The five file hashes begin with the 12 characters in their names, so the files are unchanged. A declined lead's final answer is the decline target (the guess-normal column matches it to rounding). At every declined cell at least one naive baseline has a lower pooled MAE than the final answer:
    - PM2.5, 12 months: final answer 4.641. Lower: damped persistence 4.415 and the last-5-years normal 4.508. Higher: persistence 5.234 and predict-zero 18.50.
    - NDVI, 6 months: final answer 0.04113. Lower: damped persistence 0.03993. Higher: the last-5-years normal 0.04177, persistence 0.1569 and predict-zero 0.4439.
    - NDVI, 12 months: final answer 0.04111. Lower: damped persistence 0.04002. Higher: the last-5-years normal 0.04208, persistence 0.06746 and predict-zero 0.4439.
    - Burned fraction, 6 months: final answer 0.005904. Lower: predict-zero 0.003111 and the last-5-years normal 0.005441. Higher: persistence 0.006189 and damped persistence 0.005907.
    - Burned fraction, 12 months: final answer 0.005880. Lower: predict-zero 0.003111, the last-5-years normal 0.005350 and persistence 0.005708. Higher: damped persistence 0.005889.
    - At the six cells that were not declined (PM2.5 nowcast and 6 months, NDVI nowcast, burned-fraction nowcast, forest-loss nowcast and 1 year), a naive baseline is lower than the final answer only at the burned-fraction nowcast (predict-zero, in the table above).
    - Forest loss at 1 year, the MAE pair the table lacks: final answer 0.004917 against its reference 0.005126.
    - Rows: each baseline is scored on the labelled rows where it has a value (engine/treeroute/report/metrics.py, score_table docstring, lines 855-860). The table builder reads each column's own rows_basis "all" line and drops its row count (engine/treeroute_report/table.py lines 345-359 and 637-639). The CSVs print no row count and no interval for a baseline. The nowcast rows print neither persistence nor damped persistence, so at the four nowcasts only the arithmetic normal, the last-5-years normal and predict-zero are compared.
- **P26 (final). Disclosure entries.** 45 entries in exceptions.yaml across the grids (pm25 11, fire 8, defo 9, ndvi 17), plus 5 defo entries under ruling T. Sources: Parts 583, 591.
- **P27 (final). Process overrides.**
  - Fire's shipped cell pick cannot be regenerated, because its seed used Python's string hash; the R18 stride is used instead (ruling C).
  - NDVI's late-refusal scan was skipped by owner override (Part 588).
- **P28 (final). What "pre-registered" can and cannot mean for this paper.**
  - What holds: every run's choices were written to an internal, hash-stamped pre-registration and locked before that run's test labels were opened (P1-P3, P17).
  - What does not: no problem's test window was fresh at design time. The NDVI architecture was chosen on 2020-2023 in a 701-idea campaign (PAPER_REFERENCE.md 8.12, lines 975-980); coral was designed with its test labels visible (engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT_coral.md sections 4.4, 10); several constants were set with test exposure (P23).
  - The documents are internal files with self-recorded timestamps. There is no third-party registry and no version control: `git rev-parse` fails in the project root and in engine/ ("not a git repository", checked 2026-10-26). The grid signature lines are blank (P6), and coral was signed after its run (P7). A public release is planned only at submission (APP_PLAN.md 7.1 "Reviewer release at submission", line 734).
  - Paper wording: "a locked-protocol evaluation: choices were hash-locked before each run's test labels were opened, but the test windows had earlier informed the NDVI architecture, the coral pipeline and several constants, so these results are not fully confirmatory". Do not put "pre-registered" in the title.
- **P29 (final, post hoc (Part 622); report-only). Sensitivity of the verdicts to the interval method.**
  - There are exactly **79 deciding verdicts**: 14 of ours (5 nowcasts and 9 forecast leads, 6 of them declined), 53 common-rival rows and 12 published-rival rows (12 of the 65 rival rows are IDENTICAL). No multiplicity correction is applied (P16). Say 79, not "about 80".
  - With a t(G-1) critical value instead of z = 1.96, **exactly 2 of the 79 tags change:**
    - Fire nowcast (40 groups): t39 = 2.023 gives [-1.2e-06, +8.58e-05] (two-sided p_t 0.0564; z gives a lower bound of +1.46e-07, p_z 0.0492). **HELPS becomes CANNOT TELL.** The report-only blocked jackknife also includes zero (R5).
    - Coral CRW (tier L) at 183 d, 32 groups: t31 gives [-0.07307, +3.773] (p_t 0.0588 vs p_z 0.0498). **BEATS becomes CANNOT TELL.**
    - Every other deciding verdict keeps its tag (PM2.5, NDVI, defo and coral nowcasts; every kept and declined lead; every other common and published-rival row).
  - Holm across the five nowcast verdicts: with z p-values all five survive (fire is last, adjusted p 0.0492 against 0.05); with t p-values fire fails (0.0564) and the other four survive.
  - The equivalence band as a share of the deciding reference MAE: PM2.5 1.20%, NDVI 1.52%, fire 0.31%, defo 1.93%, coral 11.06% (P15 bands over the R3 own-trees-off / donor-median MAEs). The band is computed from the same run's test-window arm contrasts (engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md lines 153-155).
  - Sources: Part 622; D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/POSTHOC_REPORT.md (P29); D:/treeroute/runs/_headline_tables/*/verdicts_*.csv (contrast_mean, contrast_se, n_groups, blocked_jackknife_se); RIVALS_TABLE.csv d_low/d_high and groups.

---

## 4. Data and the five problems

### 4.0 Shared substrate
- **D1 (final). Monthly driver panel.** The four grids read one panel: 133 regions, 2002-2023 (264 months), 2,926 files, 44 GB, 170 columns (166 drivers + 4 keys) in 45 product families (store catalogue; was 48, C19). Sources: engine/datasets/pm25/dataset.yaml; PAPER_REFERENCE.md 2.1; monthly_panel_v170_schema_catalog.yaml.
- **D2 (provisional). Cell size.** The panel cell is not 4 km everywhere: the lon step is 0.0359 deg, giving 4.00 km at the equator, 3.60 km at Thar and 1.45 km in Lapland. Source: acag_pm25_ingest.py docstring; NDVI static table, read 2026-10-26.
- **D3 (final). The seasonal normal's share of the signal.** The normal's pooled nowcast corr2 is NDVI 0.946, PM2.5 0.821, fire 0.219 and defo 0.0036. This is why anomaly R2 is reported. Sources: the _headline_tables headline CSVs.
- **D4 (final). Cross-problem hazards.**
  - SPEI ends 2022-12 and AMO ends January 2023, so both enter only as lag-12 columns (J2 a, K1); SPEI is standardised and AMO detrended over each product's whole record, so every lag-12 value still depends on data up to the record's end (pm25/exceptions.yaml line 38; ndvi/exceptions.yaml line 117; corrected 2026-10-27).
  - NoData sentinels in 47 of the 133 panel regions (coastal ones) are masked at read (G-SENTINEL). One greece_mainland unit still carries a sentinel in the fire rival export (Part 620).
  - Livestock 2022-23 values are India-scaled copies, so the 2021 layer is held (L3).
  - Held or switched human drivers: hdi after 2015, fert after 2013, ntl DMSP to VIIRS, WorldPop extrapolated after 2020.
  - luh2_urban acts as a year clock (median r with year 1.00).
  - Defo population_density (kept, lagged 1 year) is a year clock inside each region: median r with the year 1.00, r > 0.9 in 35 of 42 groups, every test year above the group's training maximum in 34 of 42 (engine/datasets/defo/exceptions.yaml line 38; measured 2026-10-05). On PM2.5, gdp_pc (keep) and population_density (watch) stay in without a year-clock measurement (engine/datasets/review/proposed/BAN_REVIEW_SUMMARY.md Q4). (Added 2026-10-27, S2 revision.)
  - Sources: OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-05.md J2, K1, L3; datasets/*/exceptions.yaml; memory reference_panel_nodata_sentinel.
  - **Round 5 fixes (2026-11-12), dated note.** "WorldPop extrapolated after 2020" (above) is right, and the step is now named: presplit_cc/ingest_worldpop_annual.py samples the WorldPop mosaics for 2002-2020 and writes copies of 2020 for 2021-2023 (lines 29 and 87-93); presplit_cc/extrapolate_worldpop.py --write then replaced those copies with its back-test winner (sidecars D:/worldpop_annual/pop_2021.parquet to pop_2023.parquet, written 2026-07-08), and ingest_worldpop_annual.py --inject wrote the sidecars into the panel column population_density. Read-only checks of 2026-11-12: the 2021/2020 ratio is one value in each region (spread below 1e-4, median 0, in all 126 regions with locations above 50 persons per pixel in 2020), 2023/2020 is the cube of 2021/2020, and each region's ratio equals, to six decimals in all 133 regions, the script's per-region growth from that region's 2014-2020 totals (K = 7; growth clamped to -2% and +5% a year); the panel files of alaska_interior_taiga, nile_valley_delta and uruguay hold 2021-2023 values that differ from 2020, with the regional mean rising each year, and uruguay's 2021 values equal the 2021 sidecar. Sources: the two scripts; D:/monthly_panel and D:/worldpop_annual (columns cell_id, month and population_density only); the round-5 check of the citation work (_archive_plan/_review_2026-11-11_round5/r5_06_check_cites.json, minor item 10), which had read only the first script.
- **D5 (final: owner ruling AT12, 2026-11-09).** The paper says the 133 panel regions were "chosen by the author to span biomes, land uses and hazards". This is the owner's own statement of intent; no written selection rule exists. Claims stay limited to these regions, and a map goes in the main text (AT13). The subsets of the 133 have their own written criteria: PM2.5's whole-region coverage rule (D10); burned fraction's fire-regime list with two arid controls, a written intent with no threshold (D24); and forest loss's forest-census threshold, at least 25% of cells with tree cover of 30% or more in 2010 (D46). The author set only the burned-fraction count; an agent drew up both lists (D24, D46; pointer added 2026-11-10, made exact later that day). Before the ruling: **How the 133 panel regions were chosen is not documented** in the files searched (PAPER_REFERENCE.md, MASTER_RESEARCH_RECORD.md, FINDINGS.md, CLAIMS_LEDGER.md, ARCHITECTURE_EXPLAINED.md; search 2026-10-26). Many names come from the phenomenon studied (e.g. borneo_peat_fires, idaho_montana_wildfire, angola_zambia_fire_belt in the verdict CSVs). The only written selection rule found is PM2.5's old test-region rule (split v3, CLAIMS_LEDGER PM-10), which no longer applies under v1 (M16) (superseded for the subsets by D24 and D46, 2026-11-10). The owner must supply the rule, or the paper says the regions were chosen by the authors, shows a map, and limits its claims to these regions.

### 4.1 PM2.5
- **D10 (final). Target.**
  - ACAG V5.GL.06 HybridPM25, monthly, ug/m3, from 0.01 deg pixels averaged per cell.
  - 127 regions and 2,034,972 cells. 6 polar or edge regions were dropped by a whole-region coverage rule.
  - Sources: engine/datasets/pm25/catalog.yaml; acag_pm25_ingest.py; CLAIMS_LEDGER.md PM-01 (Part 539).
- **D11 (provisional). The target is a model product.** It is satellite AOD x a GEOS-Chem ratio, fitted by GWR to about 10,870 monitors. GFED burned area is upstream of it, and six GWR-like covariates are watch columns. The circularity is bounded by argument, not by measurement. The monitor counts are literature figures that still need primary citations. Sources: CLAIMS_LEDGER.md PM-11, PM-01; engine/datasets/pm25/ban.yaml.
- **D12 (final). Test set.** 91,440 rows (1,905 units x 48 months), 12 without a label, mean 17.5 ug/m3, no zeros. Predict-zero MAE is 17.52, so predict-zero is not a meaningful bar here. Sources: pm25 test_preds.parquet; Part 584.
- **D13 (final). Trends.** 82 of 127 regions fell from 2002-05 to 2020-23, and 45 rose. The North China Plain fell about 5.5% a year over 2013-2019. Sources: CLAIMS_LEDGER.md PM-02; PAPER_NOTES_RIVALS.md.
- **D14 (final). Bans of note.**
  - The MODIS optical columns are conditional (out).
  - V20_burned_binary is banned: FireCCI switches sensor at the split.
  - grace_lwe is banned for an availability shift (16.2% of donor rows missing, 0% in test years); esacci_sm for an R30 flag (25.3% of donor rows missing, 30-32% in every test year) (corrected 2026-10-27; pm25/ban.yaml evidence).
  - population_density (watch) has an impossible maximum of 368,684.
  - Source: engine/datasets/pm25/ban.yaml.

### 4.2 Fire (burned fraction)
- **D20 (final). Target.** MCD64A1.061 burned 500 m pixels / valid pixels per cell, in [0, 1]. Month m's label uses month m+1 observations (R-D b), so history starts at t-2. Fire covers 40 regions and 925,737 units, 2002-2022; 2023 was never fetched. Sources: engine/datasets/fire/catalog.yaml, split.yaml; ruling B (R-D).
- **D21 (final). Zero-heavy.**
  - 98.1% of labelled nowcast test cell-months are zero: 1,759 burned cell-months in 33 of 40 regions.
  - Four savanna regions hold 56.6% of the burned mass.
  - 63.5% of scored units have a constant label history to the inner-fit end.
  - Sources: fire test_preds.parquet (98.1% and 1,759 match PAPER_NOTES_RIVALS.md, Part 606); derived.json R12:constant_history_share.
- **D22 (final). Predict-zero by year.** Predict-zero beats our nowcast MAE in 2019, 2020 and 2022. Ours is better only in 2021 (0.004590 vs 0.004674). Source: _headline_tables/fire/per_year_d657a6a40d37.csv; Part 590.
- **D23 (final). Drivers.** The panel plus a fire-history store at label lag 2.
  - Banned: last_burn_size (= y(t-L)); the single-month neighbour rings; eddi (its mask carries the month key); the lightning, VOD and biomass sidecar (incomplete).
  - Sources: engine/datasets/fire/ban.yaml; Part 585.
- **D24 (final; added 2026-11-10). How the 40 burned-fraction regions were chosen.** They are a subset of the 133 panel regions (D5). The target ingest names them in one list, with this comment: "40 regions spanning the fire regimes. Chosen for MECHANISM coverage, not for burned area -- including near-zero regions (deserts, tundra) is deliberate: they are the negative control that shows the model is not simply predicting 'somewhere flammable'" (fire_fetch_target.py lines 72-74).
  - The list has six labelled parts (lines 75-94): boreal / high-latitude 7; mediterranean / fire-adapted shrubland 7; savanna / grassland ("the global burned-area maximum") 13; temperate / continental 7; tropical forest / peat 4; "arid negative controls" 2 (thar_desert, gobi_desert).
  - So the rule is a written intent (cover the fire regimes, plus two arid controls), not a measured threshold.
  - Recounted read-only on 2026-11-10: the list's 40 names equal the 40 regions of D:/fire/target (840 files, 2002-2022) and the 40 groups of the old hand split (engine/legacy_harness/shipped_splits.yaml, 10 test + 30 donor). The fire package reads that store (engine/datasets/fire/dataset.yaml line 70; split.yaml: "D:/fire/target holds exactly 40 groups ... all 40 are trained and tested"), and its catalog.yaml (lines 54 and 75) cites this ingest.
  - Near-zero regions are not only the two named controls: seven regions have no burned area in their labelled test rows, thar_desert and gobi_desert among them (K63).
  - **Who chose (verified 2026-11-10 in one of the old machine's session transcripts (its file name, a session ID, is not written here); the transcripts hold private chat and are not archived).** The owner set the number: a proposal to fetch about 40 regions (line 1726, 2026-08-27T15:30:59Z) and then a decision to keep 40 regions (line 1768, 15:38:44Z). An agent picked the 40 names and wrote the list's comment: its Write call created fire_fetch_target.py at line 1823 (15:44:55Z). None of the owner's messages from line 1690 to line 1823 names a region. So the list is an agent choice unless the owner adopts it (Q13; S1 DA2).
  - Sources: fire_fetch_target.py (sha256 0c6f392ef60301cb..., 2026-08-28) lines 1-5, 70-94; D:/fire/target; engine/datasets/fire/dataset.yaml line 70, split.yaml, catalog.yaml lines 54 and 75; engine/legacy_harness/shipped_splits.yaml.
  - **Round 4 (2026-11-11), dated note.** Owner ruling AU3 (2026-11-11) keeps the list as a disclosed agent choice (S1 DA2; Q13 closed).

### 4.3 NDVI
- **D30 (final). Target.** MOD13C2.061 CMG 0.05 deg monthly NDVI, legal range [-0.2, 1.0], built from 16-day composites that reach about 15 days on each side. History starts at t-2. QA flags are never read. Sources: engine/datasets/ndvi/catalog.yaml; CLAIMS_LEDGER.md 2.11.
- **D31 (provisional). Effective resolution.** The ingest samples the CMG grid at the cell index rather than area-averaging. The NDVI pixel (~5.6 km) is coarser than the cell, so neighbours can share a pixel. Source: presplit_cc/ingest_modis_cmg_local.py:328-333 (inference from code).
- **D32 (final). Coverage.** 133 regions, 2,289,236 cells, 95,184 saved test rows (657 without a label). Source: Part 589.
- **D33 (final). Bans.**
  - The optical proxies (EVI, LST, albedo, GPP, MOD44B, MODIS land cover, phenology) are banned as co-products.
  - clf_dry/clf_wet are banned as target-derived.
  - A refit without the proxies drops in-sample R2 from 0.875 to 0.72, which is the circular greenness share (Parts 67-69).
  - Source: engine/datasets/ndvi/ban.yaml.
- **D34 (final). Legacy NDVI numbers are superseded.** The legacy "44 held-out regions" were 25 test + 19 donor regions (Part 426). Source: NDVI_BASELINE_REPORT.md correction notice.

### 4.4 Deforestation (defo)
- **D40 (final). Target.**
  - Hansen GFC v1.13 annual loss / year-2000 forest pixels (treecover >= 30%), in [0, 1].
  - It counts gross tree-cover loss, including fire; 26-29% of 2001-2019 loss was fire-driven (Tyukavina et al. 2022).
  - Each release re-derives loss years, so the series carries revision knowledge.
  - Sources: engine/datasets/defo/catalog.yaml; CLAIMS_LEDGER.md 3.4; DEFORESTATION_SCOPE.md section 4.
- **D41 (final). Why annual.** No sub-annual forest-loss product gives a non-circular target over both the training years and the 2020-2023 test window: the verified products covering 2020-2023 start in August 2016 or later (the earliest, DETER-Amazonia, covers one region), and Terra-i, the one product spanning the 2019/2020 boundary (16-day, 2004 to December 2022, Latin America only), is built from MODIS NDVI and precipitation, which are panel inputs. For Hansen loss, annual is the observation's native resolution. Source: DEFORESTATION_SCOPE.md sections 0-3 (probe date 2026-09-06). (Reworded 2026-10-28: the earlier 'spans 2016-2023' did not match the source.)
- **D42 (final). Coverage.** 42 forested regions, 591,809 cells, Y0 2004. 92,274 scored cells give 369,096 test cell-years. Sources: derived.json R7, R18; D:/defo_features/static.
- **D43 (final). Zero share and quantisation.**
  - The zero share of test cell-years is 62.1%. The scoping figure of 45.9% describes a different population (C7).
  - The sparsest forest quartile has model MAE 0.002925 vs predict-zero 0.002960.
  - Sources: defo test_preds.parquet; _headline_tables/defo/per_stratum_685bb0839a17.csv.
- **D44 (final). Bans and lags.**
  - Banned: forest2000_px, raw MOD44B, all LUH2 states, MODIS land cover, scPDSI, raw DeepRec, gdp_pc, CO2, food prices.
  - FireCCI burn enters at lag 1 across its 2020 sensor switch, with no visible step.
  - Sources: engine/datasets/defo/ban.yaml, exceptions.yaml; rulings K1-K5, N.
- **D45 (final). Low signal.** Defo is human-driven and has little seasonal signal: normal corr2 0.0036, model corr2 0.017. 13.8% of units have a constant history. Sources: defo headline CSV; derived.json R12.
- **D46 (final; added 2026-11-10; who chose, the census script and the store's fetch added later that day). How the 42 forest-loss regions were chosen.** They are the panel regions (D5) in which at least 25% of cells had tree cover of 30% or more in 2010. The record: a forest census over all 133 regions ("tree_cover_pct ≥ 30 at 2010"; artefact D:/run89full/forest_census.csv), whose "substantially forested (≥25%)" row gives 42 regions, 25 / 17 donor / test (DEFORESTATION_SCOPE.md section 7, lines 277-288). The old hand split applied it as forest_census frac_tc30 >= 0.25, keeping the NDVI split's roles (adaptation.py lines 1215-1219; engine/datasets/defo/AUDIT_CHECKLIST.md line 13; engine/datasets/defo/split.yaml).
  - Recounted read-only on 2026-11-10 from forest_census.csv (sha256 ca79ffe48f9438828ef3aae54013bc54d297ca3c7f573af7f9f5432d32a0eeb1, the hash split.yaml and engine/tests/test_packages_declarative.py pin; 133 rows): frac_tc30 >= 0.25 gives 42 regions (25 DONOR, 17 TEST), and they equal the 42 groups of D:/defo_target and of the old hand split (shipped_splits.yaml). The record's other rows also recount: >= 0.5 gives 22 (13 / 9), >= 0.1 gives 57 (38 / 19), and < 0.01 gives 44.
  - Nearest to the cut: caucasus_highlands 0.251 and poland_central 0.273 are in; western_ghats 0.241 and tuva_steppe 0.233 are out.
  - tree_cover_pct is the MODIS MOD44B tree-cover column (engine/datasets/_stores/monthly_panel_v170_schema_catalog.yaml line 484). Raw MOD44B is banned as a forest-loss predictor (D44); here it only selects regions, with a 2010 value from before every test year.
  - The census was an agent's scratchpad script, not a project file (a search of the project folder for its column names found none, 2026-11-10). Its text and output survive in one of the old machine's session transcripts (verified 2026-11-10; not archived; its file name, a session ID, is not written here): line 2222 of it is the Write call that created forest_census.py (2026-09-05T20:26:20Z), which "Reads ONE year per region"; line 2234 holds its output (20:26:47Z), headed "FOREST CENSUS | tree_cover_pct at 2010 | 'forested cell' = tree_cover_pct >= 30". forest_census.csv was written at 20:26:44Z (its LastWriteTimeUtc), between the two. So "at 2010" rests on that script's output and on DEFORESTATION_SCOPE.md line 279.
  - The same cut built the store the package reads: defo_fetch.py lines 47-49 and 84 (CENSUS = "D:/run89full/forest_census.csv"; FRAC_MIN = 0.25; frac_tc30 >= FRAC_MIN) and defo_ingest.py lines 231-232 (frac_tc30 >= 0.25); engine/datasets/defo/dataset.yaml line 51 (target 'D:/defo_target/{group}.parquet').
  - **Who chose (verified 2026-11-10 in the same transcript).** An agent ran the census and proposed the set: "42 regions substantially forested (≥25% of cells) — 25 donor / 17 test" (line 2242, 2026-09-05T20:27:01Z), and built the target fetch on it (line 2412, 2026-09-06T10:19:02Z). The owner's messages in lines 2180-2412 (lines 2186, 2205, 2250 and 2347; the others are task notifications) set no cut. So the cut is an agent choice unless the owner adopts it (Q13; S1 DA2).
  - Sources: DEFORESTATION_SCOPE.md lines 277-288; D:/run89full/forest_census.csv; adaptation.py lines 1215-1219; engine/datasets/defo/AUDIT_CHECKLIST.md line 13, split.yaml; engine/legacy_harness/shipped_splits.yaml; D:/defo_target.
  - **Round 4 (2026-11-11), dated note; no number of this fact changed.** (1) The census's columns and their sources (round-3 text check of the archive promises, minor item 3; the file's header and SHA-256 re-read read-only on 2026-11-11, unchanged; the script's text read in the transcript named above, line 2222): region, split and cells, then tc_mean, tc_p90, frac_tc30 and frac_tc10, the mean, the 90th percentile and the shares of cells at 30 or more and at 10 or more of the panel column tree_cover_pct (MODIS MOD44B; NASA terms: cite the data and acknowledge NASA, L6), and luh2_forest, the mean of the panel column luh2_forest (LUH2-GCB2025, CC BY 4.0, L6), each over one value per cell of the region's 2010 panel file. (Plan; provisional until built.) The archive plan files the census in records/earlier_runs/ (section 2.6), so the archive would redistribute these per-region statistics, which the two products' terms allow with citation and attribution; Section 9 (Licences), the Software and data availability section, the declarations and Supplement S9.6 (item 7) name them since round 4. (2) The paper texts say "no archived script writes it" (the lead's decision LD2 of 2026-11-11, not an owner ruling), because the census script survives only in that transcript. (3) Owner ruling AU3 (2026-11-11) keeps the cut as a disclosed agent choice (S1 DA2; Q13 closed). (Tier 1 length plan (2026-11-12, ruling AU7), dated note: the Software and data availability section now names the census only as one of the input tables of the forest-loss rebuild that no archived script writes (A4-26); its per-region MOD44B and LUH2 statistics are named in Supplement S9 (S9.6, item 7), which holds in substance the section's paragraph on third-party values and its licence table (A4-30, A4-31). No number of this fact changed.)

### 4.5 Coral bleaching
- **D50 (final). Target.** Percent bleached per survey visit, from GCBD SQLite 11_24_21 (CC0). The programmes used are Reef Check, AGRRA, FRRP, McClanahan and Donner. Sources: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT_coral.md section 2; CORAL_LABEL_AUDIT.md:4.
- **D51 (final). Census.**
  - 14,839 visits. 1,006 have no CoralTemp SST series, and 101 have under 10 years of SST; neither is written.
  - The lead-0 file has 13,694 rows in 94 groups.
  - Sources: D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_data/visits_census.json; engine/datasets/coral/exceptions.yaml.
- **D52 (final). Groups and split.** 83 groups (37 scored + 46 donor-only), 11 excluded, 11,338 train / 2,312 test visits. Sources: split.yaml; rulings V5, W1.
- **D53 (provisional). The programme confound.**
  - The drivers identify the programme (AUC 0.948).
  - Test visits: Reef_Check 1,932, FRRP 190, McClanahan 189, AGRRA 1.
  - McClanahan has 0 training rows.
  - Sources: engine/datasets/coral/exceptions.yaml; CORAL_STATE.md:30-34; lead_0.parquet counts (computed 2026-10-26, not a Part).
- **D54 (final). Test years and the 2016 event.**
  - Test visits by year: 2016 873, 2017 565, 2018 443, 2019 431.
  - 55.7% are zero, and 140 are at least 50% bleached.
  - By year at the nowcast (ours / donor median, our deciding reference / predict-zero): 2016 12.41 / 16.83 / 17.08; **2017 3.57 / 3.23 / 2.81; 2018 1.28 / 1.51 / 0.84; 2019 4.81 / 4.63 / 4.30.**
  - At 183 d (ours / donor median): 2016 14.95 / 16.83; **2017 3.47 / 3.23**; 2018 1.49 / 1.51; **2019 5.00 / 4.63.**
  - So the gain is concentrated in 2016 (with a smaller gain in 2018). The model is worse than the donor median in 2017 and 2019 at both kept leads, and predict-zero beats it in 2017-2019.
  - Sources: _headline_tables/coral/per_year_7dde14b4e308.csv (mae_adopted, mae_reference, mae_predict_zero); Parts 597, 601.
- **D55 (final). Features.** 63 tree columns: SST heat stress against a leak-free expanding MMM, heat history, marine heatwaves, IBTrACS cyclones, and lagged Nino-3.4. Banned: programme and three lifetime-DHW trend proxies. MMM in the router context is a disclosed departure. Sources: engine/datasets/coral/ban.yaml; engine/CORAL_ENGINE_PLAN.md C3.
- **D56 (final). Timing caveats.**
  - CoralTemp's content reach is unverified.
  - ERSST V6's +2-month reach is assumed.
  - **The one-day buffer (final; ruling V9; added 2026-11-10).** Coral's SST and cyclone feature windows end before C - g, with g = 1 day: one day earlier than the issue date alone would allow. C is the feature cut, C = A - L, with L the lead: for a visit with a known day, A is the survey date, so C is the issue date; for a month-precision visit, A is the month's first day (build_tables.py docstring lines 20-22). The reason: CoralTemp days and IBTrACS track times are in UTC, while survey dates are local; in the west Pacific, UTC day C - 1 runs to about midday local time on day C. So the SST windows end before C - 1, and a cyclone window of d days is [C - 1 - d, C - 1). The index lags are whole months and are not affected.
    - The buffer covers the UTC date stamp, not CoralTemp's unverified analysis reach (first item), which stays disclosed apart. It applies to every SST, MMM and Hobday column and to ctx_mmm. It is one day more than the legacy rule (g = 0; coral_forecast.py:110).
    - Ruling V9 (OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md line 111): "Keep the 1-day SST gap, the assumed ERSST V6 timing and DMI as conditional, all disclosed."
    - Checked read-only on 2026-11-10: the builder's default is g = 1 (D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_tables/build_tables.py line 801, --sst-gap-days, and docstring lines 20-25; sha256 b1f22ee7..., the original that the Part 627 lock pins). The store the coral package reads (engine/datasets/coral/dataset.yaml line 26: D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_store/20261020_014201) records "sst_gap_days": 1 (its build_manifest.json line 61). The engine refuses a row whose feature_end is not before its issue date (T-FEATEND; exceptions.yaml line 100).
  - **DMI is held out (final; ruling V9; added 2026-11-10).** dmi_lag2, dmi_lag5 and dmi_lag8 are conditional, so they are out of every run until the owner signs their window bound {3, month, centred} (engine/datasets/coral/ban.yaml lines 306-317; exceptions.yaml lines 59-64).
  - IBTrACS best tracks are revised after each season.
  - Every forecast is conditional on a survey happening.
  - 2,128 train visits have month precision only.
  - Sources: engine/datasets/coral/AUDIT_CHECKLIST.md section B; exceptions.yaml (the buffer: lines 65-70, dhw_56, and 97-102, feature_end; DMI: lines 59-64); OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md line 111 (V9); D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_tables/build_tables.py; D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_store/20261020_014201/build_manifest.json; engine/datasets/coral/ban.yaml.
