### 4.6 The study-region map (Figure 1)

- **D57 (final; added 2026-11-10 from paper/figures/fig_map/FACTS_TO_ADD.md, where the proposed facts were recounted read-only; fig_map.py recounts and asserts them on every run, except values marked there as read once). Study-region map: which problems use each region.**
  - **F1 of the map's record (Region membership combinations (133 panel regions)).**
    - pm25+ndvi 60; pm25+ndvi+defo 28; pm25+ndvi+fire 26; pm25+ndvi+fire+defo 13; ndvi only 4; ndvi+fire 1; ndvi+defo 1 (sum 133).
    - Every PM2.5, burned-fraction and forest-loss region is one of the 133 NDVI regions.
    - Sources: distinct `region` values of engine/datasets/pm25/data/static/part-0.parquet (127), engine/datasets/ndvi/data/static/part-0.parquet (133), engine/datasets/fire/data/static/part-0.parquet (40) and D:/defo_features/static/part-0.parquet (42).
    - Method: for each NDVI region, the list of problems whose static table holds that region, counted by list.
  - **F2 of the map's record (The map's four categories (by burned fraction and forest loss)).**
    - Burned fraction and forest loss 13; burned fraction only 27 (26 + finnish_lapland); forest loss only 29 (28 + tierra_del_fuego); neither 64 (60 + the four NDVI-only regions).
    - Checks: 13 + 27 = 40 (D20, D24); 13 + 29 = 42 (D42, D46); 13 + 27 + 29 + 64 = 133 (D5, D32).
    - Source and method: F1, grouped by membership of the fire and defo static tables.
  - **F3 of the map's record (The six regions without PM2.5 (D10's "6 polar or edge regions")).**
    - alaska_north_slope, canadian_arctic_nunavut, taimyr_peninsula, yamal_peninsula: NDVI only.
    - finnish_lapland: NDVI and burned fraction. tierra_del_fuego: NDVI and forest loss.
    - Caption display names: Alaska North Slope, Canadian Arctic (Nunavut), Taimyr Peninsula, Yamal Peninsula, Finnish Lapland, Tierra del Fuego.
    - Method: NDVI regions minus PM2.5 regions (F1 sources).
- **D58 (final; added 2026-11-10 from paper/figures/fig_map/FACTS_TO_ADD.md, where the proposed facts were recounted read-only; fig_map.py recounts and asserts them on every run, except values marked there as read once). Study-region map: footprints, geometry and the clipping to the coastline.**
  - **F4 of the map's record (Locations behind the footprints, footprint geometry and the clipping to the coastline).**
    - PM2.5 and forest loss use exactly the NDVI locations of their regions (2,034,972 and 591,809 locations). Burned fraction uses a subset in 26 of its 40 regions, at least 97.0% (minimum 0.9704) of the NDVI locations in each: 925,737 of 929,533 overall. So the map draws every footprint from the NDVI locations.
    - Method: join on (region, lat, lon) rounded to 6 decimals; no PM2.5, burned-fraction or forest-loss location is missing from the NDVI table; locations counted per region.
    - Footprint = the 0.25° pixels holding at least one NDVI location of the region: 38,207 occupied pixels (38,469 region-pixel pairs). 262 pixels belong to two regions (never more), in 13 pairs; the largest overlap is australian_mulga with brigalow_belt (168 pixels).
    - Clipping: each footprint pixel is cut into 8 x 8 cells of 1/32° (about the panel's 4 km spacing), and a cell is drawn when its centre lies inside a GSHHG 2.3.7 low-resolution level-1 polygon (F8). Only the sea clips; lakes do not. 71 regions keep every cell; the least kept are falkland_islands 0.516, greece_mainland 0.704, western_scotland 0.765, tongass_alaska 0.775 and new_zealand_north_island 0.790 (share of cells kept). falkland_islands keeps 1,486 of its 2,880 cells. Method: an even-odd scanline fill of the level-1 rings at the cell centres (exact for the centres).
    - Drawing order: larger footprints (more 0.25° pixels) first, so a smaller overlapping region stays on top. After the clipping, every region shows at least 0.830 of its drawn cells; the least is australian_mulga (under brigalow_belt).
    - Region extent (NDVI locations): median 3.04° latitude by 4.02° longitude, maximum 9.04° by 12.00°; latitudes -55.00 to 75.02; no region crosses the antimeridian. Smallest short side: namib_desert 0.75°, falkland_islands 0.95°, okavango_delta 1.02°, brittany_france 1.20°, sundarbans 1.43° (all in "neither"); smallest textured region: tongass_alaska (forest loss only), 1.52°.
- **D59 (final; added 2026-11-10 from paper/figures/fig_map/FACTS_TO_ADD.md, where the proposed facts were recounted read-only; fig_map.py recounts and asserts them on every run, except values marked there as read once). Study-region map: the coral bleaching reef sites drawn, and those under regions.**
  - **F5 of the map's record (Coral bleaching reef sites drawn).**
    - 8,131 distinct reef sites in the 83 kept coral bleaching regions: 7,065 in the 37 scored regions, 1,066 in the 46 training-only regions. 1,262 of the 8,131 have at least one test survey visit.
    - The 1,066 equals the header of engine/datasets/coral/split.yaml: "1,874 train visits (1,066 sites".
    - The 11 excluded regions hold 44 lead-0 rows at 26 reef sites, not drawn.
    - Source: D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_store/20261020_014201/lead_0.parquet (the path engine/datasets/coral/dataset.yaml gives for lead 0), columns unit_id, group, lat, lon, mark: 13,694 rows in 94 regions (D51); the 83 kept regions hold 13,650 rows, 11,338 train and 2,312 test (D52). Kept and excluded lists: split.yaml.
    - Method: a reef site is the package's unit_id, its key_unit '<ecoregion id>_<lat>_<lon>' at 4 decimals (K1). Each unit_id has one position and one region, unit_ids and positions (lat, lon at 6 decimals) are one-to-one, and every unit_id ends in its own lat and lon at 4 decimals. A scored region is a kept region with at least one test-marked lead-0 row (37; D52). Every reef site is one site_key of D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_data/visits.parquet in the same region. Reef-site latitudes -28.16 to 34.10. fig_map_data.csv gives the unit_id as site_id.
  - **F7 of the map's record (Reef sites under the regions (the regions are drawn above the dots)).**
    - 229 of the 8,131 reef-site centres lie in a drawn region (after the clipping): florida_everglades 188, pearl_river_delta 24, yucatan_peninsula 9, vietnam_central_highlands 6, new_guinea_papua 1, caatinga 1.
    - By coral bleaching region: Bahamas and Florida Keys 188 of its 2,848 reef sites; Hong Kong 24 of 80 (30%, the largest share); Belize and west Caribbean 9 of 598; South Vietnam 6 of 39; Bismarck Sea, New Guinea 1 of 25; Brazil 1 of 109. No other coral bleaching region has a covered reef site. The script stops if any coral bleaching region has more than half of its reef sites covered.
    - For comparison, 1,062 before the clipping (whole 0.25° pixels), all 80 of Hong Kong's among them: that coral bleaching region was hidden under pearl_river_delta.
    - Method: a reef site is covered when its 1/32° cell is a drawn cell of a region (F4); no reef site lies under two regions, and none under an open outline. The caption states 229, the 188 "in south Florida" and Hong Kong's 24 of 80. Listed per reef site in fig_map_data.csv (column covered_by_region). A covered dot (1.7 pt, about 1.2° at the map scale) still shows wherever it reaches past the region's edge. (Tier 1 length plan (2026-11-12, ruling AU7), dated note: the caption now states 229 and the 188 in south Florida; the clause with Hong Kong's 24 of 80 moved verbatim to a note after Table S9.3's GSHHG row in Supplement S9.3 (A1-08), and fig_map.py's caption check was changed to match. No number of this fact changed.)
- **D60 (final; added 2026-11-10 from paper/figures/fig_map/FACTS_TO_ADD.md, where the proposed facts were recounted read-only; fig_map.py recounts and asserts them on every run, except values marked there as read once). The coral visits table versus the coral package (why the map reads the lead-0 rows).**
  - **F6 of the map's record (visits.parquet versus the coral package (why the map reads the lead-0 rows)).**
    - visits.parquet holds 14,839 survey visits (D51) at 8,572 site keys in 97 regions: the 83 kept, the 11 excluded, and 3 on neither list of split.yaml (Darwin, north Australia; Easter Island, south central Pacific; South Java).
    - In the 83 kept regions its marks are train 12,168, test 2,581 and unused 38.
    - Gulf of Thailand has 10 test-marked survey visits (219 train, 2 unused) in visits.parquet but 152 train rows and no test row in the lead-0 file. Taking visits.parquet's marks would therefore give 38 scored regions, not D52's 37.
    - The kept regions have 8,533 reef sites with a train or test visit in visits.parquet, 402 more than the 8,131 the package uses.
    - Method: counts of visits.parquet columns group, mark, site_key, lat, lon (reef sites as distinct lat, lon at 6 decimals); Gulf of Thailand compared with its lead-0 rows.
- **D61 (final; added 2026-11-10 from paper/figures/fig_map/FACTS_TO_ADD.md, where the proposed facts were recounted read-only; fig_map.py recounts and asserts them on every run, except values marked there as read once). The map's coastline input (GSHHG 2.3.7).**
  - **F8 of the map's record (Coastline input).**
    - GSHHG 2.3.7: README.TXT says "Version 2.3.7 June 15, 2017" and "Distributed under the Lesser GNU Public License"; LICENSE.TXT says GSHHG is distributed under the GNU Lesser General Public License (LGPL) version 3 or later (from GSHHG 2.2.2 on).
    - Low resolution, all polygons, read and counted: level 1: 5,706 records (land and ocean boundary; drawn, and the only level that clips the regions); level 2: 4,367 records (lakes); level 3: 506 records (islands in lakes); level 4: 11 records (ponds on those islands).
    - Lakes (levels 2-4) are not drawn, as the figure brief named level 1 only, and the caption says "Lakes are not drawn." The Caspian Sea and the large lakes therefore show as land. For the lead to decide: setting LAKES = True in fig_map.py draws them (lakes white, islands in lakes as land, ponds white) and the script then requires the credit "Coastlines and lakes: ..." without "Lakes are not drawn." in the caption. Against it: README.TXT says the lake polygons derive from WDBII, an older database, so the Aral Sea is drawn at its full former extent (level-2 polygon id 5837, area 67,543 km² in the attribute table GSHHS_l_L2.dbf; read once, not asserted), about 2 mm across at print, where today's lake is far smaller.
      - **Ruling AV (2026-11-13), dated note.** Settled by owner ruling AV24 (2026-11-12): the lakes are left out of the region map, because the lake data are old, so the Aral Sea would be drawn at its former size. The choice this item left to the lead is closed.
    - Source: D:/coral/drivers/gshhg/gshhg-shp-2.3.7.zip, read in memory with Python's zipfile module and a struct-based shapefile reader; nothing was extracted.
    - Licence note for the lead: LICENSE.TXT also asks that the copyright and permission notice appear in all copies of the data and its documentation. The figure is a rendering, not a copy of the data; the caption's credit names the source, version and licence. README.TXT's history note for version 2.2.2 says the data are "now released under the lesser GNU License, v3 or any earlier version", while LICENSE.TXT says "version 3 or later"; the caption follows LICENSE.TXT.

Sources: coral_report/coral_report.md lead 0 ALL rows (lines 17-22); coral/headline/20261020_f3b8e3da/jackknife.csv.

Only 2 of 12 arms were eligible (both arcsine GLS; R1). Source: fire adoption.json (surface_mae, beside/predict_zero_mae, eligible). On test, every log-link tree arm was worse than the log trees-off in the run's own pre-registered band-pair contrasts: plain and corrector unweighted HURT (-4.19e-05 [-6.88e-05, -1.50e-05] and -4.03e-05 [-6.66e-05, -1.40e-05]); plain and corrector GLS CANNOT TELL (both point estimates negative). Source: fire/headline/20261017_869d1f38/jackknife.csv.

Sources: Part 622; D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/POSTHOC_REPORT.md (R12); fire test_preds.parquet and score_pooled.csv.

    - Adopted vs predict-zero: d -0.000104 [-0.00062, +0.000413], CANNOT TELL; the adopted arm is better in 3 of 40 regions.
    - So both baselines have a lower pooled MAE than the adopted arm, but on the per-region estimand neither difference can be told apart from zero.

- **R14 (final, post hoc (Part 622); arithmetic from the frozen formula). Implied absolute penalties** of the adopted points: PM2.5 a_tree 15 / a_ctx 15; NDVI 0.5 / 0.03125; fire 50 / 50; defo lambda 10; coral 50 / 50. Both Part 622 scripts asserted for every run that the adopted penalty equals the best tree arm's penalty, that the nowcast did not decline and that the point lies in the run's derived R14 grid. The context centre (0.25) does not enter: a_ctx = a_tree x ratio. M47 (the same point pinned at the later leads) was not re-checked. Sources: Part 622; route/kinds.py point_penalties applied to the adoption.json points and derived.json R14.
- **R15 (final, post hoc (Part 622); the cheap ablation table of ruling AK4(a)). Every saved column on test at the nowcast (pooled MAE), and the regret of the adopted arm.** Part 622 scored every saved column, 89 in all (PM2.5 17, NDVI 10, fire 24, defo 15, coral 23: arms including each link's trees-off, placebos and baselines), each equal to the run's score_pooled.csv, with anomaly R2, corr2 and hit rate beside. This is the ablation that the saved rows allow; it is NOT a test of the per-unit axis (M56). The real per-unit ablation (AK4(b), Q10) was dropped for submission (ruling AR, 2026-11-09) and is future work.
  - **Regret of the adopted arm** (adopted MAE minus the best selectable tree arm's MAE): PM2.5 0.00061 (0.017%), NDVI 0, fire 8.93e-05 (+2.08%), defo 0, coral 0.5505 (+8.95%).
  - PM2.5: best arm log corrector GLS 3.5875 vs adopted log corrector unweighted 3.5881 (regret 0.00061). Plain vs corrector: 3.5965 vs 3.5881 (unweighted). Identity arms 3.724-3.778; log trees-off 3.853.
  - NDVI: the adopted identity plain unweighted (0.03146) is the best arm (regret 0). Other arms 0.03148-0.03151.
  - Fire: best tree arm log corrector GLS 0.004296 vs adopted arcsine corrector GLS 0.004385 (regret 8.93e-05, +2.08%); all four log tree arms (0.004296-0.004322) beat the adopted arm; the log trees-off (0.004281) beats them all. Identity arms 0.00648-0.00669, worse than the identity normal 0.006259.
  - Defo: adopted log corrector 0.0054158 < log plain 0.0054164 (6.1e-07 apart; regret 0, the adopted arm is the best). Arcsine arms 0.00573; identity arms 0.00791-0.00797 (worse than predict-zero 0.00612).
  - Coral: every unweighted arcsine or log arm (6.147-6.376) beats the adopted arcsine corrector GLS (6.698); best arcsine corrector unweighted 6.147 (regret 0.5505, +8.95%; the A7 reversal).
  - Reading: GLS weighting helped on PM2.5 by 0.0006 and hurt on coral by 0.55; the corrector adds nothing beyond its placebo (R13); the link choice decided the fire result (R12).
  - Sources: Part 622; D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/POSTHOC_REPORT.md (R15); D:/treeroute/runs/<pkg>/headline/<run>/test_preds.parquet and score_pooled.csv, lead 0, rows with a label. Predict-zero hit rates from the same tables: K66.

  - By test year: coral nowcast 2017 (3.569 vs 3.231) and 2019 (4.806 vs 4.627); coral 183 d 2017 (3.465 vs 3.231) and 2019 (4.996 vs 4.627); PM2.5 lead 6 2022 (3.8855 vs 3.8851) and 2023 (4.094 vs 4.042). Every other kept lead is better than its reference in every test year (PM2.5 nowcast, NDVI nowcast, fire nowcast, defo nowcast and 1 yr).
  - By group at the nowcast (sign_flip_groups = groups where the model is worse than the reference): fire 13 of 40, coral 14 of 37, PM2.5 19 of 127, NDVI 11 of 133, defo 0 of 42.

- **R17 (final). At every declined lead, a naive baseline printed in our own tables beat the decline answer.**
  - PM2.5 lead 12: damped persistence 3.835 vs our answer 4.084.
  - NDVI leads 6 and 12: damped persistence 0.03862 / 0.03882 vs 0.03957 / 0.03958.
  - Fire leads 6 and 12: predict-zero 0.004284 vs 0.006312 / 0.006311 (the L5 normal, 0.005983 / 0.005957, also beats it).
  - Coral 365 d: predict-zero 8.100 vs the donor median 8.294.
  - So 6 of 6 decline answers lost to a naive baseline. Pooled over the test window the guarded answer was never worse than its own deciding reference (the kept leads beat it; the declined leads equal it). Six of the 9 common-board losses (RC8) are rivals beating our declined normal (PM2.5 lead 12 x3, NDVI lead 6 Ridge, fire leads 6 and 12 Tweedie).
  - Sources: the R3 headline CSVs (mae_damped_persistence, mae_predict_zero, mae_last5_normal); coral/headline_344838cf7de1.csv; RIVALS_TABLE.csv.

## 6. Rival comparison: common board

- **RC1 (final). Protocol.**
  - Signed on 2026-10-21 under harness 46d0306a, before any rival test score. The first lock came 6 minutes after the signature (Part 598).
  - The lean board: Ridge (all problems); RF (300 oneDAL trees); LightGBM (255 bins); LightGBM-Tweedie (only where legal_min = 0 and at least 20% of labels are zero: fire 96.8%, defo 62%, coral 40%); hgb_l1 (coral only, "timid-prone").
  - Dropped for machine time: XGBoost, CatBoost, the MLP and the donor-only board (AA1).
  - Sources: D:/treeroute/registry/rivals/PREREGISTRATION_rivals_common_20261021T125123Z.md; rulings Y-AD.
- **RC2 (final). Information parity.**
  - Rivals fit on the run's own bank-unit rows up to each cut.
  - They see the tree columns, the history block, the router context (including lat, which is banned from our trees) and the normal (as an offset and as a column).
  - They get no unit ID. The board is global, so a rival sees the test region's own pre-test rows.
  - Source: common rival Part section 3, section 9.
- **RC3 (final). Tuning.** Rivals are tuned once, at lead 0, on the inner window only, by clipped MAE. The pick is pinned at every lead. Every rival is reported; there is no "best rival" headline. Source: common rival Part section 3 items 8, 17.
- **RC4 (final). Symmetric guard.** Each rival gets our guard on its own guard rows. Its guarded answer is the raw prediction if it passes, otherwise the normal. Guarded vs our headline decides; raw vs raw is printed beside (Q11 c). Source: common rival Part section 3 item 13.
- **RC5 (final). Locks and parity checks.**
  - Picks are hashed before any test file is read, under a per-run ledger.
  - Parity checks P1-P15 and I1-I9 are run.
  - PM2.5 was accepted with I4 and I5 refused, conditional on P1-P15 passing at every lead. They passed, and the seal ACCEPTED_ALL_LEADS was written (ruling Z1; Part 610).
  - Sources: D:/treeroute/rivals/_ledger/*.jsonl; D:/treeroute/rivals_build/SPEC_RIVALS.md 2.2, 2.7.
- **RC6 (final). Disclosed tilts in the rivals' favour.**
  - The any-round pick is made on the same inner rows as the rival's guard.
  - Patience 150 can stop a trial early.
  - The fit-cut c is read at tuning.
  - oneDAL RF differs from stock sklearn.
  - The 63-bin tuning shortcut was withdrawn before any score, because it weakened the rival (AA3 to AC1).
  - Source: common rival Part sections 9, 10b. The c item is shared with our engine, so it is parity rather than a tilt: K81.
- **RC6a (final). Tilts AGAINST the rivals, which RC6 does not list.**
  - **Search budget.** Our layer searched 45-424 inner-window candidates per run (rows of inner_candidates.csv: defo 45, NDVI 141, PM2.5 282, fire 423, coral 424; of which 3, 1, 2, 3 and 4 are non-selectable reference fits), after a design phase that had seen the test window (P23, P28). The rivals searched **4-24** settings per learner on narrow grids (K75): on a 3-link run (fire, defo, coral) Ridge 24, RF 12, LightGBM 12, Tweedie 8, hgb_l1 12; on PM2.5 (2 links) Ridge 16, RF 8, LightGBM 8; on NDVI (1 link) 8, 4, 4. LightGBM also picks its round count (any round up to the 800 cap, 1,600 after extension) inside each trial. (Corrected 2026-10-26: the earlier "8-24" held for the three-link problems only; draft/review_logs/rivals_review_log.md item 18 and limits_review_log.md item 2.)
  - **Fixed rival hyperparameters:** LightGBM learning rate 0.1, feature_fraction 0.5, no bagging, a 2 x 2 grid (num_leaves {31, 127} x min_child_samples {20, 200}); RF 300 trees with max_features {0.2, 0.5} x min_samples_leaf {5, 50}; hgb_l1 learning rate 0.1.
  - **No local per-unit statistical rival outside NDVI**, although per-unit fitting is the claimed novel axis (M56). The only local rival is BARDL (NDVI).
  - **The only deep-learning rival is weaker in information:** the Kraft LSTM reads no NDVI history ("it reads no NDVI, so it is weaker in information than H1 and the engine", D:/treeroute/rivals_plan_human/research_ndvi.md lines 61-62), yet it is tiered P (parity). Its raw MAE 0.0607 is 54% worse than the normal 0.0394 (RH4).
  - XGBoost, CatBoost and the MLP were dropped for machine time (RC1).
  - Sources: D:/treeroute/registry/rivals/PREREGISTRATION_rivals_common_20261021T125123Z.md section 2 (lines 65-74); D:/treeroute/runs/<pkg>/headline/<run>/inner_candidates.csv (row counts, 2026-10-26).

## 7. Rival comparison: human and published rivals

- **RH1 (final). Part A was signed before any human score.**
  - Signed on 2026-10-25 (sha 4db89965...; tool 32ec5df0), and byte-identical to the draft.
  - Tiers: P = parity (decides). L = lagged on purpose (decides, labelled). A = input-advantaged (never decides). X = labelled extension (never decides).
  - Sources: Part 617; engine/RIVALS_PLAN_HUMAN.md sections 2-3.
- **RH2 (final). Calibration maps F0-F6** are fitted per lead on train rows only. (Precision added 2026-10-26: signed Part A has F0-F5; F6 is fire-only and belongs to the unsigned Part B, K58; draft/review_logs/protocol_review_log.md item 20.) A group decides only with at least 10 covered test rows. Source: human Part A sections 3, 5, 8.
- **RH3 (final). Timing parity.**
  - T1: drivers end at or before issue - S.
  - T2: target observations end at or before the newest readable label.
  - An A row must break one of them.
  - Source: human Part A section 4.
- **RH4 (final). The human deciding rows.** d = rival - ours on the rival's rows; "ours" is our MAE on those rows.
  - **Coral crw_dhw (P), nowcast:** 8.089 vs ours 6.471. CANNOT TELL, d 1.27 [-1.28, 3.82].
  - **Coral crw_dhw (L, "NOAA's nowcast rule fed old heat"), 183 d:** BEATS, 7.977 vs 7.397, d 1.85 [0.0019, 3.70]. The rival's guard FAILED, so its answer is the normal. Raw 9.248 also BEATS.
  - **Coral 365 d:** IDENTICAL (both answers are the normal).
  - **Defo VT0007, all three forms, leads 0 and 1:** BEATS, 0.008405 vs 0.005416 / 0.005408. Every guard FAILED. Raw also BEATS every form; the calibrated raw is closest (0.005676, d 0.00026 [0.00013, 0.00039]).
  - **PM2.5 MERRA-2 lag 1 (P):** 8.444 vs 3.588. CANNOT TELL, d 4.86 [-4.15, 13.86].
  - **NDVI BARDL rain-lag H1 (P):** BEATS, 0.0342 vs 0.0315, d 0.00275 [0.00224, 0.00326]. **Guard passed: the only human WIN over a method that beat the normal.**
  - **NDVI Kraft LSTM (P):** BEATS, but its guard FAILED (answer = normal 0.0394; raw 0.0607).
  - Row totals over the deciding rows: **WIN 9, cannot tell 2, same 1. 8 of the 9 WINs are over guard-failed rivals.** Use these row counts in Table 7 only; never as a tally in the abstract or main text (the rows are not independent comparisons). (Tier 1 length plan (2026-11-12, ruling AU7), dated note: Table 7's note (a) now only points to Supplement S6.9, which prints these row counts, so the paper prints them only in S6.9 (A3-09).)
  - **Counted by published method (the form to use in the text):**
    - **One real win:** NDVI BARDL H1, the only published method that beat the seasonal normal; ours beat it.
    - **Two methods failed their skill guard (so their answer is the seasonal normal), while ours passed and beat it:** VT0007 (defo), the Kraft LSTM (NDVI).
    - **Two inconclusive at the nowcast:** NOAA CRW DHW; MERRA-2 at t-1 (PM2.5).
    - **NOAA's rule at 183 d (tier L, "nowcast rule fed old heat") is an entry of a method already counted:** its guard failed and ours beat it (BEATS; CANNOT TELL under the post hoc t interval, P29).
    - **Five published methods decided** (BARDL, VT0007, the Kraft LSTM, NOAA's DHW rule, MERRA-2). Owner ruling AM2 (2026-10-27): NOAA's rule is counted once. The earlier list ("three methods did not beat the seasonal normal", including NOAA at 183 d) counted NOAA twice; pre-edit copy in paper/draft/_snapshots/20261027T142214Z_postwf_fixes/PAPER_FACTS_before_AM2.md.
    - **Never deciding, and they beat us:** same-month MERRA-2 (A) and the 365 d CRW Outlook (A, group-mean contrast only); the NDVI H1 extension at 6 months (X). See RH6.
  - **Why the 9 rows are not 9 comparisons.** Six are VT0007 (3 forms x 2 leads). All six failed their guards and share one guarded answer, the arithmetic normal 0.008405, so each is our model against the normal (d 0.002989 [0.001978, 0.004000] at lead 0 for all three forms; 0.002997 [0.001984, 0.004011] at lead 1). Two of the six are the frozen-2019 form, which the table itself labels "not part of the 'ours beats VT0007' claim". The Kraft LSTM row (d 0.007918 [0.006750, 0.009086]) is numerically identical to our own NDVI nowcast verdict (ndvi/verdicts_c4cd7e7c2610.csv: 0.0079179 [0.00675, 0.009086]). The CRW 183 d row compares ours with the donor median on 2,290 rows. So 8 of the 9 rows are comparisons of our model with a seasonal normal.
  - **Sensitivity:** the CRW 183 d BEATS becomes CANNOT TELL with a t31 critical value (P29).
  - Sources: D:/treeroute/rivals_table/20261025T162302876402Z/RIVALS_TABLE.csv (rows 72-103 by file line; guard_passed, rival_mae, verdict text); D:/treeroute/rivals_human/coral/20261020_f3b8e3da/human_board.md; engine/RIVALS_TABLE_FINAL.md; Part 619. Guard records and picks: K69; other Table 7 cells: K70; the lagged MERRA-2 extreme forecast: K71.

Predict-zero already beats our nowcast (0.004284 vs 0.004385) and our declined answers at 6 and 12 months (0.004284 vs 0.00631) (R3, R11).   - Whether the runners decide at lead 6 is open (OD-4.2).

## 8. Losses, negatives and limitations (pre-registered or design facts)

- **N1 (final). PM2.5: Ridge beats us at 6 and 12 months.** See RC9. Ridge's edge was already visible before the test window (inner lead 0: 3.3275 vs 3.3395; guard window lead 6: 3.356 vs 3.470). Source: Part 611.
- **N2 (final). Fire: Tweedie and predict-zero beat us on pooled MAE.** Tweedie's wins are TIMID. See R3 and RC11. Sources: Parts 590, 606.
- **N3 (final). Coral: hgb_l1 beats our nowcast**, and predict-zero beats us on Reef_Check (3.17 vs 3.83). Our hit rate (0.742) is below the donor median's (0.773). **On Reef_Check (1,932 of 2,312 test visits, 84%) the model is also worse than the donor median, our own deciding reference and decline target: 3.83 vs 3.58 at the nowcast and 3.78 vs 3.58 at 183 d (report-only verdicts CANNOT TELL and EQUIVALENT; R9). By year it is worse than the donor median in 2017 and 2019 at both kept leads (D54, R16).** Sources: Parts 597, 600; coral_report.md; coral/per_year_7dde14b4e308.csv.
- **N4 (final). Forecast leads are weak.** 6 of 9 declined. Damped persistence beats the declined answer at PM2.5 lead 12 and NDVI leads 6 and 12 (R6, R7).
- **N5 (final). The guard and the fallback are different objects in the grid kinds**, which hurts fire leads 6 and 12 (M45, R11).
- **N6 (final; the fire pooled MAEs final, post hoc (Part 622)). The corrector shows no demonstrated mechanism:** every adopted corrector is tagged does_not_help_equivalent against its placebo in the run's own pre-registered band-pair contrasts (R13, jackknife.csv). On fire the trees never beat the best normal: the log trees-off beat every log tree arm on test (jackknife.csv) and the adopted arm on pooled MAE (R12; post hoc; per region CANNOT TELL).
- **N7 (final). Edge picks** (R2), together with the test-exposed penalty centres (M24, P23).
- **N8 (final). Coral severe events are flattened 3-13 times** (R10). Most coral trees have no corrector (M15).
- **N9 (final). The router does not see the recent level** (M31). This is the trend limitation shared with the tree rivals.
- **N10 (final). The nowcast uses recent labels** (M10).
- **N11 (final). Test-window exposure of design choices** (P23). NDVI's architecture was chosen on its test window. The coral design was chosen with the test labels visible.
- **N12 (final). PM2.5's hit rate (0.457) does not beat the last-5-years normal (0.458).** Fire's hit rate is near the L5 normal (0.948 vs 0.945). Source: the headline CSVs.
- **N13 (final). Defo corr2 is tiny** (0.017). The tree rivals have slightly higher corr2.
- **N14 (final). The scored-unit budget departs from the old totals:** fire x2, NDVI x0.45, defo x1.31 (ruling E L1).
- **N15 (final). Reproducibility depends on sklearnex/oneDAL** (M14).
- **N16 (final). The lean rival board** omits XGBoost, CatBoost and deep learning. The only deep-learning rival is the Kraft LSTM on NDVI, which reads no NDVI history; label it "information-poorer (no NDVI history)", not as the deep-learning baseline (RC1, RC6a, RH4).
- **N17 (final; corrected 2026-10-26, consolidation pass). Code-archive hygiene.**
  - Stale labels: '0.0.1-skeleton' and the README (M2). 33 stale tests fail.
  - Notes awaiting the owner's confirmation remain in the frozen modules. **Their number depends on the search pattern, so the paper gives no count** ("code annotations awaiting the authors' confirmation"). Verified 2026-10-26 by grep of engine/treeroute/**/*.py (LIVE 930d961c):
    - Case-sensitive `OWNER CONFIRMS|READING \(owner confirm`: **14 lines in 10 files**: engine_constants.py:196 and :306; fit/bank.py:62; fit/correctors.py:31; fit/tables.py:99; report/metrics.py:111, :114 and :117; route/solve.py:44; route/region.py:22; derive/orchestrate.py:636 and :640; io/apply.py:536; io/lastvalue.py:36.
    - Case-insensitive `owner (confirm|to confirm)`, leaving out the 17 lines that read "owner confirmed" (engine_constants.py, resolved by ruling N): **21 lines in 13 files**: the 14 above plus check/guards.py:1160 and :1191 (an error message repeating 1160), check/recompute.py:32, derive/orchestrate.py:701, pipeline/ladder.py:183 and :186 (two "(owner confirms, or ...)" message strings) and engine_constants.py:15 (the docstring that defines the status "extra", not an annotation).
    - engine_constants.py:306 ("OWNER CONFIRMS each") heads constants whose lines 308-334 carry "owner confirmed: ruling N", so that note may already be resolved (Q8).
  - Correction: the earlier list of five locations (engine_constants.py:196; route/region.py:22; io/apply.py:536; io/lastvalue.py:36; derive/orchestrate.py:636-640) was incomplete (draft/review_logs/limits_review_log.md, item 1 and blocker B1).
  - Source: grep of engine/treeroute with the patterns above.
- **N18 (final). "N2" is a name collision.** The selection guard and ruling N's static-map rule are both called "N2". Sources: layer/decide.py; OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md N.
- **N19 (final). "Rarely does worse than climatology" is not supported.** At all 6 declined leads a naive baseline in our own tables beat the decline answer (R17). The kept model is worse than its reference in some test years (coral 2017 and 2019; PM2.5 lead 6 in 2022 and 2023) and in about a third of fire and coral regions (13 of 40, 14 of 37; R16). What holds: pooled over the test window, the guarded answer was never worse than its own deciding reference (R17).
- **N20 (final). Fire's nowcast "win" is relative to the arcsine normal only.** The geometric normal (log trees-off) and predict-zero both beat the model (R12, R3b). The HELPS verdict does not survive a t critical value or the blocked jackknife (R5, P29).
- **N21 (final). No ablation isolates the novel parts.** The per-unit axis was never compared with a uniform average or a global ridge over the same masked trees (M56). The saved rows show the corrector adds nothing beyond its placebo (R13), GLS hurt coral by 0.55 MAE on test (R15, A7), the link decided fire (R12), and all five adoptions are edge picks (R2). The ablation that would isolate the per-unit axis is built and timed but not run (Q10, Part 626).
- **N22 (final). The per-unit router with the exact climatology identity runs on 3 of 5 problems** (PM2.5, NDVI, fire); defo uses a region router and coral a neighbour router with no normal column (M59).
- **N23 (final). The rival comparison is asymmetric in search budget and in local models** (RC6a): 45-424 inner candidates for us vs 4-24 settings per rival learner (K75; corrected 2026-10-26 from "8-24", which held for the three-link problems only); no local per-unit rival outside NDVI; the only deep-learning rival reads no NDVI history.
- **N24 (final). Single forecast origin.** Each run has one fit cut; routers are fitted once and never refitted inside the 4-year test window (M30), so skill can decay with test year (PM2.5 lead 6: better than the reference in 2020-2021, worse in 2022-2023; R16; Ridge's edge grows with test year, A4). Rolling-origin evaluation was not done.
- **N25 (final; the P29 numbers final, post hoc (Part 622)). Statistical fragility.** z = 1.96 with 32-42 groups on fire, defo and coral; the band is set from the same run's test contrasts and ranges from 0.31% (fire) to 11.1% (coral) of the reference MAE; no multiplicity correction over 79 deciding verdicts, of which 2 change tag under a t(G-1) interval (P29); the verdict estimand (equal-weight group mean) differs from the headline pooled MAE (P16).
- **N26 (final). Reproducibility limits.** No seed is varied while some margins are tiny (fire 4.2e-05) and selection reversed on test (coral GLS vs unweighted, 0.55 MAE; A7, R15). Bit-equality depends on oneDAL and 4 pinned threads (M5, M14). There is no version control (P28). The engine runs Python 3.9.13 until submission (APP_PLAN.md A13, line 886). 33 tests fail as stale and the manifests say '0.0.1-skeleton' (M2). Input licences are listed in Supplement S9: several provider pages state none, and the sources or versions of some static tree inputs are not recorded (L6-L8). The inputs (a 44 GB panel from 45 product families, D1; C19) cannot all be rebuilt from upstream because some sources revise their history (Hansen re-derives loss years each release, D40).
- **N27 (final). The nowcast is a retrospective one-step-ahead test.** It uses same-month drivers and labels to t-1 (t-2 on NDVI and fire) without modelling the operational latency of drivers or labels (M8, M10), and the targets are retrospective products (D10: ACAG V5.GL.06 is a model product). The method gives point forecasts only; conformal intervals belong to the predecessor (X1).
- **N28 (final). PM2.5 skill is measured against the ACAG gridded surfaces, not against monitors** (D10, D11).
- **N29 (pending). Region selection is undocumented** (D5).

## 9. Post-hoc analyses (label every one "post hoc"; never the headline)

- **A1 (post hoc, outcome-conditional, owner MUST-INCLUDE; final, post hoc (Part 622)). Fire active cells** (y > 0; 1,759 cell-months, 33 regions). Nowcast, 94,702 labelled cell-months, 40 regions; d = MAE(Tweedie) - MAE(ours) per region, positive = ours better, z = 1.96, fire band 1.36e-05.
  - **Active cells (final, Part 606 addendum; confirmed by Part 622):** ours 0.191 vs Tweedie 0.201, d +0.00472 [+0.00237, +0.00706], HELPS; ours is better in 25 of 33 regions.
  - **Top 1% (final, post hoc (Part 622)):** y >= q99 = 0.102941, the 99th percentile of the TEST labels (949 cell-months, 31 regions): ours 0.308 vs Tweedie 0.335, d +0.0116 [+0.0057, +0.0176], HELPS (ours better in 24 of 31). Mean label 0.391; mean prediction ours 0.098, Tweedie 0.062.
  - **Active cells below the top 1% (final, post hoc (Part 622)):** 0 < y < q99 (810 cell-months, 29 regions): ours 0.0539 vs Tweedie 0.0434, d -0.00238 [-0.00541, +0.00066], CANNOT TELL.
  - **The complement must be shown beside it (final, post hoc (Part 622))**, because conditioning on y > 0 favours the less shrunk model by construction: on the zero rows (92,943, 98.14%) ours predicts a mean of 0.00085 and Tweedie 0.00032, d -0.000549 [-0.000867, -0.000232], HURTS (ours better in 4 of 40 regions). This is why Tweedie wins pooled MAE (RC11). Show A1 in one stratified table with the zero rows, never alone.
  - **Ridge's guarded column** (its guard failed at the nowcast) is the identity trees-off, the decline target, not the guess-normal exactly; the two differ by up to 8.09e-07 per row (M34).
  - Sources: Part 622; D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/POSTHOC_REPORT.md (A1); Part 606 addendum; fire test_preds.parquet and the rivals' preds_0.parquet. The arithmetic-normal column: K78.
- **A2 (post hoc, outcome-conditional; final, post hoc (Part 622)). Coral bleached visits** (y > 0; 1,024 of 2,312 visits, 36 of 37 regions; all five rivals passed their nowcast guard). Ours 13.37 is mid-pack: the absolute-error booster 11.69, LightGBM 12.70 and RF 12.87 are lower; Tweedie 13.62 and Ridge 14.48 are higher. All five per-region contrasts are CANNOT TELL (vs the absolute-error booster d -1.90 [-4.76, +0.97]). Ours beats the donor median (17.47) and predict-zero (18.29), both HELPS. The fire result does not carry over. Sources: Part 622; D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/POSTHOC_REPORT.md (A2). Detail: K80.
- **A3 (final, post hoc). The Ridge audit on PM2.5** found the win leak-free and fair. Removing Ridge's extra advantages moves its MAE by at most 0.026, and no verdict changes. At lead 0 the gap rests on 3 Chinese groups (without them, d -0.042 [-0.128, +0.043]). Source: Part 611.
- **A4 (final, post hoc). The Ridge mechanism**: a "recent level minus long-run level" contrast. Ridge's advantage grows with test year and with the pre-2020 slope (Spearman about -0.3). Refitting without the history columns removes it. Source: Part 611; PAPER_NOTES_RIVALS.md. Values by test year: K77.
- **A5 (final, post hoc). PM2.5 trend strata** (cut points +/-1 and +/-3 %/yr chosen by an agent, no multiplicity correction; d = Ridge MAE - ours, > 0 = ours better). Quote every stratum or the terciles, never a selection.
  - Nowcast: falling > 3%/yr (298 units / 48 regions) **-0.50 [-0.96, -0.04] (Ridge significantly better)**; flat within 1%/yr +0.008 [-0.09, +0.11] (tie); rising 1-3% +0.057 (n.s.); rising > 3% +0.22 [+0.09, +0.35] (ours better). Terciles -0.54 / +0.006 / +0.051.
  - 6 months: falling > 3% -0.55 (sig.); falling 1-3% -0.27 (sig.); flat -0.10 [-0.165, -0.040] (Ridge better); rising -0.06 and -0.004 (n.s.). Terciles -0.57 / -0.09 / -0.08.
  - 12 months, our answer (the normal): Ridge better everywhere except rising places (ties). Our rejected model: falling > 3% -0.47 (sig.); flat -0.075 [-0.135, -0.015]; rising +0.004 / +0.043 (ties). Terciles -0.46 / -0.09 / -0.01.
  - Source: Part 612.
- **A6 (final, post hoc, outcome-conditional, owner MUST-INCLUDE, with its REQUIRED qualifier). PM2.5 spike months** (above the location's 2003-2019 p90; 7,966 cell-months).
  - Ours beats Ridge at every lead: nowcast 9.15 vs 10.39, d +1.35 [+0.99, +1.71].
  - Qualifier: the normal also beats Ridge on spikes (9.42 vs 10.39). Our model only ties the normal at the nowcast (+0.03 [-0.18, +0.25]) and loses to it at 6 and 12 months (-0.53 [-0.75, -0.32]).
  - Complement (must sit in the same table): **Ridge wins the ordinary (non-spike) months at every lead** (Part 612 addendum).
  - Source: Part 612 addendum. Every lead and the ordinary-month complement: K79.
- **A7 (final, post hoc). Coral GLS diagnosis.** The layer picked GLS (6.70) over its unweighted twin (6.15 on test) by a surface margin of 0.077 MAE (GLS won in 98% of resamples). The reversal on test is the 2016 Indo-Pacific mass bleaching. Source: Part 601.
- **A8 (final, post hoc). Label-free tool guidance.** Adversarial validation gave no robust gain (Part 602). Of 7 label-free methods only the disagreement alarm, in its two forms (M1 switch to the simpler twin, M2 even blend), passed, and that test is circular on coral (Parts 603-604).
  - Caveats (added 2026-10-27, S5 revision; Parts 603-604; D:/treeroute/analysis/guidance/RESULT.txt and its skeptic addendum; PROTOCOL.txt lines 18-20; coral/results_coral.json): M1 coral -0.600 bands, M2 -0.331. The success rule (PROTOCOL c51fc39a; no harm > 0.25 band elsewhere) was hashed before the seven-method run, but after Part 603 had scored the switch and the always-blend, with the alarm ratios, on pm25, ndvi, fire and coral (defo had no weighting twin), so M1's and M2's results on those four were known. M1's coral gain is McClanahan 2016 (130% of the net gain); M1 is worse in 2017, 2018 and 2019, on Reef_Check (1,932 visits, 3.829 -> 4.023) and on FRRP (190 visits, 7.660 -> 7.736); the single AGRRA visit improved. Neither form fired on the other four problems. In the one measurable wrong-fire case (fire), M1 would cost +3.54 bands and M2 +1.73. The second safeguard (the twin must pass the skill guard on the surface) was proposed by the Part 604 skeptic after that check and adopted in AE1.
- **A9 (final, post hoc (Part 628)). The SHAP comparison's timeline** (local time, as the rulings file, the SHAP protocol and its sample register record it). Ruling AW, 2026-11-12 (AW1-AW5): a SHAP analysis of treeroute v1's headline runs of all five problems and of every rival it can be run on, a table of how the models disagree, several agreement statistics (Spearman's rank correlation among them) with a bootstrap over regions, and a fixed sample of 1,000 test rows per problem and lead in scope; it amends outline decision O3 as confirmed by AT17 (e), which had left SHAP out of v1 and offered a labelled post hoc analysis if a reviewer asked. Ruling AY, 2026-11-13: AY1 scope (i), the nowcast of each problem; AY2 the recorded path map (gate E3m; protocol decision D14 (b)); AY3 a true relative tolerance of 1e-12 on arcsine arms only (decision D8), with which the coral pilot had already run. Ruling AZ1, 2026-11-14 (answered at about 04:35, before the ridge pieces of 04:46-04:48): the author had not opened the burned-fraction comparison of 2026-11-13 21:18 (four models, without Ridge) before Ridge was added (the author's answer); it had been opened once, by an agent, for its layout only (A26 (a)). Ruling BA, 2026-11-15 (answered at about 19:45 for BA1 and BA2 and at about 22:50 for BA3), when no statistic of the PM2.5, NDVI, burned-fraction or forest-loss comparisons had been read. Ruling BB, 2026-11-16 (answered at 20:59), after the independent re-computation. Protocol: version 1 (2026-11-12) was written before any SHAP value was computed on a real data row; the v2 and v3 smoke tests computed SHAP values on 40-250 rows of the real coral bleaching headline run (2 regions) and on synthetic engine worlds, as plumbing, with no statistic read as a result; version 3 (2026-11-12, before any registration) changed sections 3 and 8 after those smokes, because two design reviewers had found that the code and the text disagreed and that several statistics were biased, and every change answers a method finding, not a value (preamble; section 14, v3 entry); sections 3 (sample) and 8 (statistics) were frozen when the first sample was registered; the later checks of the ridge and BARDL loaders computed SHAP values on rows of registered samples (ridge: the registered burned-fraction and coral samples; BARDL: 29 rows of the NDVI smoke sample, which equals the NDVI sample registered on 2026-11-14), as plumbing, and no agreement statistic was read from them (the BARDL smoke's family-share check value was not read). Sample registrations (SAMPLE_REGISTER.txt): coral 2026-11-12T23:49:45, fire 2026-11-13T17:23:16, PM2.5 2026-11-13T21:44:18, defo 2026-11-14T04:37:20, NDVI 2026-11-14T14:37:52. Runs: the coral pilot 2026-11-12 23:49 to 2026-11-13 00:06; the last nowcast comparison (NDVI, five models) 2026-11-15 19:11:39; the B1 command 2026-11-15 23:06:16 to 2026-11-16 08:14:14; the independent re-computation (round 14) 2026-11-16 17:09 to about 19:20. So the runs went from 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-15, the B1 runs from 2026-11-15 to 2026-11-16 and the re-computation ran on 2026-11-16 (S3.0's index: 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-16). Sources: engine/OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md sections AW, AY, AZ, BA and BB; D:/treeroute/analysis/shap_v1/PROTOCOL.md (SHA-256 32daa77b), preamble and sections 1, 3 and 14 (entries of 2026-11-14, of 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-15, and of 2026-11-16); D:/treeroute/analysis/shap_v1/SAMPLE_REGISTER.txt lines 1-5 (time stamps).
- **A10 (final, post hoc (Part 628)). The SHAP comparison's fixed settings.** N = 1,000 explained test rows per problem, the same rows for every model (ruling AW5; per_lead.json rows = 1000 and SAMPLE_REGISTER.txt n=1000 on all five problems). B = 2,000 paired bootstrap draws of regions with replacement, the same draws for every model, from the SHA-256 generator with the salt shap_v1|AW4|<problem>|0 (per_lead.json draws = 2000 and salt on all five problems); percentile 95% intervals, each with its number of finite draws; no BCa and no p-value; with 37-133 regions the intervals may under-cover (protocol section 8). J, per level: the players to which some model gives more than 1e-12 of its total mean |phi| (per_lead.json j_rule "player active when some model gives it > 1e-12 of its total"). Pair statistics, at the feature and the variable level over J: Spearman over J and over the union of the two top-20 sets; Kendall's tau-b; Jaccard of the top-5 and top-10 sets, and of the top-20 sets when J has 200 or more players; RBO at p = 0.9 and 0.95; the total-variation distances between the shares and between the family shares; the sign agreement on the union of the two top-10 sets. Main set: Kendall's W with the tie correction and the mean pairwise Spearman; each model's stability under region resampling; the main-text row with treeroute v1's and the consensus's top 3. Two levels (feature, variable) and five families (own history, global indices, local drivers, context and calendar, the seasonal normal). Stop rules: S-ADD |base + sum phi + R - eta| <= 1e-9 at both levels; S-EVAL within 1e-9 (forest loss's region router: within the float32 summation bound); S-ZERO: a player whose columns equal the explained row's on every background row gets exactly 0, in every model, at both levels (so under B2 the columns that take the explained row's value on every background row, such as static columns, latitude, the month and the seasonal normal on the gridded problems, are exactly 0); D8 (ruling AY3): a relative difference of at most 1e-12 on arcsine arms only. Link space only (decision D4). Sources: D:/treeroute/analysis/shap_v1/PROTOCOL.md sections 3, 5, 8 and 12; D:/treeroute/analysis/shap_v1/out/<problem>/lead_0/compare/per_lead.json keys rows, draws, salt and j_rule (read 2026-11-17); rulings AW5 and AY3.
- **A11 (final, post hoc (Part 628)). Coral bleaching's B2 background.** Each sample row's background is its survey visit's saved router fit rows, the neighbour router's pool of earlier nearby survey visits (Section 2.4: the 50 visits most similar in tree inputs among the 150 nearest earlier training visits; M28, K19): 50 per sample row, 50,000 background pairs in all (Table S3.19). Of the 50,000 pairs, 980 pair the explained visit with an earlier visit at its own reef site, in 415 of the 1,000 sample rows, and in none is the background visit dated on or after the explained visit (line 33: "background pairs: 50000; same reef site: 980 in 415 sample rows; dated on/after the explained visit: 0"). The protocol's wording "other sites' visits" (PROTOCOL.md section 5, lines 375 and 381-382, and section 10, "Not claimed") is therefore inexact; the paper says "a pool of earlier nearby survey visits". Sources: paper/supp_build/s3_13/_brief/lead_checks/verify_v5_claims_out.txt line 33 (from paper/supp_build/s3_13/_brief/lead_checks/verify_v5_claims.py, a read-only count over D:/treeroute/analysis/shap_v1/out/coral/lead_0/treeroute/background_keys.parquet against rows.parquet; the reef site is a survey visit key's text before "|"); background pairs D:/treeroute/analysis/shap_v1/out/coral/lead_0/treeroute/meta.json pairs = 50000 and background rows D:/treeroute/analysis/shap_v1/sample/coral/sample_manifest.json background_rows["0"].min = 50 (paper/supp_build/s3_13/reconcile/cells.json, Table S3.19, line 79 of paper/supp_build/s3_13/reconcile/tables_agreed.md).
- **A12 (final, post hoc (Part 628)). Exactness checks and rebuild gates.** treeroute v1's fitted trees were not kept (the engine deletes its checkpoint folders when a run finishes), so each problem's tree bank, with its correctors and placebos, and the sampled locations' routers were rebuilt with the run's own engine snapshot (gated by its code hash, E1), which read the run's adoption (arm and penalty point) and guard outcome (never recomputed) and opened no test label; the random forests were rebuilt with the boards' own rf_forest. S-EXACT (Table S3.20): every rival bit-identical on every checked row (every test row of its saved predictions; rival_shap.py checks the pre-clip column only); treeroute v1 bit-identical on PM2.5 (12,192 of 12,192 rows), NDVI (12,768 of 12,768) and forest loss (1,008 of 1,008); its arcsine arms on burned fraction 13,056 of 13,440 rows bit-identical and the rest within D8 (largest relative difference 2.4 × 10⁻¹³, stored 2.3560131176637307e-13), and on coral bleaching 2,228 of 2,312 (1.1 × 10⁻¹⁵, stored 1.055062494413031e-15), licensed by gate E5a (13,440 of 13,440 and 2,312 of 2,312 rows bit-identical). S-ADD, S-EVAL, S-RESID and S-ZERO passed for every model, with 0 S-ZERO violations (all 25 models). E5 (bank fingerprint, SHA-256 over every node array of every tree, corrector and placebo): pass false on all five problems, informational ("cause not established (answers decide: E5a, E8, E10)"). E3a on coral bleaching: static_files_sha256 4f53cda18c2baa0c on both sides, the start of the SHA-256 of an empty list ("[]"), and target_files_sha256 listed under not_before_read. E8 on coral bleaching: rows_checked 2,312, every test survey visit, not only the 1,000 sampled. E10: as Table S3.21 states it. The registered samples.** 1,000 test rows per problem, drawn from the frame (the labelled test rows that every compared model covers) by the SHA-256 generator shap_v1|AW5|<problem>|<what>|<key>, stratified by region x test year. PM2.5: frame 91,428 rows, 127 regions, 254 locations (2 per region). NDVI: frame 94,527 rows, 133 regions, 266 locations (2 per region). Burned fraction: frame 94,702 rows, 40 regions, 280 locations (7 per region). Forest loss: frame 369,096 rows, 42 regions, 252 locations (6 per region; all 42 regions, decision D10's default). Coral bleaching: frame 2,312 rows, 37 regions, 1,000 survey visits (the engine's unit there). Regions per problem: 37 to 133. On the gridded problems ceil(N / (G x Y)) locations per region, with Y = 4 test years, one row per location and test year, trimmed to exactly N by dropping rows in reverse hash order; on coral bleaching an equal allocation per region, capped by availability, with the rows left over given in turn to the regions that still have rows, the test years in turn. Each sample and its background files are registered with their SHA-256 in SAMPLE_REGISTER.txt, and every SHAP script refuses a sample or background file whose hash is not the registered one. ## 10. The engine-v2 twin-disagreement alarm (designed AFTER v1; ruling AE3)

- **V1 (final). The rule.**
  - Per issue batch of at least 30 rows, it compares mean |adopted - twin| on test inputs with the same gap on the decision surface.
  - If the ratio is above 2.0, the twin is simpler (uniform where the adopted arm is error-weighted, or plain where it is corrected; SPEC_ALARM.txt 2.2), and the twin passes the skill guard at the adopted point (AF2), the batch uses the twin. At a lead the reference gap and the twin's guard use the lead's guard rows (SPEC D2 default); every saved test row counts toward the 30 (SPEC D4 default); a batch is the issue step when the step is a month or longer, else the calendar month in which it begins (AE1 amendment; SPEC 2.5). (Detail added 2026-10-27, S5 revision.)
  - Decisions are locked before labels open, and results are reported with and without the alarm (AE2).
  - Sources: rulings AE1-AE2, AF1-AF2; D:/treeroute/engine_v2/ALARM_REPORT.txt section 6.
- **V2 (final). Status.**
  - It was designed after v1, from the coral diagnosis. The paper must say so (AE3), and the owner's request to present it otherwise was declined.
  - v2 hash 88f4db2c. It is NOT installed (LIVE is 930d961c) and may be installed only after every rival board finishes (AE4, AF3).
  - It has never run on new data.
  - Earlier v2 builds: fc051e5a (before the skeptic fixes) and 663ab8ef (Part 605). AF1-AF2 were ruled after the first replay had been scored; AF1 kept the built behaviour and AF2 changed it; the replay under 88f4db2c matched bit for bit (Part 605 addendum; D:/treeroute/engine_v2/ALARM_REPORT.txt ATTENTION 3, sections 1 and 6, lines 77-78, 197-198, 234). Added 2026-10-27 (S4 and S5 revisions).
  - Sources: rulings AE3, AE4, AF3; Parts 605, 606 addendum.
- **V3 (final, post hoc (Part 625); re-statused 2026-10-28 under ruling AN2). Coral nowcast 6.698 -> 6.120.**
  - 11 of 33 tested month batches switch (737 of 2,312 survey visits).
  - By year: 2016 12.41 -> 10.68; 2017 3.57 -> 3.69 (worse); 2018 unchanged; 2019 4.81 -> 5.04 (worse).
  - Nothing switched at the other four nowcasts, for two different reasons: at PM2.5 and NDVI (twin not simpler) and at burned fraction (twin fails its skill guard) the rule cannot act; at forest loss it can act, but no tested batch's ratio exceeded 2.0 (largest 1.166). Defo lead 1 and coral 183 d cannot be replayed (twin not saved). (Wording corrected 2026-10-28 from "silent at the other four nowcasts", which blurred the two cases; v2_replay/SPEC.md O5; the owner accepted this "cannot act" vs "could act, nothing switched" wording in ruling AO2, 2026-10-28.)
  - This replay is on the same coral test data the alarm was designed from, so it is circular (A8; Parts 603-604); coral's 2016-2019 window was set by hand. Keep the number out of the main text (supplement S5 only). The alarm has never run on new data.
  - Re-computed twice by independent implementations that agree bit for bit (Part 625). Full-precision values, the survey-programme split, the subsets with their complements, the small batches and the information-only checks: K90.
  - Sources: Part 625 (D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/v2_replay/); ALARM_REPORT.txt; Part 605 and its addendum.

## 11. Predecessor material (NOT v1 results; do not cite as results)

- **X1 (final). Predecessor documents.** ARCHITECTURE_EXPLAINED.md, PAPER_REFERENCE.md, MASTER_RESEARCH_RECORD.md and FINDINGS.md describe predecessor pipelines: CatBoost bundles, recipe dictionaries with a mother router, kNN routers, hurdle/odds/cascade/EVT heads, conformal intervals and 44-region hard splits. Numbers such as bundle R2 0.921 are not v1 results. Sources: those files; CLAIMS_LEDGER.md X1, X15.
- **X2 (final). The feature-gating contrast** (-0.41 vs 0.92 pooled R2) has no Part, uses pooled R2 (about 77% climatology) and mixes two code bases. If used at all, cite Part 109 (0.46 < 0.61 < 0.79) and scope it to this panel. Coral's v1 router itself selects by feature similarity. Source: CLAIMS_LEDGER.md X2.
- **X3 (final). No SHAP analysis exists for v1.** The SHAP notes in memory (reference_shap_driver_causality) concern the predecessor and found that albedo, LST and GPP were vegetation-state proxies. Those columns are now banned on NDVI (D33).
- **X4 (final; S2 recount, added 2026-10-27). Earlier REMOVE lists.** The predecessor pipelines carried three versions of the proxy-ban list: 9 columns in 25 files; 11 defined in 2 files and imported by 10 (bundle_lag_compare.py among them); 13 in 2 files. None is a v1 list. Source: presplit_cc/**/*.py (29 defining files; the S2 recount).

## 12. Conflicts between sources

- **C1. Fire test years** (authoritative: the run). engine/HEADLINE_MODEL_TABLE.md:3 says "test years 2020-2023". Fire's derived.json R7, its pre-registration section 4 and _headline_tables/fire/per_year_d657a6a40d37.csv all say **2019-2022**. Fix the header before citing.
- **C2. Defo bank and scored counts** (authoritative: the run). engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md lines 64-65 and 113-115 say 4,285 bank / 2,040 scored per group (85,680). The run's derived.json R18 and the filled defo pre-registration (registry/freeze/PREREGISTRATION_20261017T105545Z.md lines 86, 135-136) say **4,615 / 2,197 (92,274)**, after Y0 moved to 2004 (N S17 a). One fact list carried the stale 4,285.
- **C3. The lead-decline count** (authoritative: the run logs). One agent wrote "7 of 10 forecast leads". There are 9 non-zero leads: **6 declined and 3 kept** (R6).
- **C4. Human WINs over guard-failed rivals** (authoritative: the table). Part 619 says 7 of 9, then lists 8. RIVALS_TABLE.csv shows guard_passed False for **8 of 9**; only NDVI BARDL H1 passed.
- **C5. Coral human rows** (authoritative: the board). Part 619 and PAPER_NOTES_RIVALS.md / RIVALS_TABLE_FINAL.md say "2,312 NOAA site-days, a subset". human_board.md shows crw_dhw covers **2,312 of 2,312** rows. The deciding contrast uses **2,290 rows in 32 of 37 ecoregions** (groups with at least 10 rows). "Ours 6.471" is our MAE on those rows.
- **C6. The v2 alarm's coral number** (authoritative: Part 605). 6.147 is the Part 603 whole-window twin. 6.15 is AE3's first text and the stale memory note. **6.120** is the frozen per-batch rule (Part 605; AE3 corrected).
- **C7. Defo zero share** (both valid; name the population). 62.1% is the test cell-years (defo test_preds). 45.9% is all 4 km cell-years before the build (DEFORESTATION_SCOPE.md section 5).
- **C8. Why fire's 2023 was not fetched.** fire_fetch_target.py:15-16, 70 says it was because SPEI and AMO vanish in 2023. HEADLINE_QUESTIONS_AND_FIXES.md:445 calls that reason wrong for the window choice: fire's data simply ends in 2022. The current state (fire test 2019-2022, chosen knowing earlier results) is authoritative; disclose it.
- **C9. PM2.5 lead-12 model MAE** (resolved). The old table had 3.841 (-5.9%). Since the Part 621 addendum it is **3.840 (-6.0%)**. PAPER_NOTES_RIVALS.md still mentions the old value as "to fix".
- **C10. The shrinkage description.** ARCHITECTURE_EXPLAINED.md section 4 says it is "toward the average". v1 shrinks **toward the unit's climatology** (M23).
- **C11. Coral persistence vs ours.** The headline CSV prints coral persistence 4.53 / 5.48 in the same row as our 7.71. They are on different row sets (1,419 vs 2,312 rows; R8) and are not comparable.
- **C12. The D2 download time** (no source is a measurement). HUMAN_OWNER_COMMANDS_partB.txt sections 3 and 10: "D2 5-16 h (x)". Ruling AJ1: "about 18 h". The first sheet's "about 7 h" extrapolated a SEAS5 probe request at constant time per request; scaled by size it gives about 13 h. Quote "about 5-18 h, not measured" (S3).
- **C13. Source of the Crammer and Mansour citations.** The first sheet attributed them to TREE_ROUTE_ALGORITHM_AND_PRIOR_ART.md; they are only in CLAIMS_LEDGER.md line 771 (X14 skeptic note, Part 403) (M55).
- **C14. MERRA-2 and the ACAG target.** The first sheet said "MERRA-2 is upstream of the ACAG target (D11)". The sources support only MERRA-2 meteorology driving GEOS-Chem and shared same-month MODIS AOD (RH8).
- **C15. Fire lead 6/12 corr2 of "ours".** The RIVALS_TABLE "ours (headline)" row prints 0.2147 / 0.2136, which is the declined answer (the identity normal). The pinned model's corr2 is 0.2144 / 0.2140 (board_lead_6/12.csv ours_corr2). Part 606's "corr2 0.214" refers to the pinned model. Both round to 0.214; name which one is meant.
- **C16. Coral by magnitude** (authoritative: Part 622). engine/PAPER_NOTES_RIVALS.md (computed 2026-10-21) says the absolute-error booster "wins on every bleached stratum". Part 622's two scripts find ours lower in the three bleached quartile bins below q75, and the booster lower only above q75 and at y >= 50 (RC12). The notes line needs fixing before it is quoted.
- **C17. Coral trees without a corrector** (authoritative: the run log). Part 597 (and M15 before 2026-10-28) gave 5,915-6,384 of the 8,300 trees per bank. The coral run.log reports six banks, and the 5,915-6,384 range left out the nowcast's fit-cut bank (5,851; line 27). The range is **5,851-6,384** (M15; Part 597 addendum, 2026-10-28).
- **C18. Short-history fallback: candidate or scored locations** (resolved 2026-10-28: the owner confirmed the reading, ruling AO3; two read-only checks of the run records confirm Supplement S7.10). Before 2026-10-28, M29, Section 2.4, S7.4, S1 RL12, DE5 and DE6, S8.2 (iv) and the Figure 3 caption described the 75 NDVI and 317 burned-fraction locations as routed by their region's router. In the runs' derived.json, R12 counts them over every candidate unit of every group (units_scope "every candidate unit of every group"): 75 of 2,289,116 (NDVI), 317 of 925,710 (burned fraction), 0 of 2,028,667 (PM2.5). R20:fallback_rows, under the rule "a scored unit falls back at any router cut (every lead's fit and guard cut) with fewer fit rows than the minimum", gives groups_with_fallback 0, short_at_cuts {} and extra_rows 0 in all three runs. So none of the 75 or 317 is scored, and the fallback routed no scored row (S7.10, 'Short-history fallback in the runs'). Re-read read-only 2026-10-28 (fixes1 fix pass). Second read-only check the same day (C18 record fix; script first run from a session scratchpad folder on the old machine, as c18/c18_check.py; the script and its output are now kept in D:/treeroute/analysis/paper_posthoc/c18/, c18_check.py sha256 18bdaffe and c18_check_output.txt sha256 44f6886d, the same hashes as the scratchpad files, ruling AO3): R12's window ends at the lead-0 guard cut (NDVI step 24204, fire 24192), after the earliest router cut R20 checks (24193, 24181), and a count at an earlier cut is never larger, so a scored unit among the 75 or 317 would have been listed; at run time decision_checks.json has fallback null, every fallback_levels entry (decision surface and each lead's guard surface) has fallback_units_scored 0, used_centre true and surface_mae_by_level null (the no-short-unit path of pipeline/router.py), no test_pass_router.json, ladder/contract_lead_0.json or lead.json guard or test record holds a fallback or all_short entry, and test_preds.parquet has no per-row router column. The passages above now state this reading (C18 record fix, 2026-10-28); M29 keeps its counts as candidate units only. The owner confirmed this reading on 2026-10-28 (ruling AO3: "Confirmed: the short-history fallback is in the design but routed no scored row in any run. C18 and M29 are resolved"), and S7.10's [OWNER] marker was removed (paper/draft/review_logs/AO_edit_log.md). (Tier 1 length plan (2026-11-12, ruling AU7), dated note: the Figure 3 caption no longer mentions the fallback, as its sentence moved to Supplement S7.4 (A1-28); Section 2.4 keeps one sentence, that the fallback routed no scored row, and its rule, with the threshold of 24 fit rows, moved to S7.4 (A1-21).)
- **C19. Product families of the monthly panel** (authoritative: the store catalogue). D1 (and N26, S1 RP6) said 48 product families. None of D1's named sources gives 48: engine/datasets/pm25/dataset.yaml and PAPER_REFERENCE.md section 2.1 give no family count, and the product_family field of engine/datasets/_stores/monthly_panel_v170_schema_catalog.yaml puts the 166 non-key columns in **45** families, three of them not third-party products (grid: lat and lon; derived: 6 columns computed from TerraClimate; regime classifier: 2). Recounted read-only 2026-10-28 (fixes1 fix pass); the S9 drafting script gave the same 45. D1, N26 and S1 RP6 now say 45.

Crammer et al. 2008 supports the sentence of Section 1.2 that multi-source learning learns from the data of several sources, and its entry matches the JMLR page. Mansour et al. 2009 supports the same sentence, on domain adaptation combining the models of several sources; the NeurIPS page lists it as NIPS 2008, while the entry keeps the print year 2009, as its note says, and its pages 1041-1048 could not be checked from the open pages. The Super Learner (van der Laan et al. 2007) supports its citations in part: its abstract supports Table 2's cells for it (candidate learners, one set of weights chosen by V-fold cross-validation), and that it extends stacking (Section 1.2) is a fair reading, although the abstract does not name stacking; its full text, behind a login, was not reached. Krishnamurti et al. 2000 supports its citation in part: its abstract supports the multimodel superensemble as precedent (Section 1.2 and Table 2's row label), while the per-grid-point, climatology-plus-anomaly form rests on Krishnamurti et al. 2003, as the text says; the AMS page was blocked (403), so its full text was not reached. Wolpert 1992 supports the sentence of Section 1.2 that stacking fits its combination weights on predictions for held-out folds (with Breiman 1996b); its abstract was read (no reading of its full text is recorded), and its entry matches its DOI. The Super Learner's entry matches its DOI, and that of Krishnamurti et al. 2000 its Crossref and Semantic Scholar records. ## 14. Licences and data availability (facts found in the project)

- **L1 (final).** GCBD coral labels: CC0 (CORAL_LABEL_AUDIT.md:4; figshare article 17076290).
- **L2 (final).** CoralTemp's 1985-2002 segment carries an OSTIA clause: "pure academic research only, with no commercial or other application", "for a maximum period of 5 years". It does not block a paper, but it constrains redistribution of derived products (CORAL_DATA_PLAN.md:185).
  - **Ruling AV (2026-11-13), dated note: the CoralTemp product the project read** (for reference NOAACRW2018_DHW under owner ruling AV12, option (a); records read read-only on 2026-11-13). (1) Product: NOAA Coral Reef Watch's CoralTemp, the daily global 5 km sea-surface temperature, version 3.1 (variable analysed_sst on a 0.05° grid), read from the ERDDAP data set CRW_sst_v3_1 of NOAA PIFSC OceanWatch at https://oceanwatch.pifsc.noaa.gov/erddap/griddap/CRW_sst_v3_1 (coral_fetch_sst_full.py line 52; coral_fetch_sst.py line 42; the rival tool's product registry D:/treeroute/human_rivals_build/human/registry/_products.yaml, coral_sst_npz: 'built from NOAA CRW CoralTemp v3.1 (coral_fetch_sst.py, PIFSC OceanWatch ERDDAP)'). The data set's information page (https://oceanwatch.pifsc.noaa.gov/erddap/info/CRW_sst_v3_1/index.html, read 2026-11-13) gives the title 'Sea Surface Temperature, Coral Reef Watch, CoralTemp, v3.1 - Daily, 1985-present', the summary 'NOAA Coral Reef Watch Daily Global 5km Satellite Sea Surface Temperature (CoralTemp)', product_version 3.1, a latitude step of 0.05° and date_created 2018-01-01; CRW's product page (https://coralreefwatch.noaa.gov/product/5km/index_5km_sst.php, read 2026-11-13) heads it 'Daily Global 5km Satellite Sea Surface Temperature (a.k.a. CoralTemp)', 'Version 3.1, released August 1, 2018', spanning January 1, 1985 to the present. (2) Dates read: 1 January 1985 to 31 December 2020. coral_fetch_sst_full.py asks for each 2-degree cluster of reef sites from 1985 (START, line 58) to the end of the cluster's last survey year, in requests of two years (lines 94-101 and 115-116); the 5,139 files in D:/coral/drivers/sst_full span 1985 to 2020 by their names (first year 1985; last year 2020 in 7 files), the time column of a 1985-1986 file starts on 1985-01-01, and those of the seven 2019-2020 files end on 2020-12-31. (3) When: the 5,139 files were written as their requests returned (the downloaded boxes are discarded, docstring lines 32-33), 1,061 on 2026-08-22 (the pilot run; sst_full_fetch.log: 1061 requests) and 4,078 on 2026-08-25 (file dates read 2026-11-13). The coral package reads the compact D:/coral/drivers/sst_compact_all.npz built from them (engine/datasets/coral/catalog.yaml provenance lines; engine/datasets/coral/AUDIT_CHECKLIST.md item 4: 'CoralTemp compact manifest = sst_full (5,139 files)'), whose modification time the coral store's build manifest records as 2026-08-25T22:53:43 (D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_store/20261020_014201/build_manifest.json); the npz itself is no longer in D:/coral/drivers (checked 2026-11-13). (4) Address: as in (1). Besides CoralTemp, the project read two other Coral Reef Watch products that this reference does not cite: the 5 km climatology ct5km_climatology_v3.1.nc (D:/coral/drivers/static, written 2026-08-22, from https://www.star.nesdis.noaa.gov/pub/socd/mecb/crw/data/5km/v3.1_op/climatology/nc/; coral_fetch_drivers.py lines 137-140), whose maximum monthly mean the NOAA degree-heating-week rival uses (_products.yaml crw_climatology; the docstring of D:/treeroute/human_rivals_build/human/adapters/coral_crw.py), and a check sample of NOAA's degree heating weeks, alert areas, HotSpots and SST at 300 site-days (product noaa_crw_dhw_sitedays, created 2026-10-21 from the ERDDAP data sets CRW_dhw_v1_0, CRW_baa_max_7d_v1_0, CRW_hs_v1_0 and CRW_sst_v3_1), against which that rival's adapter checks its own values; the Outlook archive is cited as Liu et al. 2018. The entry's year stays 2018, the year the product page gives for version 3.1's release, so its in-text citations do not change.
  - **Ruling AV (2026-11-13), dated note: the days the coral inputs read** (after the independent checks of the AV apply; store tables read read-only on 2026-11-13). The files read cover 1 January 1985 to 31 December 2020 (item (2) of the note above), and the reference's dates used are those of the files read. The inputs in the coral stores read only days before each issue date (Table S9.4): each row's feature_end is the last day any of its feature windows may read (engine/datasets/coral/AUDIT_CHECKLIST.md item 6). In the coral store that the reported run reads (D:/treeroute/coral_build/coral_store/20261020_014201: lead_0.parquet, lead_183.parquet and lead_365.parquet) the last issue date is 2019-12-31 and the last feature_end 2019-12-29, so the reported run reads days to 2019 only. The store of the declared 2017-2020 sensitivity row (D:/treeroute/analysis/coral_sens_2017_2020/coral_data/full) holds 29 rows with issue dates in 2020 at lead 0, the last on 2020-08-15 with feature_end 2020-08-13, so no row of either store reads a day after 2020-08-13. The latest survey date in the coral visit tables of both builds (visits.parquet) and in L13's events_dated.parquet is also 2020-08-15. Table S9.4's CoralTemp row now cites the reference and says that the files read end on 31 December 2020.

- **L6 (final; provider pages read 2026-10-28; was 'no licence record found in the project').** The 17 sources, as their providers' pages state them (each URL is in paper/draft/S9_inputs_licences.md):
  - CC BY 4.0: ACAG SatPM2.5 V5.GL.06 ("SatPM2.5 data V5.GL.06 are licensed under CC BY 4.0"); Hansen GFC-2025-v1.13 (display credit "Source: Hansen/UMD/Google/USGS/NASA"); ERA5-Land monthly and hourly, and AgERA5 (CDS catalogue records: "CC-BY-4.0"; ERA5 itself is not read, only ERA5-Land and ERA5-based products); WorldPop; UCDP GED 26.1 ("All datasets are free of charge and licensed under CC BY 4.0"); LUH2-GCB2025 (Zenodo 15557904); GHSL (GHSL FAQ); FAO statistical databases (FAO database terms of use, unless the metadata or page say otherwise; the Food Price Index page states none).
  - "GLW" is the AGLW annual livestock maps (Du et al., Zenodo 17128483): CC BY 4.0. Record 17128483 is the third version of the record (published 2025-09-17; corrected 2026-10-28 from "version 2", which read the API's 0-based version index). The ingest reads LivestockMap.zip (presplit_cc/fetch_livestock_extract.py), whose docstring gives it as 7.4 GB: the third version's LivestockMap.zip is 7,377,312,937 bytes, the second version's (Zenodo 17107247) 484,086,516 bytes, and the first version (Zenodo 11545701) has no file of that name (Zenodo API, read 2026-10-28). So the file read matches the third version by name and size; its checksum was not compared (the zip is no longer in Downloads).
  - No use restrictions: the MODIS products and MERRA-2 (NASA Earthdata data-use guidance: "there are no restrictions on the use of these data"; cite the data and acknowledge NASA).
  - Public domain: NOAA Coral Reef Watch ("All content on this website is considered to be in the public domain and may be distributed freely"; credit requested). CoralTemp's 1985-2002 segment still carries the L2 clause.
  - No licence on the provider page: TerraClimate (the Google Earth Engine catalogue says CC0); IBTrACS (no named licence; "full and open access" under the WDC policy; WMO guidance for commercial use).
  - Conflict: GRIP4 (globio.info: CC-0; Zenodo 6420961 metadata: cc-by-4.0). It is not a tree input on any problem.
  - Kraft LSTM and BARDL: re-implemented in the rival tool (docstrings of D:/treeroute/human_rivals_build/human/adapters/ndvi_kraft.py and ndvi_bardl.py), so no third-party code is used or archived. The BARDL authors' code (Zenodo 7005168) gives licence "other-open"; its GitHub repository has none.
  - Sources: web reads 2026-10-28, quoted with URLs in S9 Tables S9.1-S9.5. Not legal advice.

(1) UCDP: the downloads page (https://ucdp.uu.se/downloads/), under 'Please cite' for the UCDP Georeferenced Event Dataset (GED) Global version 26.1, gives after the two references that Table S9.2 cites (Davies et al. 2026; Sundberg and Melander 2013) the line 'UCDP is part of and funded by DEMSCORE, national research infrastructure grant 2021-00162 from the Swedish Research Council.' The same page says that all its datasets are free of charge and licensed under CC BY 4.0, and may be used and redistributed provided the publications listed with each are cited. (2) OpenStreetMap, for the travel-time map: the Malaria Atlas Project's catalogue record of the layer 2015_accessibility_to_cities_v1.0 (https://data.malariaatlas.org/geoserver/ows?service=CSW&version=2.0.2&request=GetRecordById&id=Explorer:2015_accessibility_to_cities_v1.0&elementSetName=full) ends its abstract with the notice 'Contains data from OpenStreetMap © OpenStreetMap contributors.', in which 'Contains data from OpenStreetMap' links to http://www.openstreetmap.org/copyright. Not legal advice.

Round 5 (2026-11-12, lead), the same MERIT Hydro page on the same day: notwithstanding the free licences, it asks users not to redistribute the data, in whole and in its original format, on other websites without the authors' explicit written permission; it states that the developers hold the copyright (2019), all rights reserved; and under ODbL 1.0 derived data must be made public under the same licence.
