The year in 'Contains modified Copernicus Climate Change Service information [year]' on the declarations' Copernicus line (ERA5-Land, AgERA5 and the FireCCI burned area, all from the Climate Data Store) is 2026. What the year denotes: ECMWF's guidance on acknowledging Climate Data Store data (https://confluence.ecmwf.int/display/CKB/How+to+acknowledge+and+cite+a+Climate+Data+Store+%28CDS%29+catalogue+entry+and+the+data+published+as+part+of+it, which now redirects to https://confluence.ecmwf.int/spaces/CKB/pages/197704114/How+to+acknowledge+and+cite+a+Climate+Data+Store+CDS+catalogue+entry+and+the+data+published+as+part+of+it; last modified 2025-07-03; re-read 2026-11-13) gives the notice as '[Generated using/Contains modified] Copernicus Climate Change Service information [year]. Neither the European Commission nor ECMWF is responsible for any use that may be made of the Copernicus information or data it contains.' and does not say what the year denotes. The record behind 2026: the Climate Data Store downloads that the project's ingest scripts record all fall in 2026 (file dates in UTC, read read-only on 2026-11-13): AgERA5 downloads in D:/agera5_dl (22 files, 2026-06-19 to 2026-06-22) and D:/agera5_dl2 (11 files, 2026-06-19), with the tables made from them in D:/agera5_daycounts (2026-06-19 to 2026-06-22; presplit_cc/ingest_agera5_daycounts.py); FireCCI downloads in D:/fire_dl (4 files, 2026-06-22) and their tables in D:/fire_monthly (2026-06-22; ingest_firecci_fire.py); an ERA5-Land download in D:/era5land_dl (2026-06-23) and the ERA5-Land tables that ingest_era5land_soilmoisture.py wrote in D:/soil_moisture_monthly (2026-06-22), that ingest_era5land_snowphen.py wrote in D:/snowphen_monthly (2026-06-23) and that ingest_era5land_extra.py wrote in D:/era5land_extra_monthly (2026-07-14). So 2026 is the year the project retrieved the Copernicus information; the guidance itself does not tie the year to retrieval. Not legal advice. (Corrected 2026-11-13 after the independent checks of the AV apply: the address had been given as https://confluence.ecmwf.int/spaces/CKB/pages/197704114/ with a trailing slash, which returns 404, and the file dates in this machine's local time, UTC+2 in June and July, without saying so; in UTC, the folders fire_dl, fire_monthly and soil_moisture_monthly are dated 2026-06-22, and agera5_daycounts begins on 2026-06-19. The year 2026 is unaffected.)
  - **GPCC download outcome (2026-11-13, ruling AX1; pre-edit copy paper/draft/_snapshots/20261113T1440Z_gpcc/apply/paper/PAPER_FACTS.md), dated note: the one GPCC SPI file read, and what it names.** Owner ruling AX1 (engine/OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md, section AX): on 2026-11-13 the owner answered item 1 of the decision list _archive_plan/OWNER_DECISIONS_2026-11-13.md by choosing option (a), which allows one file, NOAA's own October 2026 copy of gpcc-spi-gamma-01.nc, to be read: its first megabyte first, and the rest of the same file only if the product labels are not there. (1) The file: https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/nidis/gpcc_backup_11-10-2026/spi-gamma/gpcc-spi-gamma-01.nc. A HEAD request just before each read (the first at about 14:31 UTC) returned status 200, Content-Length 113,869,335, Content-Type application/x-netcdf, Accept-Ranges bytes, Last-Modified Tue, 10 Nov 2026 21:16:40 GMT and ETag "6c98217-65ff298358669". (2) The first request, started 2026-11-13T14:31:53Z: Range bytes=0-1048575, with If-Range on that ETag; reply 206, Content-Range bytes 0-1048575/113869335, with the same ETag and Last-Modified; 1,048,576 bytes, SHA-256 0b588810f70a8372bb544e8c815319bb836a509c66fc4d146680ebe532e08000. Its first eight bytes are the HDF5 signature (hex 89 48 44 46 0d 0a 1a 0a), so the file is netCDF-4, and the root attributes in that megabyte name no GPCC product, version or DOI, so the rest of the same file was fetched, as the answer allows. (3) The second request, started 2026-11-13T14:33:19Z: Range bytes=1048576-113869334, with If-Range on the same ETag; reply 206, Content-Range bytes 1048576-113869334/113869335, with the same ETag and Last-Modified; 112,820,759 bytes, SHA-256 40839570b0ad241ae78ba45c41020bfce4f1d4a1a1d43193ec899b38f9586b50. (4) The joined file: 113,869,335 bytes, SHA-256 378c9970dd153880b2341a1159f4026b7e220e3fe007f9373b08f646800c7f8e; netCDF data model NETCDF4, _NCProperties 'version=2,netcdf=4.9.3,hdf5=1.14.6', no groups. It was read as data only, with Python in isolated mode, in a scratch folder outside the project folder; an independent re-read on 2026-11-13 found the same sizes, hashes and attributes, and that the two pieces join to the file byte for byte. (5) What its attributes say. Of its 16 global attributes, title and summary both read 'SPI/Gamma 1-month values computed by NIDIS/NCEI/NOAA, for internal use only'; source 'NIDIS/NCEI/NOAA'; institution 'National Centers for Environmental Information (NCEI), NESDIS, NOAA, U.S. Department of Commerce'; date_created and date_modified '2026-10-10  05:30:13' (two spaces in the file); the other ten give the metadata conventions, the naming authority and the grid (ncei_template_version 'NCEI_NetCDF_Grid_Template_v2.0', standard_name_vocabulary 'Standard Name Table v35', naming_authority 'gov.noaa.ncei', Conventions 'CF-1.6, ACDD-1.3', and latitudes -89.5 to 89.5 degrees_north and longitudes 0.5 to 359.5 degrees_east). The variables are lat (180), lon (360), time (1,509 monthly steps, in days since 1901-01-01, from 1901-01-01 to 2026-09-01) and spi_01 (time, lat, lon; float32; calibration_start_year 1901 and calibration_end_year 2025; long_name and standard_name 'SPI/Gamma 1-month'; cell_methods 'time: standard_deviation (SPI at 1 month scale)'). No attribute names a GPCC product, version or DOI, and no printable run of the file's bytes holds 'GPCC', a DOI or the name Deutscher Wetterdienst. No attribute holds a person's name or an e-mail address (none is a creator, contributor, publisher or contact field, and no value matches an e-mail pattern); the only e-mail-like byte strings in the file are ten short random runs inside the compressed spi_01 data, and none is recorded here. (6) What follows. The creation date 2026-10-10 confirms that this backup copy is NCEI's run of 2026-10-10, as REFERENCES.md section B argues from file sizes. The project read an earlier run: NCEI rebuilds these files every month ('The process runs monthly, on the 10th of the month', nidis_gpcc_readme_c20220520.txt, as REFERENCES.md section B quotes it), and the project's 665 SPI sidecars were written on 2026-07-15 (section B), so by that schedule and those folder dates the project read the run of 2026-07-10, which was not kept and survives in none of the places checked (NCEI's server, the project's download folders and web.archive.org; REFERENCES.md section B). So the GPCC product behind the files the project read is not recorded, and this file shows only that its own metadata, those of the 1-month file of NCEI's run of 2026-10-10, name none. Nothing more is downloaded; the file and its two pieces stay in the scratch folder for the checks of this pass and are not archived. (7) In the paper: Table S9.2's GPCC SPI row keeps 'NCEI n.d.' and says 'GPCC product not recorded (S9.6, item 3)' in place of its citation marker, and S9.6 item 3 states the outcome, with the dates 2026-07-10, 2026-10-10 and 2026-11-13 from this note; the owner marker on the GPCC SPI row in the acknowledgements (submission/declarations.md) is closed; REFERENCES.md section B's GPCC row is closed, and GPCC_SPI_NCEI's Check says why the product is not recorded; Supplement S1's row LI7 says that the GPCC product is not recorded either, and S4.10's pending marker names the rulings file's section AX. (8) The file's title and summary call its values 'for internal use only'. Table S9.2's licence cell for the GPCC SPI ('Not stated; the page asks users to "refer to the GPCC and the product specific DOI"') is unchanged in this pass; whether it should quote these words is a question for the owner's next decision list. (GPCC download outcome, fixes after the checks (2026-11-13; pre-edit copy paper/draft/_snapshots/20261113T1440Z_gpcc/fix/paper/PAPER_FACTS.md): after the two independent checks of the AX1 apply, the heading of this note adds 'dated note', as the four other dated notes of 2026-11-13 under L7 have it; item (5) had said that 'the other ten describe the format and the grid', though naming_authority and standard_name_vocabulary describe neither; item (6) had said that the run of 2026-07-10 'survives nowhere', more than the places checked in REFERENCES.md section B show, and that 'this file shows only that the metadata of NCEI's run of 2026-10-10 name none', though only the 1-month file of that run was read; item (7) now also names Supplement S1's row LI7 and S4.10's pending marker, which the AX1 apply changed too; L8 gains a dated note that points here. No number or date changed.)

  - **Ruling AV (2026-11-13), dated note: what the rulings settle here** (after the independent checks of the AV apply). Ruling AT7 (owner, 2026-11-09) keeps out of the public archive the files that questions (a)-(d) are about (the coral per-visit answers built on CoralTemp/OSTIA, the VT0007 per-location answers and maps tied to GADM, the rival input exports holding CoralTemp- or TerraClimate-derived per-location values, and the per-visit cyclone columns) and, in its words, 'Settles licence calls L10 (a)-(d) for submission'. Owner ruling AV11 (2026-11-12) treats the Coral Reef Watch Outlook's per-visit answers as built on CoralTemp, so AT7 keeps them out too (question (a)). Owner ruling AV10 (2026-11-12) keeps TerraClimate's per-location values out of the archive, cited, not shared, like those of the other inputs whose call read 'until confirmed', so question (c) no longer bears on what the archive holds; the licence is left as the two pages state it (Supplement S9, Table S9.2). No ruling answers the questions as asked, whether these files count as derived products or as redistribution, so this fact stays pending for them; for submission none of these files is archived (Supplement S9, S9.6 items 1 and 8; S1 LI9).

  - **The O9 extract (section 5B; not built, ruling AU2, 2026-11-11; until then outline decision O9 was not ruled; see the round-4 note).** The per-location values, as stored before lagging, of the tree inputs whose S9 row says Yes (the SPEIbase-derived columns in their own ODbL files; the soil columns held until the SoilGrids release is recorded), with the labels, the label-derived tree inputs and the MOD13C2 annual aggregates that forest loss uses, at the bank and scored locations of the four gridded runs. **Size:** about 2.0-2.2 GB (GB = 10^9 bytes), the plan's planning figure, from three sampled write tests that wrote to memory only (section 5B.5): 1.94-2.21 GB with 12 regions per package, written as one table per region, part and store, rows in store order, which is not the planned layout (the plan's own test; corrected in archive fix 3 from "1.93-2.20 GB", a rounding down, and from "wrote the planned layout"); 1.96-2.22 GB with 6 regions per package (the first independent plan check); and about 1.6-1.9 GB with 10-13 regions per package (the second independent plan check, which wrote the planned layout of section 5B.3: per region one inputs, one SPEIbase and one labels table, sorted by location). 972,379,720 bytes, scaled from 11,284 Parquet footers, is only a lower bound (corrected label, see the archive fix 2 note). **Coverage of each run's tree inputs** (M13: PM2.5 122, NDVI 498, burned fraction 131, forest loss 308; section 5B.2, recounted from derived.json R8, the run manifests and the store catalogs, and agreeing with the independent plan check): covered 54, 176, 62 and 135 (of which own-history inputs, built from the labels: 6, 6, 6 and 5); held until the SoilGrids release is recorded: 10 on each; not covered: 58, 312, 59 and 163. **Router context, counted apart:** covered 2, 7, 2 and 0; not covered 0, 2 (NDVI's two TerraClimate context columns), 0 and 0. Corrected label: the earlier wording, "50 of PM2.5's 118 kept tree inputs, 177 of NDVI's 501, 58 of burned fraction's 127 and 130 of forest loss's 303 (not covered: 58, 314, 59 and 163)", counted router-context columns as covered or not covered and set them against Table S2.3's keep and watch columns (P18c); those are not tree inputs (the glossary: tree inputs never include the router context).
  - **None of the engine's fitted trees (sections 4.1, 5B.7 and 7.1).** No headline run folder holds fitted trees, and the plan archives none of the engine's fitted trees (specialist trees, correctors, error-weight forests; decision D5). The only fitted trees in the archive are the 16 LightGBM model dumps of the gridded common-rival boards (model_lgbm*.txt, 196,667,764 bytes; with the 11 ridge dumps model_ridge.json, 27 files, 197,170,333 bytes), which hold only aggregate statistics of their inputs and stay in (decision D1). So the archive alone does not re-run the routing, with or without the extract: the tree inputs it does not hold must be rebuilt by script where their sources are recorded, some cannot be rebuilt (L8; Section 6.4), and every tree must be refitted.
  - **One keep-out rule (decision D1; section 4.2).** Per-location values of an input whose licence is not stated or not yet read stay out (TerraClimate, GPCC SPI, ERSST, the NOAA indices, GMIA, IBTrACS and the unrecorded static sources); per-visit values and fitted models derived from CoralTemp stay out (L2; ruling AT7), while the coral run's and the boards' records and aggregate scores stay in (AT7; corrected in archive fix 3 from "anything derived from CoralTemp stays out", which the plan does not apply); aggregate statistics over all rows (the model dumps' feature ranges and split values, the ridge dumps' means and standard deviations) may stay in. The plan's column scan of the second revision's listing (23:12 UTC: 736 Parquet schemas, 619 CSV header lines and the JSON maps) finds no other planned file with per-location values of those inputs once the BARDL maps are out; the NDVI run's context-missing tables hold keys, true/false flags and, in three of them, the label y (L9), but no input value (corrected in archive fix 3 from "true/false flags only").

## 15. Facts added from code and protocols during drafting (2026-10-26)

Added 2026-10-26 by the drafting agent. Each fact below was checked against its source before it was written here. Each replaces a "src <path>" comment in paper/draft/02_method.md, 03_protocol.md, S7_implementation_checks.md or fig1_spec.md, which now cite the K ID instead. Code paths are under engine/treeroute/ unless stated. The code read is the LIVE engine (930d961c); the grid runs' engines are linked to it by byte-identical no-op proofs (M1). Status "final" means read from frozen code, a signed protocol, a ruling or a run file. Where the check found a draft sentence or an older fact imprecise, the K fact gives the corrected statement and says so.

### 15.1 Units, rows and router type
- **K1 (final). Coral's unit, site and visit.** In the table (coral) run each survey visit is one row, and that row is the engine's unit for the bank, the scored set and routing (ruling U1: "each sample row is the engine unit"; R18: "every row of a group is a bank unit; its test rows are the scored units"). The package's key_unit is the reef site ('<ecoregion id>_<lat>_<lon>' at 4 decimals), and the overlap-block rule (R14) and the history depth h (R12) are computed over sites, not visits. Sources: pipeline/table_passes.py module docstring; derive/table.py (R12, R14:overlap_blocks and R18 for tables); engine/datasets/coral/catalog.yaml (key_row, key_unit).
- **K2 (final). The newest readable label.** A row predicting step t may read labels up to the issue step t - L at a lead L >= 1 and up to t - 1 at the nowcast. The history block then moves this back by the R17 start offset (1 step on NDVI and fire, 0 on PM2.5 and defo; M35). Sources: timing/cuts.py latest_label_step; io/apply.py history_block.
- **K3 (final). Bank units.** A group's bank units are a fixed stride over the sorted ids of its candidate units (units with at least one valid label at or before the training cut): stride s = max(1, N // n), the first n kept, no seed. n is 100 for the per-unit router type (C7), floor(60,000 / the median valid inner-fit rows per unit) for the region type (4,615 on defo), and every unit for the neighbour type (on coral, every visit). Sources: derive/unit_rules.py derive_r18_bank_count; split.py stride_pick, candidate_units; engine_constants.py C7.
- **K4 (final). Scored units.** Per group: max(10, floor(20 x n / G)) (C16), with n the bank-count rule value and G the number of groups, capped at the group's bank count. They are the bank units at the evenly spaced positions floor(i (N - 1)/(m - 1)), i = 0..m - 1, of the sorted bank units (first and last included; no seed). On coral the scored units are the test-marked visits. Sources: split.py scored_count_budget, evenly_spaced_subset; engine_constants.py C16; derive/table.py R18.
- **K5 (final). Training rows (the code's "donor rows").** At a cut: every group's bank-unit rows with a valid label, dated at or before the cut and with the label's own window ended, in ascending order of unit id, then step. y comes from the label vault on its original scale; X is the kept tree inputs, 0-filled (R30). Coral's 46 training-only groups contribute their rows (ruling W1). The training mean is the mean of link(y) over these rows (the normal's last fallback, R15). The training median is the median of y over the fit-cut rows; it is coral's reference and decline target. Sources: fit/context.py DonorRows, FitContext.donor_rows; pipeline/stages.py donor_link_means; report/metrics.py baseline_donor_median; pipeline/table_passes.py (median_fit from the fit-cut rows); engine/datasets/coral/exceptions.yaml (W1).
- **K6 (final). History depth h.** h is the numpy median, over every candidate unit of every group (before the bank pick), of the number of valid target rows from Y0 to the inner-fit end; a candidate with none counts 0. Per-unit if h >= 24 (C5), region if 2 <= h < 24, neighbour if h < 2. On coral the units counted are sites (K1). Sources: derive/router_rules.py derive_r12_router_kind; derive/orchestrate.py (inner_counts).

### 15.2 Trees
- **K7 (final). Region router.** One pooled ridge per group on the largest evenly spaced set of whole bank units whose fit rows total at most 60,000 (C7). The fit rows are centred (the intercept); the normal enters unpenalised, centred on its pooled fit-row mean, and is projected out first (closed form). The tree weights are penalised by lambda x (trace of the Gram of the tree block centred on its fit-row column means / number of tree columns + 1e-12). No context, no weighted solve. Scored units are decoupled from the cap: a scored unit outside the kept set is still scored, with its own normal. Sources: route/region.py module docstring, region_ridge, region_predict.
- **K8 (final). Region climatology identity.** The region router's trees-off (intercept + normal, fitted on the capped fit rows) is asserted equal to the normal on every score row of every region fit, within the R36 tolerance (K60); a breach stops the run. This is the region-router counterpart of M33. Source: route/region.py _route (route/kinds.py _trees_off_checked).
- **K9 (final). Tree targets, inputs and answers.** Trees are fitted to y as the vault serves it, with no link. The link is applied to y (and to the normal) only when the router rows are built, so tree answers T_k, and T_k + C_k on corrected arms, enter every router untransformed. Tree inputs are the kept tree features (after the ban-list verdicts and the lags, 0-filled) plus the own-history features; router-context columns are a separate block and are not tree inputs. Sources: fit/bank.py fit_donor_bank; fit/context.py DonorRows (y from the vault, X the kept tree features); pipeline/stages.py per_unit_rows; fit/tables.py _fill_body; io/lastvalue.py docstring (verdicts applied before fills).
- **K10 (final). Trees without a corrector, and the placebo.** A tree with fewer than 50 out-of-bag rows gets no corrector (D52); its column in the corrected table is its plain answer T_k. The placebo is the corrector fit on the tree's out-of-bag residuals, which are in the group's training-row order (unit id, then step), rolled by half their length (np.roll), with the corrector's own seed. It is never selectable. Sources: fit/tables.py _fill_body; fit/correctors.py CORRECTOR_MIN_OOB_ROWS, fit_placebo_corrector; fit/context.py DonorRows.
- **K11 (final). Overlap link rule.** With grid indices, two groups are linked when a unit of one is at the same grid position as, or in the 8-neighbourhood of, a unit of the other. Without grid indices (coral), they are linked when a unit of one lies within sqrt(2) x the larger of the two groups' median nearest-unit spacings (central angles) of a unit of the other; on coral these units are sites (K1). A group with one unit and no grid indices is refused, because no spacing exists. Blocks are the connected components. Sources: split.py derive_overlap_blocks docstring, _linked_units_spacing; APP_PLAN.md R14.

### 15.3 Routers
- **K12 (final). Router context per package.** PM2.5 and fire: ctx:month (1-12) and ctx:lat, the unit's static panel latitude (features/build.py of each package). NDVI: four month harmonics (sin1, cos1, sin2, cos2) and five drivers from the run89 top-5 list (test-exposed, P23). Coral: the calendar month, sin and cos of the day of year, |lat| of the site, and the MMM from SST dated before the feature cut. Defo: none. Sources: engine/datasets/{pm25,fire,ndvi,coral}/catalog.yaml router_context entries; engine/datasets/{pm25,fire}/features/build.py.
- **K13 (final). Context standardisation.** Each context column is centred and scaled by its mean and population standard deviation (+ 1e-9) over all router fit rows of the pass, after rows with missing context leave the fit. On the per-unit router these are every scored unit's own fit rows, pooled; on coral, the pass's candidate surveys. The same constants are applied to the score rows. Sources: route/kinds.py router_standardisation, standardise_context; pipeline/stages.py pass_rows; pipeline/table_router.py step 3.
- **K14 (final from code; numerically checked on synthetic data by the agent, 2026-10-26; not measured in the runs). Latitude in the per-unit router (PM2.5 and fire).** Verified, with one addition to the draft claim. ctx:lat is the unit's static latitude, so after the pooled standardisation (K13) it is one constant per unit and lies in the span of the unpenalised intercept. Every per-unit fit has fewer rows than columns, so the exact dual solve is used: it projects the intercept and normal out first, which turns the latitude column into zero up to rounding. Latitude's coefficient is therefore zero; the same holds in the primal and on the weighted arms, since the intercept is free. But the context penalty scale kappa_ctx is the mean of the raw (unprojected) diagonal over the two context columns, (sum_t w_t month_t^2 + lat_u^2 sum_t w_t) / 2. So latitude changes kappa_ctx, and through it the penalty on the month coefficient: units far from the pooled mean latitude have their month coefficient shrunk more. It is not inert in the forecast. A synthetic check with route/solve.py (168 rows, 600 trees, uniform and random weights) gave latitude coefficients of order 1e-19; predictions moved by up to 0.0028 when lat_std changed from 0.5 to 2.5, through the month coefficient. Sources: engine/datasets/pm25/features/build.py and fire/features/build.py ('ctx:lat': frame['lat']); route/kinds.py router_standardisation; route/solve.py solve_local, solve_local_dual; M13 (two context columns on PM2.5 and fire).
- **K15 (final). Per-unit design and fit rows.** The design is [1, n_u - n-bar_u (centred on the unit's fit-row mean), standardised context, masked tree answers]. The fit rows are the scored unit's own rows with a valid label and complete router context (ruling G1), dated at or before the pass's cut. The link is applied to y and to the normal only. Sources: route/kinds.py build_column_block, PerUnitRows; pipeline/stages.py per_unit_rows, drop_missing_context; pipeline/router.py module docstring.
- **K16 (final). Penalty scale.** Each penalised block (context, trees) is penalised by a x (the weighted sum of squares of its own raw columns / number of columns), floored at 1e-12. That is the mean diagonal of its block of X'WX before the unpenalised intercept and normal are projected out; the dual takes the trace on the raw sqrt(w)-scaled block, not the projected one. GLS weights are first divided by their mean over the unit's fit rows, which does not move the minimiser. Sources: route/solve.py solve_local, solve_local_dual, grid_dual, unit_mean_weights.
- **K17 (final). Back-transform and clip.** identity z; log exp(z) - c; arcsine UB x sin^2(clip(z, 0, pi/2)). There is no smearing or bias correction. Every back-transformed prediction and every baseline is clipped to the target's legal (declared) range, and the pre-clip value is saved. Sources: layer/links.py link_inverse, clip_to_legal.
- **K18 (final). Short-history fallback.** A scored unit with fewer than 24 valid fit rows is routed by its group's region router, fitted on the group's bank units (the 60,000-row cap included), with no context. The fallback has no weighted form, so a GLS arm's short-history rows take its unweighted twin's predictions. Its level is searched once per arm's link and table on the fallback units' inner-score rows pooled across groups (lowest pooled MAE, ties to the first level). The lambda centre is used when fewer than 10 fallback units have such rows (C15). Each forecast lead searches its own level on its guard surface (ruling H5). A filled row carries the same prediction in every per-unit grid column. Sources: route/region.py module docstring, search_fallback_level, fill_fallback; pipeline/router.py module docstring, FALLBACK_GLS_NOTE; engine_constants.py C15; pipeline/ladder.py step 4.
- **K19 (final). Neighbour pool and design.** Candidates are training rows with a valid label dated at or before the pass's cut. A candidate is eligible for a scored row when its target day is strictly before the row's issue day (target day minus lead) and it is not the row itself. The pool is the 150 nearest eligible candidates by haversine central angle, then the 50 of those nearest in Z, the standardised tree inputs (C11). Z fills missing inputs with 0, uses the mean and population SD over the pass's candidates, and sets candidate-constant columns to 0. With fewer than 12 eligible candidates the row takes the training median in every arm. The local design is [intercept, standardised context, kept tree answers], with only the intercept unpenalised (NEIGHBOUR_UNPENALISED_COLUMNS = 1) and a_tree = 0.5 x level, a_ctx = a_tree x ratio (C12 neighbour centres 0.5 / 0.25), each penalised block trace-normalised on its own raw block (its penalty scaled by the block's mean diagonal, as K16; added 2026-10-27, Figure 2 revision). There is no normal column; trees-off is the intercept + context ridge on the pool. Sources: route/neighbour.py module docstring (line 43: "each penalised block trace-normalised on its own raw block"), NEIGHBOUR_UNPENALISED_COLUMNS; route/solve.py solve_local (block trace / column count); engine_constants.py C11, C12.
- **K20 (final). Neighbour drop set.** A scored row's router drops the trees of its own overlap block and of the block of every group present in its pool, because each pool row was trained on by its own group's trees. A row whose drop set holds every tree of the bank takes its pool's trees-off in every tree arm (owner item O8). Source: route/neighbour.py module docstring.

### 15.4 Cuts, passes and the ladder
- **K21 (final). Cuts.** The training cut tau is the last step of the year before the test window (R6), and the inner cut tau_in the last step of the inner-fit end year (R7). Grid fit cut: tau_L = min(tau, tau - (L - 1)); guard cut min(tau_in, tau_in - (L - 1)). Events (coral, in days): lead 0 takes the last day of the train-end year (and of the inner-fit end year for the guard); a lead L >= 1 takes the first test day minus L (and the first inner-score day minus L), which equals tau - L + 1 (tau_in - L + 1). Source: timing/cuts.py fit_cut_step, guard_cut_step, event_fit_cut_day, event_guard_cut_day, cuts_for_leads.
- **K22 (final). Inner decision and test round.** The nowcast decision is made as if the run stood at the inner cut: the bank is refit at the inner (guard) cut and the decision rows carry an own-history block built at that cut (L6 b; A7.inner_cut_bank_refit, test-exposed). The test round reads the fit-cut objects. It routes every tree arm (plain, corrected and placebo tables) at the adopted grid point, or at the best tree arm's penalty when the layer declined, and opens the test labels only after the adoption is locked. c is one value per lead, from the training rows at that lead's fit cut, and serves both that lead's decision or guard surface and its test round (D63 a). Sources: pipeline/passes.py module docstring, test_pass; layer/links.py module docstring (D63 a).
- **K23 (final). Forecast-ladder steps.** At lead L the whole feature row, router context included, moves to the issue step, and the history block is rebuilt (step 1). The bank, correctors, placebos and c are refit at the lead's fit cut; the error-weight forest is refit only when the pinned arm is weighted (step 2). The pin is the nowcast's adopted arm and penalty, or, when the nowcast declined, its best tree arm and that arm's penalty (R29). A second bank is fitted at the guard cut, and no guard row lies in its label window (H2; step 3). The guard rows are the scored units' valid rows after the inner-fit end up to the lead's fit cut. A pinned arm that fails the guard declines to the decline target (the identity-link trees-off), and the lead's adoption is hashed before its test labels open (step 4). The guard reads the lead's fit-cut c and, for a weighted pin, a weight model whose target uses the fit-cut bank (GUARD_FIT_CUT_RULE). Sources: pipeline/ladder.py module docstring steps 1-6, GUARD_FIT_CUT_RULE; layer/decide.py module docstring (R29).
- **K24 (final). Last-value fill of tree inputs.** A tree feature missing at a step inside the unit's record takes the unit's most recent earlier value only when the gap lasts at most C18 = 2 months. That is 2 steps on monthly data and 0 on annual data, so there is no fill on annual data. A longer gap is left whole to R30 (0-fill), and so are the steps before a unit's first value and after its last. Router context is never filled. Correction to the draft wording "from at most two months back": a gap longer than two months is not partly filled. Source: io/lastvalue.py module docstring (ruling J2 d, reading (1)).
- **K25 (final). Missing router context and missing issue rows.** A router fit row with missing or non-finite context leaves the fit (G1). A scored row with missing context is left out of scoring, saved with a flag and counted (S1). At a lead, a test row whose issue row is absent is left out and counted (H4); a row with a partly missing moved feature is counted, not left out. Sources: pipeline/stages.py G1_RULE, S1_RULE; pipeline/router.py module docstring; pipeline/ladder.py steps 1 and 6.
- **K26 (final). Own-history fill chain.** A missing own-history value is filled from, in order: the row's own trailing means (shortest window first); the unit's as-of season mean; the unit's as-of all-season mean; the training mean at the cut. It is never filled with 0, and never from a label at or after the row. Steps 2 and 3 read only labels dated at or before the row's newest readable step and at or before the history's cut. Two readings remain marked "owner confirmation asked" (M36). Source: io/apply.py history_block.

### 15.5 Adaptation layer
- **K27 (final). Link set and c (refines M39).** Identity is always in. log(y + c) joins when the legal minimum is >= 0, the training rows hold at least one positive value, and training y has more than 2 distinct values (zero counts as a value). Arcsine joins when the legal range is [0, UB] with UB finite and training y has more than 2 distinct values. The link set is read from the training rows at the nowcast fit cut. c is the 1st percentile (numpy linear) of positive training y at each lead's fit cut (D63 a: one c per lead). M39's "more than 2 distinct positive values" is imprecise; the rule above is the code's. Sources: derive/rules.py derive_r22_links; layer/links.py module docstring, c_for_cut.
- **K28 (final). Error-weight forest.** The target is |y - mean of the plain answers of the trees outside the row's own overlap block|, per training row. The forest has 120 trees, depth 8, minimum leaf 20, max_features 1.0, bootstrap, random_state 0 and 4 threads (C13). It is fitted on the 0-filled tree inputs of all groups' training rows at the lead's guard cut (tau_in at the nowcast). On a row it was fitted on, sigma is its out-of-bag prediction; elsewhere, the whole forest's. The target's tree answers come from the bank of the lead's fit cut, so at the nowcast they come from trees that saw the inner-score years (L6_ANSWERS_DISCLOSURE; M20). Sources: fit/weights.py module docstring, cross_region_error, fit_weight_model, weight_model_for_cut, L6_ANSWERS_DISCLOSURE; engine_constants.py C13.
- **K29 (final). Error weights.** sigma is floored at 1% of the training SD of y (identity link, ddof 0) on the forest's fit rows. It is then clipped to the 5th-95th percentiles (numpy linear) of the floored out-of-bag sigma on those rows, and inverted, w = 1/sigma^2. The smallest weights are then raised to (largest / 100) over the set of rows weighted at once: all router fit rows of the pass on the grids, and the candidate surveys of each part (fold or round) on coral. Sources: fit/weights.py sigma_floor, sigma_clip_bounds, row_weights, weights_for_rows; pipeline/router.py fit_weights; pipeline/table_router.py (candidates' weights per part).
- **K30 (final). Penalty search (R26).** Every grid point of an arm is scored by MAE in y units, on back-transformed, clipped predictions over all decision-surface rows. A tie goes to the first grid point; the grid is level-major (levels outer, ratios inner). An edge pick is flagged and never extended. Sources: layer/decide.py module docstring; route/kinds.py per_unit_grid.
- **K31 (final). Coral decision surface.** The rows are the training visits dated in (tau_in, tau] with a valid label, routed with the inner-cut bank, with pools drawn from candidates at or before tau_in. There is one fold per group (R13), and the fold's rows are that group's rows in the inner-score years. Each fold's c, error-weight forest and training median come from the guard-cut training rows outside that group's overlap block. Training-only groups form folds too (W1). Sources: pipeline/table_passes.py _fold_objects, decision; fit/weights.py weight_models_for_folds; APP_PLAN.md R13.
- **K32 (final). Candidate order (the adoption tie rule).** Links in the order identity, log, arcsine; within a link, plain before corrected and unweighted before error-weighted; then the trees-off arms; then the placebos. The first eligible arm in this order wins an MAE tie. Source: layer/arms.py module docstring.
- **K33 (final). Guard reference and decline target on the grids.** Each link's trees-off, back-transformed and clipped, is that link's guard reference; the decline target is the identity-link trees-off, the arithmetic normal. This holds for log and arcsine arms alike. For the neighbour router (V1) both are the training median. Sources: route/kinds.py reference_predictions; layer/decide.py module docstring (N2, R27, V1).

### 15.6 Package and plug-in
- **K34 (final). Package facts.** catalog.yaml holds package facts only: each column's role and, for the target, its window, time stamp, legal range and measurement-error floor. Physical store facts (dtype, units, cadence, fills) live in shared store catalogs included by dataset.yaml. Sources: engine/datasets/pm25/catalog.yaml header; engine/datasets/pm25/dataset.yaml.
- **K35 (final). The Type-3 plug-in contract (not built, M53).** An optional per-dataset plug-in is run only after adoption.json is hashed, twice ('shipped' and 'blind'). It writes its own prediction file and a separate report block, and it never enters Type-2 selection or the Type-2 headline row. Source: APP_PLAN.md 1.6.
- **K36 (final). Why 11 coral ecoregions are excluded.** Recorded reason: the engine's default rules refuse them, and owner ruling V5 (2026-10-19) leaves them out in split.yaml, disclosed. Seven have a single reef site (Ashmore Reef, Gulf of California, Houtman Abrolhos Islands, Line Islands, Pompey and Swain Reefs, South China Sea, Southern Seychelles). The overlap rule cannot compute a nearest-site spacing for a one-site group without grid indices, and split.py refuses it (SPEC_TABLE_MODE.md 7 O14). Three have no training visit at one or more of the six bank cuts (Birds Head Peninsula, Gulf of Guinea to Sierra Leone, Kimberley Coast); the table option refuses a group with 0 rows at a bank cut (O13; a tree bag needs at least one row). One has both problems (North-west Arabian Sea). Together they hold 35 test and 9 training visits. No scientific or data-quality reason beyond these refusals is recorded. Sources: engine/datasets/coral/split.yaml (header and 'excluded' entries); engine/datasets/coral/exceptions.yaml lines 124-126; engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT_coral.md line 300 (also the signed copy D:/treeroute/registry/freeze/PREREGISTRATION_20261020T100907Z.md line 300); engine/OWNER_RULINGS_2026-10-06.md V5 (line 107); split.py _linked_units_spacing; fit/bank.py bag_size; derive/table.py docstring (O13).

### 15.7 Protocol: locks and metrics
- **K37 (final). Opening test labels.** The vault releases a pass's test labels only if that pass's adoption file exists, its SHA-256 equals both the hash given and the hash the lock recorded, and the lock was written before the request. A lead pass also needs a locked nowcast adoption. The opening is logged and marked on disk, write-once. Source: labels/vault.py open_test_labels.
- **K38 (final). Coral rival dry pass.** Before the common-rival protocol was signed, the coral rival picks were tuned and locked on the inner window only, in a dry pass with no test row read (picks content hash 5ad9502f..., two identical passes). The real coral tune had to reproduce them. Source: D:/treeroute/registry/rivals/PREREGISTRATION_rivals_common_20261021T125123Z.md section 7.
- **K39 (final). MAE.** MAE is the mean absolute error in y units on clipped predictions. The pooled MAE over all scored rows is the headline, with the macro value (unweighted mean over groups) beside it; the verdict uses per-group MAEs. Sources: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md sections 6-7; report/metrics.py mae.
- **K40 (final). Anomaly R2.** 1 - SSE(prediction) / SSE(normal), with the arithmetic (identity-link) normal for every arm (D68), so guessing the normal scores 0. It is NaN under 10 rows or when SSE(normal) is 0. Source: report/metrics.py anomaly_r2.
- **K41 (final). Hit tolerance.** delta = 0.5 x the mean absolute deviation of the fit-cut training rows from their identity-link normal (on coral, from the training median). It is computed once per run and reused at every lead; a declared measurement floor gives a second hit rate. Sources: report/metrics.py hit_tolerance; pipeline/passes.py hit_tolerance; pipeline/table_passes.py hit_tolerance; engine_constants.py S2.hit_tolerance_deviation_share.
- **K42 (final). Baselines (corrects the persistence lag in the draft).** Predict-zero: 0, clipped. Guess-normal: the arithmetic normal, clipped. Last-5-years normal: per season key, the mean of y over the unit's rows in the 5 calendar years ending with the cut's year; a season with no row takes the unit's mean over those years. At lead L, persistence is the newest readable label y(t - g), with g = L + the R17 start offset (g = L on PM2.5 and defo, L + 1 on NDVI and fire). Damped persistence is normal(t) + phi_u (y(t - g) - normal(t - g)). phi_u is the unit's regression-through-the-origin slope of leave-out seasonal anomalies on their values g steps earlier, from label pairs at or before the lead's fit cut, clipped to [0, 1], and 0 when undefined. Sources: report/metrics.py baseline_predict_zero, baseline_guess_normal, baseline_last5_normal, baseline_persistence, damped_persistence_phi, baseline_damped_persistence; pipeline/ladder.py step 5 (gap = L + offset); engine_constants.py S2.recent_normal_years.
- **K43 (final). What the headline tables print.** Full metric sets (MAE, anomaly R2, corr2, hit rate, hit rate at the floor, calibration ratio, pre-clip rate, detection) are printed for the adopted answer, the reference, the guess-normal and the last-5-years normal. Only MAE is printed for predict-zero, persistence and damped persistence. Source: D:/treeroute/runs/_headline_tables/headline_93b64854d1f9.csv header.
- **K44 (final). Nowcast persistence as a reference row.** Part A lists persistence and damped persistence as never-deciding reference rows at every problem and lead (the nowcast rows come from the human tool; flagged "the human baseline to beat" for PM2.5 only). RIVALS_TABLE.csv holds base__human_persistence rows at lead 0 for PM2.5, NDVI and defo. Sources: D:/treeroute/registry/rivals/PREREGISTRATION_human_partA_20261025T105940Z.txt section 2; D:/treeroute/rivals_table/20261025T162302876402Z/RIVALS_TABLE.csv. Their values: K76.
- **K45 (final). TIMID flag.** A rival's MAE win is flagged when its calibration ratio (mean prediction / mean observation) is below 0.5 or its corr2 is below half of ours; the verdict does not change. Sources: common rival protocol section 4; report/metrics.py calibration_ratio.
- **K46 (final; completed 2026-10-26, consolidation pass). A declined lead's contrast.** single_verdict tags a declined lead DECLINED but still computes and prints its contrast (reference MAE - adopted MAE per group). **In the runs, a declined lead's verdict row names the decline target as both the adopted answer and the reference** (arm__identity_trees_off_unweighted on the grids, base__donor_median on coral), **so the printed contrast is exactly 0 [0, 0] at all six declined leads**, including PM2.5 at 12 months, whose pinned arm uses the log link; groups_better is 0. The skill guard's own reference (the pinned link's trees-off) is not the printed reference. So the statement that a declined log-link contrast "need not be zero" (draft/03_protocol.md Section 3.4; draft/S8_protocol_details.md S8.4) is wrong for these runs (draft/review_logs/limits_review_log.md blocker B2). Sources: report/metrics.py single_verdict; D:/treeroute/runs/_headline_tables/verdicts_9abcea419ce2.csv, ndvi/verdicts_c4cd7e7c2610.csv, fire/verdicts_7ee7167afe8d.csv, coral/verdicts_a9d948cbf26b.csv (adopted, reference, declined, contrast_mean, contrast_low, contrast_high).
- **K47 (final). Jackknife and blocked jackknife.** SE = sqrt((n - 1)/n x sum of squared deviations of the leave-one-out means from their mean); interval mean +/- 1.96 SE; NaN under 3 groups. The blocked jackknife uses overlap blocks as the leave-out unit, each block valued at the mean of its groups' values (report only). Sources: report/metrics.py group_jackknife, blocked_jackknife.
- **K48 (final). The equivalence band.** The band is the median jackknife SE of the run's band pairs on the nowcast test window, with identical contrasts left out. The pairs are every tree arm against its own link's unweighted trees-off and every corrector against its placebo, in every candidate link. verdict.json records the source as "this run's own D75 contrasts". The nowcast band decides at every lead and on both rival boards. Sources: engine/PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT.md section 7; report/metrics.py band_from_pairs, equivalence_band; each run's verdict.json (band_pairs, band_source); common rival protocol section 4; Part A section 5.
- **K49 (final). Coral look-ahead canary.** The canary rebuilds rows from sources truncated at the cut and compares them byte for byte: 822 row-leads, 0 mismatches. The 21 of the 74 columns that the checker does not recompute rest on it. Sources: engine/datasets/coral/AUDIT_CHECKLIST.md (full build 2026-10-20); engine/datasets/coral/ingest/README.md (V10).

### 15.8 Protocol: rivals
- **K50 (final). Why the absolute-error booster runs on coral only.** It was ruled in for coral only by owner ruling C12 (QUESTIONS_ROUND3_HANDOFF.md:473), before the protocol was written, and is labelled timid-prone. No further rationale is recorded. Sources: common rival protocol section 2 (C6); engine/RIVALS_PLAN_COMMON.md C6.
- **K51 (final). Rival rows.** Rivals fit on the run's own bank-unit rows up to each cut, all rows with no stride, refit at each lead's fit cut; the test rows are the run's saved test rows. The board is global ("gauged global"): each rival fits on every kept group's bank units, including the target region's own past. Sources: common rival protocol section 3 items 1 and 4; section 9 item 4.
- **K52 (final). Rival inputs.** The run's kept tree columns, history columns, declared router context and the unit's normal; the normal is both a fixed offset and the last column. No unit ID is given. Coral's normal is one tune-row training median (the guard-cut median). Latitude reaches the rivals on PM2.5 and fire through the context. Sources: common rival protocol section 3 items 2-3; section 9 items 7-8.
- **K53 (final). Rival tuning and lock.** Tuning is at lead 0 only, on the inner window only, once. Trials fit on the tune rows (labels at or before the nowcast guard cut) and are scored on our layer's own decision rows, by MAE of the clipped prediction. Ties go to R22 link order, then grid order, then the smaller checkpoint. The pick is pinned at every lead and hashed before any test file is read; every rival is reported, with no best-rival headline. Sources: common rival protocol section 3 items 8, 14 and 17.
- **K54 (final). Rival guard.** Each rival gets our N2 guard on its own guard rows: at lead 0 on the inner rows, at lead L on the lead's guard rows, against the run's saved unweighted trees-off of the rival's link. It passes if and only if its MAE is strictly lower. The guarded answer is the raw answer if it passes, else the run's decline target (on coral, the training median). Guarded vs our headline decides; raw vs our pinned model is printed beside. Source: common rival protocol section 3 item 13.
- **K55 (final). Rival contrast and board checks.** d_g = MAE_rival(g) - MAE_ours(g), with every group weighted the same, judged by the engine's contrast_tag with the run's nowcast band and the z = 1.96 jackknife; BEATS = HELPS, LOSES = HURTS. The export refuses unless the fit and test rows match the run's (row-key hashes, counts). At lead 0 the board refuses unless our pooled and every per-group MAE equal the run's own score files to a relative 1e-12; at the leads this is recorded as "not checkable". Sources: common rival protocol section 3 item 1; section 4.
- **K56 (final). Common-rival settings (Table 3).** Ridge: alpha {0.1, 1, 10, ..., 1e6}, numpy Cholesky, columns standardised on the fit rows, fit-row mean plus a missing flag, intercept unpenalised. RF: 300 one-tree oneDAL forests, each on its own draw of min(200,000, n) fit rows with replacement; max_features {0.2, 0.5} x min_samples_leaf {5, 50}. LightGBM: learning rate 0.1, feature fraction 0.5, no bagging, 255 bins, early stopping with patience 150 and cap 800, a 1,600 refit for a trial that ran to the cap; num_leaves {31, 127} x min_child_samples {20, 200}. Tweedie: the LightGBM grid x power {1.2, 1.5}, offset log(max(normal, c)), on targets with legal minimum 0 and at least 20% zero lead-0 refit labels. hgb_l1: scikit-learn HistGradientBoosting, absolute error, learning rate 0.1, no early stopping, max_leaf_nodes {31, 127} x min_samples_leaf {20, 200}, iterations {100, 200, 400, 800} (extended to 1,600 when the best is at 800). Settings on a 3-link run: Ridge 24, RF 12, LightGBM 12, Tweedie 8, hgb_l1 12. Libraries: lightgbm 4.6.0, scikit-learn 1.6.1, scikit-learn-intelex 2025.10.0. Source: common rival protocol sections 1-2 and section 3 item 9. (Tier 1 length plan (2026-11-12, ruling AU7), dated note: Table 3 keeps each learner's loss, grid and setting count; its implementation and fixed-settings columns moved verbatim to an unnumbered table in Supplement S8.6 (A2-09), and panel 6c, which also cites this fact, moved to Supplement S6.13 (A3-07).)
- **K57 (final). Published-rival board and tiers.** Human rivals sit on the same saved test rows, cuts, normals and metrics as the common board, and every fit uses training rows only. Entries are tiered P (parity) and L (lagged on purpose), which decide, and A (more information) and X (extension), which never decide. Sources: Part A sections 2 and 10.
- **K58 (final). Published-rival maps and picks.** The calibration maps are F0 (index as shipped), F1 (linear in the link), F2 (isotonic), F3 and F4 (the same two on anomalies from the normal) and F5 (per unit, shrunk to F3); F6 is fire-only and unsigned. The pick has the lowest MAE of the clipped prediction. Nowcast rivals are fitted on tune rows and picked on lead-0 inner rows; forecast-only products are picked on the guard rows of their lowest covered lead. There is one pick per rival, pinned at every lead, refit per lead, and locked before any test file is read. The re-run models (bardl_h1, kraft_lstm) have one configuration. Sources: Part A sections 2, 3 and 6.
- **K59 (final). Published-rival guard and contrast.** The comparator is the run's saved trees-off of the pick's link (identity for F0 and the re-run models) or, on coral, the training median, on the same rows; the rival passes if and only if its MAE is strictly lower, else it takes the run's decline target. coral crw_outlook at 365 d is scored without the safety net. The deciding contrast uses the covered rows of groups with at least 10 covered test rows, with our MAE on the same rows, under the run's nowcast band at every lead. Source: Part A section 5.

### 15.9 Implementation checks (Supplement S7)
- **K60 (final). The R36 identity tolerance.** max(k x s, 9e-7), where s is the training-row SD of link(y) (ddof 0) and k = 5.230771281733543e-08 (C9). The floor is 10 x the measured arithmetic error of 9e-8 (ruling O4). It is used only by the trees-off identity checks; the skill guard has no margin (M43). Sources: route/kinds.py r36_tolerance, r36_floor; pipeline/stages.py guard_tolerances; engine_constants.py C9.
- **K61 (final). Seeds.** Each tree has three seeds (bag draw, tree, corrector), each the first 4 bytes (big-endian) of SHA-256 of '<group>|<tree number>|<role>'; the placebo reuses its corrector's seed. Seeds depend on the group name and tree number only, so adding or removing a group changes no other group's trees. Source: fit/bank.py module docstring.

    - **PM2.5 (old Board C, entry M2: same-month MERRA-2 with a per-location log calibration; files of 2026-09-25).** The per-location calibration was fitted on February 2003 to December 2019: 203 monthly rows per location, 1,997 locations. Its shrink was picked on fits to 2016 scored on 2017-2019 (pm25_rivals.py line 74: FIT_END, INNER_END = 2016, 2019). It was scored on 2020-2023 (the board's per_year keys) in the old split's 20 test regions (the board's region counts: architecture better 7, ties 3, rival better 10). The 20 regions in the calibration rows' location ids are exactly the 20 PM2.5 test groups of engine/legacy_harness/shipped_splits.yaml, the continuity groups of P25. Sources: D:/pm25_rivals/full/board.csv (sha256 6446242a41e20429..., row C, M2: mae 3.926, per_year, arch_wins / ties / rival_wins, code 8c6e945f6602, which is the sha256 prefix of pm25_rivals.py on disk); D:/pm25_rivals/full/rivals.log lines 85-86 and 96; D:/pm25_forecast/full/L1/calib_rows.parquet (cell_id, year, month); PM25_RIVALS_PLAN.md lines 11, 80 and 84.
    - **NDVI (old Kraft LSTM; files of 2026-09-05).** It was trained on rows of 2002-2023 (ndvi_kraft.py line 204) of 1,600 cells, 25 per region, in the first 64 of the old split's 89 training regions; the 400,000-sequence cap stopped collection at the 64th (D:/run89full/kraft_even.log; D:/run89full/kraft/train_cells.csv). It was calibrated per cell on 2017-2019 (ndvi_kraft.py lines 468-469; kraft_even.log, "per-cell linear fit on 2017-2019"; NDVI_BASELINE_REPORT.md section 7.3). Its 44 region prediction files hold the years 2021-2023, in 25 of the old split's 44 test regions and 19 of its training regions (D34; D:/run89full/ndvi_split.txt). Under v1 every NDVI region is scored on 2020-2023 (M7), so its training read today's test window.
    - **NDVI, partly in-sample.** 13 of those 19 scored training regions supplied training cells: alaska_north_slope, atacama_desert, borneo_rainforest, central_india_dry_forest, east_african_savanna, hulunbuir_grassland, hungarian_puszta, iberian_wildfire, karoo, mediterranean_andalusia, mopane_woodland, namib_desert and new_zealand_north_island. The other six did not (pearl_river_delta, scandinavian_taiga, sundarbans, tuva_steppe, uruguay, western_europe_temperate). train_cells.csv names 64 regions, all old-split training regions. The count 13 equals NDVI_BASELINE_REPORT.md's correction notice (lines 12-13: "additionally trained on 13 of the 19 donor regions it was scored on"). So its 44-region score was partly in-sample. *Provisional (computed read-only on 2026-11-10 from the saved files; not a Part):* in those 13 regions, 119 of the 38,199 cells in the prediction files are themselves training cells.
    - **Forest loss (old udef_a run, VT0007 UDef-A; files of 2026-09-07).** It was fitted on rows up to 2019 (donor rows plus each test region's own rows) and scored on 2020-2023 (defo_papers.py lines 76-78), in the old split's 17 test regions ("8 donors | 17 regions | fitted ON", D:/defo_papers/_papers.log line 38). Its 17 per-region files are exactly the 17 forest-loss test groups of shipped_splits.yaml.
    - **Coral (CRW Parts 456-457): a correction to "same test years".** The earlier CRW runs scored surveys dated 2011-2019, not only 2016-2019. The old coral test set held out ecoregions over all survey years (CRW_BENCHMARK_REPORT.md section 4, line 103: "full coral test set", 4,051 rows in 89 ecoregions; lines 23-24: "The CRW archive begins in 2011; our surveys begin in 1994"). The CRW Outlook archive starts in 2011 (lines 57-58), so the CRW-scoreable rows run from 2011 to 2019 (line 11; line 230: "The claim describes 2011–2019 reefs only"). *Provisional (computed read-only on 2026-11-10; not a Part):* joining the saved scored rows (D:/coral/crw/crw_rows_060perc.parquet, 999 rows at lead 1; crw_leadrows_060perc.parquet, Part 457's fixed set of 953 rows) on sid to the feature table that coral_crw.py reads (forecast_features_L0.parquet, one date per sid) gives 2011-2019 in both sets, with 452 rows dated 2016-2019 in each. So the earlier coral runs scored today's test years (2016-2019) and also 2011-2015, which are today's inner-fit (2011-2012) and inner-score (2013-2015) years (M7).
    - **In short:** the earlier PM2.5, NDVI and forest-loss runs scored only years inside today's test windows (2020-2023, 2021-2023 and 2020-2023). The earlier coral runs did not (2011-2019, against today's 2016-2019).
